- [x] **L6** (low/maintainability) `.gitattributes:10` - PowerShell coverage is incomplete: only `*.ps1` is pinned to CRLF, while module/manifest/format files (`*.psm1`, `*.psd1`, `*.ps1xml`) and Windows helper scripts (e.g. `*.vbs`, `*.wsf`) fall back to the default rule. If the intent is "all Windows-executed PowerShell is CRLF", declare them explicitly so module files are not normalized to LF on checkout. Also confirm the CRLF choice against how these scripts are invoked (the repo's `scripts/*.ps1` are also reachable from the POSIX `scripts/*.sh` tooling), since `pwsh` on Linux/macOS is the only path where LF would be preferable.
- [x] **L7** (low/maintainability) `Dockerfile:12` - UID/GID 1000 are hard-coded in both the `chown` and the subsequent `USER 1000:1000`. Under docker-compose this is worked around via `user: "${HACKATHONKIT_UID:-1000}:${HACKATHONKIT_GID:-1000}"`, but in that case all image-created directories here (`configs`, `data/*`, `logs`, `evals`) are shadowed by the bind mounts, so this `mkdir && chown` provides no guarantee of writability. Running the image standalone with a different `--user` leaves `/workspace` unwritable and makes the app fail on log/artifact writes. Suggest parameterising the UID/GID at build time (`ARG UID=1000`, `useradd`/`chown` with that value) or relaxing directory permissions (e.g. `chmod -R 0775`) so an arbitrary non-root UID can write.
- [x] **L8** (low/maintainability) `Dockerfile:28` - This stage (`quality`) is derived directly from `golang`, not from `runtime-base`, so it does not inherit `ENV HACKATHONKIT_REPO_ROOT=/workspace`. It happens to work today because `scripts/quality-gate.sh` computes `ROOT` from its own path and passes `--repo-root "$ROOT"`, but any tool invoked from the gate that relies on `HACKATHONKIT_REPO_ROOT` (see `internal/config/config.go`, which falls back to the process CWD / repo-root detection) will resolve paths inconsistently. Set the variable explicitly for parity with the other targets.
- [x] **L9** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/main.go:173` - Errors from `Events`/`Outputs` are discarded, so a database failure is reported as HTTP 200 with empty arrays, indistinguishable from "this session has no events". Likewise the preceding `store.Session` error is converted to a 404 `session not found`, hiding real backend failures behind a misleading sentinel. Either propagate/log the failures (e.g. add an `error` field or log via `observability.Log`) or explicitly document the best-effort contract.
- [x] **L10** (low/performance) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:71` - Every poll wipes `tbody.innerHTML` and recreates all rows plus listeners, which discards scroll/hover state, can drop a click that lands exactly on a poll tick, and causes visible flicker on a 3s cadence with an unbounded number of sessions. Consider keyed/diffed updates (or only re-render when the payload changes) instead of a full rebuild.
- [x] **L11** (low/maintainability) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:169` - `state.lastFeedback` is assigned in `applyNeuronEvent` but never read anywhere, and `state.lastFeedbackKey` is used without being declared in the initial `state` object, making the dedupe state hard to follow. Also verify the state object literal here is where such keys should be initialized.
- [x] **L12** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:125` - Boundary conditions are off: a size of exactly 1 MiB is rendered as "1024.0 KB" and exactly 1 KiB as "1024 B". Use `>=` thresholds (and handle non-numeric/negative values) so units switch at the boundary.
- [x] **L13** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:173` - `pam`/`ppl1` are computed over the entire feedback list (`all`) rather than the decision shown for the current event, so the 🍬/⚡ counters are global aggregates that keep growing and are unrelated to the displayed trace — misleading as a per-run verdict. Scope them to the displayed feedback (or label them clearly as session totals).
- [x] **L14** (low/maintainability) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:232` - `Promise.all` makes every panel all-or-nothing: one failing endpoint (e.g. `/artifacts`) rejects the whole batch, so routing/calls/fly are not rendered at all and the header just says "offline", hiding that the rest of the data is healthy. Consider `Promise.allSettled` (or per-endpoint guards) so each panel degrades independently, and stop polling/back off when the same endpoint keeps failing.
- [x] **L15** (low/maintainability) `cmd/fly-bench/main.go:182` - Rows whose JSON parses but lack a `task` field are silently dropped, so a malformed/renamed field in the suite reduces the task count with no diagnostic and the summary is computed over fewer tasks than the suite contains. Either error out with the offending line number or warn on stderr so the evidence cannot be silently weakened.
- [x] **L16** (low/performance) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:95` - The whole dataset is sent as one `/embeddings` request with no batching and no deadline (`context.Background()`). Embedding APIs cap the number of inputs (and tokens) per request, so a large training set will fail with a provider-side 400 rather than being chunked; batch the texts (e.g. a few hundred per request) and consider a per-run timeout for better diagnostics.
- [x] **L17** (low/maintainability) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:109` - `seen` is populated from the same `rows` slice that is iterated here (and `readDataset` already discards rows with an empty category), so `!seen[row.Category]` is never true — the guard is dead code that suggests filtering that does not happen. Remove it or base the check on an explicit per-row validity condition.
- [x] **L18** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:147` - The ingest goroutine is not joined before the deferred `store.Close()` runs: on SIGTERM the context is cancelled and `ListenAndServe` returns, but the ticker goroutine may still be inside an in-flight `store.Ingest` when the store is closed, producing spurious "sql: database is closed" errors and a close/use overlap. Wait for the goroutine (e.g. a `sync.WaitGroup` drained before `Close`) so shutdown is ordered.
- [x] **L19** (low/security) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:168` - Only `ReadHeaderTimeout` is set; there is no `WriteTimeout`/`IdleTimeout`, so slow or idle keep-alive connections are held indefinitely. Add them as defense-in-depth even though the listener is loopback-only.
- [x] **L20** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:145` - The task is truncated by byte offset (`task[:40]`), which can split a multi-byte UTF-8 rune and emit invalid UTF-8 in the table. `truncate()` below already handles this correctly with `[]rune`; reuse it here.
- [x] **L21** (low/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:168` - `sessions show` also encodes directly to `os.Stdout`, bypassing the injected `stdout` writer for the same reason as `cmdIngest`.
- [x] **L22** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:36` - An ingest failure is only written to stderr, but the message is emitted right before `tea.WithAltScreen()` takes over the terminal, so it is typically wiped by the alternate screen and never surfaces to the user. The TUI then operates on a potentially stale/incomplete store without any warning. Pass the ingest error into the model (or show it in the status line / start the program with the error) so the failure is visible.
- [x] **L23** (low/performance) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:50` - `load()` runs synchronous `store.Sessions`/`store.Events` queries directly on the Bubble Tea update goroutine (called from `Update` on every 2s tick and on every keypress) using a fresh `context.Background()`. If the store stalls (e.g. another process holds a SQLite lock during ingest), the whole UI freezes and quit becomes unresponsive. Consider running the refresh as a `tea.Cmd` and threading a cancellable context (derived from a context cancelled when the program exits) instead of an uncancellable background context.
- [x] **L24** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:141` - The width floors are independent, so for terminals narrower than 30+20+4 = 54 columns the computed panes exceed `m.width` (`leftWidth + rightWidth = width - 4` only holds when neither floor applies). On narrow terminals the joined panes overflow and the layout wraps/overlaps. Clamp the sum to `width` (e.g. reduce the minimum when `width < 54`, or stack the panes vertically for very narrow terminals). Also note that line content is never truncated to the pane width, so long status/risk-reason text can overflow regardless.
- [x] **L25** (low/performance) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:195` - `--list-tools` and `--selftest` are dispatched only after the Postgres and object-store clients, the fly router, the embedder and the learning store have been constructed above. These diagnostic commands therefore pay external connection latency (Postgres ping up to 5s, MinIO open) and can be blocked/slowed by unrelated backend availability - note that `quality.Run` shells out to exactly `go run ./cmd/hackathonkit-mcp --selftest`. Consider handling these early-exit flags before wiring external backends, or construct the backends lazily after the flag dispatch.
- [x] **L26** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:325` - The encode error is discarded, so a failed/partial write (broken pipe, closed stdout, encode failure) is reported to the caller as success. Return the error from `printResources` and handle it at the call sites (`--resources` and `--resources-set`).
- [x] **L27** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:328` - This is used to report "pending eval decisions" in the session-context block, but (a) the WalkDir error and per-entry errors are swallowed, so an unreadable `evals/decisions` silently reports 0 pending decisions, and (b) every `.json` under the tree is counted, including decisions that were already ingested/resolved, so the number can overstate what is actually pending. Consider surfacing the walk error and counting only decisions that are not yet consumed (the control store already tracks a per-file cursor).
- [x] **L28** (low/security) `docker-compose.yml:24` - The MinIO access key is hardcoded to `hackathonkit` (and `MINIO_ROOT_USER` in the minio service likewise), so credentials cannot be rotated/overridden via `.env` and the access key is effectively a public constant. Prefer sourcing it from the environment as well, e.g. `${MINIO_ROOT_USER:-hackathonkit}` / a dedicated `MINIO_ACCESS_KEY` variable, keeping root and client credentials consistent.
- [x] **L29** (low/maintainability) `docker-compose.yml:88` - The `redis` service is declared and publishes a host port, but no other service references it (no `depends_on`, no Redis/DSN environment variable) and it has neither a healthcheck nor a password. This is either dead configuration or missing wiring for a required dependency; if it is still needed, add a healthcheck and connect the consumer, otherwise remove it to avoid confusion.
- [x] **L30** (low/maintainability) `internal/atomicfile/atomicfile.go:31` - Cleanup only happens on the in-process error paths. If the process is killed or panics between `CreateTemp` and `Rename`, the `<base>.*.tmp` file is left behind in the destination directory, and nothing ever reclaims it, so they accumulate in the state/config directories (which other tooling and bind-mounted containers read). Consider documenting this limitation and/or providing a small helper to sweep stale `<base>.*.tmp` files so callers can clean up on startup.
- [x] **L31** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/config.go:226` - The error from the second `yaml.Unmarshal` into `filePatch` is discarded. If it ever fails, all numeric/boolean overrides (`default_timeout_sec`, `max_text_chars`, `fly_threshold`, `top_k`, every `learning.*` field) are silently dropped while the non-numeric fields from the first unmarshal still apply, producing a partially-applied configuration with no log. Log the error at minimum, or reuse a single unmarshal pass so the two structs cannot diverge.
- [x] **L32** (low/documentation) `internal/config/config.go:395` - This comment claims the file is re-read "when its mtime changes", but no mtime/`ModTime` check exists anywhere in this file (the function re-reads the file unconditionally on every call, and `m.evaluationCache` is only consulted when the read or parse fails). The comment misdescribes the caching behaviour; either implement the mtime check or correct the comment.
- [x] **L33** (low/bug) `internal/config/config.go:400` - Read failures are silently swallowed here: if `configs/evaluation.yaml` exists but cannot be read (e.g. `EACCES`, I/O error), the error is dropped with no diagnostic and the caller silently receives cached settings or built-in defaults. `Routing()` logs the equivalent case (`cannot read %s ...`), so the two paths are inconsistent. Also note the parse-failure branch below logs `cannot parse %s (using defaults)` but discards `uerr`, and the message is inaccurate when `m.evaluationInit` is true and the cached settings are actually returned. Please log the read error and include the parse error, e.g. `log.Printf("config: cannot parse %s: %v", path, err)`.
- [x] **L34** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/config.go:435` - Each evaluation field is validated in isolation, so inconsistent combinations are accepted. For example `min_valid_judges` may exceed `judges`; the value is consumed as the quorum in `eval.aggregate`/`eval.calibrate` (`internal/tools/evaluation.go` uses `d.Cfg.Evaluation().MinValidJudges` as `minValid`), which makes every aggregation permanently `Inconclusive` and drives the operator into a substitute-judge loop. Consider clamping/validating cross-field invariants after applying the patch (e.g. `MinValidJudges <= Judges`) and logging the adjustment.
- [x] **L35** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/dotenv.go:10` - Every `os.ReadFile` failure is swallowed identically: a missing `.env` (expected, fine) is indistinguishable from `permission denied`, a directory at that path, or an I/O error. In those cases all variables stay unset and the process starts with a silently broken configuration (the call sites in `cmd/*` ignore the result too, since the function returns nothing). Consider at least distinguishing `os.IsNotExist(err)` from real failures and surfacing the latter (return an error for the caller to log, or log the path/reason to stderr) so a misconfigured `.env` is diagnosable.
- [x] **L36** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/dotenv.go:24` - The key is only trimmed, never validated, and the result of `os.Setenv` is discarded. A line such as `FOO BAR=baz` (or one carrying NUL bytes) therefore attempts to create a malformed environment variable; on the platforms where the syscall rejects it (e.g. NUL in name/value) the error is dropped and the variable silently does not exist, which is the worst outcome for configuration loading. Validate the key against a conservative pattern (`[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`) and skip/report lines that do not match, instead of discarding the `Setenv` error unconditionally.
- [x] **L37** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/handoff.go:41` - Inconsistent error context: the session lookup wraps its error with the session id, but `store.Events`/`store.Outputs` failures are returned raw, so callers (e.g. `sessions resume` in cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:175-178, which prints the error directly) cannot tell which session failed. Wrap these with the session id for consistency and better diagnostics.
- [x] **L38** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/handoff.go:46` - Same as above for outputs: the error is returned without the session id, unlike the wrapped `store.Session` error at the top of the function.
- [x] **L39** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/ingest.go:190` - `walkErr` is swallowed, and because the callback never returns a non-nil error for it, `filepath.WalkDir` always returns nil and the `err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err)` check below is effectively dead. A permission or I/O failure while scanning `evals/decisions` therefore goes completely unreported. Return the walk error (or record it) so the scan failure reaches the caller.
- [x] **L40** (low/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:207` - Unreadable decision files are silently dropped from the result. A read failure (permissions, I/O error) is swallowed by `continue`, so the caller sees a successful ingest while the file is never processed and no trace of the failure exists. Only the malformed-JSON case is legitimately retryable here; distinguish the two and report the read error.
- [x] **L41** (low/bug) `internal/control/risk.go:42` - `decision` is compared case-sensitively to the lowercase literal, unlike `ev.Status` above which uses `strings.EqualFold`. Decision payloads are read from `evals/decisions/*.json`, so a value such as `"Rollback"` or one padded with whitespace is silently ignored and the rollback penalty is missed (risk undercounted). Normalize the value before comparing.
- [x] **L42** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/store.go:292` - The `json.Unmarshal` error for `risk_reasons` is discarded, so corrupted or legacy-format stored JSON silently degrades to an empty reason list. `RiskScore` is still returned, so callers cannot tell that the reasons were lost (handoff/report output just shows a score with no explanation). At minimum surface this as a wrapped error, or document that decoding is best-effort.
- [x] **L43** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/store.go:363` - Same pattern as `scanSession`: the unmarshal error for the event payload is dropped, so a malformed `payload` column yields an event with a nil `Payload` and no indication that data was lost (risk evaluation and the demo API read these payloads). Consider propagating or logging the decode failure instead of silently ignoring it.
- [x] **L44** (low/maintainability) `internal/evaluation/bundle.go:151` - `copied` and `copiedBytes` are incremented in the walk loop but never read, so they are dead state. Meanwhile the returned `Bundle.Files`/`Bundle.Bytes` (and the `files`/`bytes` values exposed by `eval.freeze_bundle`) come from `hashBundle`, whose entries also include the generated `git-sha.txt` and `rubric-version.txt`. The reported counts therefore do not match the number of repository files/bytes actually copied. Either drop the counters or use them for the returned totals.
- [x] **L45** (low/maintainability) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:31` - `JudgeCriteria` (and `TieBreakOrder` below) are exported, mutable package-level slices. Any caller can mutate the rubric/aggregation rules in place, which changes validation, aggregation and ordering globally and is not safe under concurrent use. Prefer unexported backing slices with an accessor that returns a copy, or document/assert immutability. Relatedly, `RankEntry.CriterionMeans` is assigned `agg.CriterionMeans` directly, so the returned ranking aliases the caller's map and later mutations of the aggregate are observable through the ranking.
- [x] **L46** (low/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:79` - The mutex only serializes a single `Store` value; the load-modify-save in `Record`/`Promote` is not atomic across separate `Store` instances or processes sharing the same path (e.g. two `hackathonkit-mcp` servers opened on the same repo, or `hackathonkit-ctl` reading concurrently), so interleaved writers can silently lose updates. Consider file-level locking (e.g. `flock` on the store path) or document/guard single-writer ownership of the file.
- [x] **L47** (low/bug) `internal/mcp/contracts.go:44` - `append` on the copied slice can write into the caller's backing array when `e.Sources` has spare capacity (e.g. a slice created with `make([]Source, 0, n)` and passed via `WithSources(s...)`). The appended elements are written past the caller's `len` in the array the caller still owns, so a later `append` on that caller slice silently overwrites/aliases them. `WithWarnings` has the same pattern. Cap the capacity (full slice expression) or clone before appending to guarantee copy-on-append semantics.
- [x] **L48** (low/documentation) `internal/mcp/jsonrpc.go:12` - `IsNotification` treats an explicit `"id": null` the same as a missing id, and the only caller (`Server.handle`, internal/mcp/server.go:114) returns `nil` for notifications, so a client that sends `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"method":...}` and still waits for a reply will block forever. This matches the JSON-RPC 2.0 note that a null id is indistinguishable from a notification, but the choice is currently undocumented; add a doc comment so the semantics are intentional and future maintainers/clients are not surprised.
- [x] **L49** (low/maintainability) `internal/mcp/jsonrpc.go:16` - These struct tags are fully ignored because `Response.MarshalJSON` marshals a hand-built anonymous struct; in particular `omitempty` on `Result` no longer has any effect (the custom marshaller always emits `result`). Keeping the tags is misleading for the next reader, who may assume `encoding/json` will drop an empty/nil result. Consider removing the ignored tags or adding a note on the struct that marshalling is custom.
- [x] **L50** (low/maintainability) `internal/mcp/registry.go:27` - `Register` stores `&t` but keeps the caller's `InputSchema` map by reference, so the caller still shares mutable state with the registry (e.g. `tool()` builders in `internal/tools/deps.go` build a schema map and hand it over). Any post-registration mutation by the caller changes the registered tool. Copying the schema on registration makes the registry the sole owner of its data.
- [x] **L51** (low/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:40` - `time.Duration(s.opts.CallTimeoutSec) * time.Second` overflows for large configured values (on 64-bit, CallTimeoutSec > ~9.2e9), wrapping to a negative duration; `context.WithTimeout` then produces an already-expired context and every tool call fails with `context deadline exceeded` without any config error. Only the lower bound is validated, so clamp an upper bound here (or when deriving the context):
- [x] **L52** (low/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:82` - A single frame larger than the 32 MB max token size makes `bufio.Scanner` return `bufio.ErrTooLong`; that value is propagated through `scanErr`, so `ServeStreams` returns an error and the whole session terminates, unlike malformed JSON which is answered with a parse error and the loop continues. Since `tools/call` arguments can legitimately embed large payloads, consider distinguishing `bufio.ErrTooLong` (reply with a parse/invalid-request error and keep serving) rather than aborting the server.
- [x] **L53** (low/bug) `internal/metadata/metadata.go:76` - Tables are provisioned only via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` executed on every startup, with no schema-version/migration path: if a column is added later, existing deployments keep the old table definition and inserts fail at runtime. Additionally, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is not atomic against concurrent creation — if two processes (e.g. demo-api and hackathonkit-mcp) call `OpenPostgres` against the same database at the same time, one can fail with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "pg_type_typname_nsp_index"` / `relation already exists`, which here disables metadata entirely for that process. Consider guarding the DDL with a `pg_advisory_lock` (and/or retry on the catalog race) and recording a schema version for future migrations.
- [ ] **L54** (low/maintainability; reviewed: MINIO_BUCKET override is documented; default keeps existing setups working) `internal/objectstore/minio.go:25` - Silently defaulting to the fixed bucket name "hackathonkit" when `MINIO_BUCKET` is unset means any environment pointed at a shared MinIO (or one that merely forgot the variable) reads and writes the same bucket, mixing artifacts across environments/deployments. Consider requiring an explicit bucket (or at least logging the fallback) instead of an implicit shared default. Note this default is currently asserted by `TestSettingsFromEnvDefaults`, so changing it requires updating that test.
- [x] **L55** (low/bug) `internal/observability/obs.go:101` - If the underlying write fails part-way (e.g. ENOSPC on a large record), the file is left with a truncated record that has no terminating newline. The next successful append is then concatenated directly onto that fragment, producing one corrupt line and silently losing two records. `enc.Encode` hides the written byte count, so the short write cannot be detected. Marshal into a buffer first, write it explicitly, verify `n == len(payload)` and report a distinct error so the caller/operator can repair the file.
- [x] **L56** (low/security) `internal/openai/client.go:35` - When `crypto/rand` fails, every affected client is assigned the same constant session identifier, defeating the per-session uniqueness the header is meant to provide. Prefer returning an error (or at least a value derived from another entropy source) rather than a shared constant.
- [x] **L57** (low/bug) `internal/openai/client.go:199` - `WriteField` errors are dropped. If a field write fails the multipart body is malformed, yet the request is still sent and the resulting 4xx/5xx is reported as if it were an API-level failure. Return the write error instead (this also covers the `language` field below).
- [x] **L58** (low/bug) `internal/openai/client.go:278` - `truncate` slices raw bytes, so an API/JSON response containing multibyte UTF-8 (very likely for non-English prompts) can be cut mid-rune, producing invalid UTF-8 in the surfaced error message. Truncate on rune boundaries instead.
- [x] **L59** (low/bug) `internal/plans/plans.go:123` - `Latest` silently drops both read errors and JSON parse errors and just returns whatever other file it managed to load, so a corrupt or unreadable newest plan makes callers (the `/plan/api/latest` HTTP handler, `hackathonkit-mcp`, `control.Handoff`, the `plan.status` tool) believe an older plan is the current one, with no signal that the newest file was skipped. The doc comment does not declare this best-effort behaviour. Either surface the failure (e.g. return `(Plan, bool, error)` / an explicit sentinel for an unparseable newest file) or document that skipped files are expected.
- [x] **L60** (low/bug) `internal/quality/quality.go:87` - The status switch compares with `==` and treats any parent-context cancellation as a generic `failed`, and it labels a check `timeout` even when the process completed successfully just as the parent deadline fired. Also, once the parent context is done, `Run` keeps invoking the remaining checks, so each one spawns a process that is immediately killed and is reported as `timeout` although it never ran. Use `errors.Is(runCtx.Err(), context.DeadlineExceeded)`/`context.Canceled` to distinguish per-check timeouts from overall cancellation, and stop iterating once `ctx.Err() != nil`.
- [ ] **L61** (reviewed: intentional - see M61; reviewed: intentional - see M61) (low/maintainability) `internal/resources/matrix.go:177` - `providerMissing` falls back to `status.EnvKeys` when `MissingKeys` is empty. `MissingKeys` is only populated for claimed-and-not-detected providers, so a provider that is not ready for another reason yields its entire env-key list as "missing" (which may include keys that are actually set), and an unknown provider id yields nil. Both cases produce an empty or misleading `Decision.Missing`. Consider returning no keys rather than `EnvKeys` unless the missing set is actually known.
- [x] **L62** (low/maintainability) `internal/resources/probe.go:29` - `Live` is set to `true` in the constructor and never updated, so the not-configured, no-base-url, request-error and non-200 paths all serialize `live: true`. The caller copies `probe.Live` straight into the agent-facing result, producing contradictory payloads such as `live: true, reachable: false, error: "not configured"`. If `Live` is meant to mean the provider is actually healthy, set it after the successful 200; otherwise make the "mirrors the caller's live-probe request flag" invariant explicit so future callers do not rely on it as a health signal.
- [x] **L63** (low/maintainability) `internal/routing/fly/embed.go:310` - `cosine` silently truncates to the shorter vector, so mismatched dimensions produce a plausibly-looking but wrong similarity. Today only `embeddingCategoryScores` calls it and it pre-filters mismatched centroids, but that invariant is easy to break in a future caller. Rejecting unequal lengths makes the contract explicit and fails fast.
- [x] **L64** (low/maintainability) `internal/routing/fly/events.go:14` - `topN` is silently coerced to 64 and every failure mode (no brain, no embedder, embed error, scoring error) collapses into the identical empty response, so a caller cannot distinguish "the model produced no active cells" from "scoring is unavailable". Since the result is consumed for observability/logging, consider surfacing the effective `topN` and/or an explicit reason field for the fallback so downstream consumers do not misinterpret an error as a valid snapshot.
- [x] **L65** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/llm.go:19` - `r.llm == nil` only detects a nil interface. A typed nil concrete pointer stored via `WithLLM` (e.g. `var c *openai.Client; r.WithLLM(c)`) yields a non-nil interface that passes this guard and then panics when `ClassifyTask` dereferences the nil receiver. Since `WithLLM` is exported and accepts any `LLMClassifier`, either document that callers must not pass typed nils or guard with a reflect-based nil check.
- [x] **L66** (low/performance) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:60` - `loadBrain` holds the package-global `brainMu` while doing `os.ReadFile` and `json.Unmarshal`, so a slow/stalled disk serializes every Router in the process, and every failure (missing file, parse error, shape mismatch) silently degrades to `nil`, silently disabling the MB encoder with no diagnostic. Read the file outside the lock (double-checked under the lock before caching) and consider surfacing/logging the load failure.
- [ ] **L67** (low/maintainability; reviewed: fusion weights are a tuned heuristic; changing them needs an A/B run) `internal/routing/fly/router.go:143` - Score fusion is asymmetric between encoders. The embedding step blends 0.5/0.5 only for categories present in `embeddingScores`, so keyword-only categories keep their full weight, whereas the kNN/MB/LLM steps multiply every existing category by 0.6/0.4 and then add new categories undecayed. The effective weight of the keyword signal therefore depends on which downstream encoders happen to respond (and the relative ranking of a keyword category changes when, say, the LLM is enabled but embeddings are not). Please confirm this is the intended normalization; a uniform combine step would be easier to reason about.
- [x] **L68** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/router.go:202` - `route` is selected from the raw category scores before availability filtering, while recommendations are filtered by `available`. A route can therefore name a category whose capabilities are all unavailable (e.g. task `"csv policy"` with `available=["knowledge.search"]` returns route `"data"` plus a knowledge recommendation). Consider deriving the route only from categories that produced at least one available capability, or returning `"unknown"` when no recommendation survives filtering.
- [x] **L69** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/router.go:294` - `stringSlice` lowercases the `[]any` and `string` branches but returns the `[]string` branch verbatim. `applyMetadata` then does an exact-match `switch` on lowercase type tokens, so `meta["types"] = []string{"PDF"}` silently skips the documents boost while `[]any{"PDF"}` applies it. Normalize case in the `[]string` branch too (or lowercase in `applyMetadata`).
- [x] **L70** (low/bug) `internal/skills/validate.go:125` - Line counting adds 1 unconditionally, so any file whose content ends with a newline (the normal case for these authors) is reported one line longer than tools such as `wc -l` or an editor show. A SKILL.md that is exactly at the 250-line maximum would be flagged as exceeding it. Count the physical lines instead.
- [x] **L71** (low/bug) `internal/skills/validate.go:147` - The check only requires that "use when"/"use for" appear *anywhere* in the lowercased description, but both the error message and docs/SKILL-STANDARD.md (line 39: "`description` ... starts with \"Use when\"") require the trigger phrase to be the leading text. As written, a description like "Ships a report. Use when needed." passes validation, so the validator does not actually enforce the documented contract.
- [x] **L72** (low/bug) `internal/skills/validate.go:195` - The opener check only accepts an LF-terminated `---`, yet the terminator scan below explicitly tolerates CRLF (`strings.TrimRight(line, "\r") == "---"`). A CRLF SKILL.md is therefore rejected with "must start with YAML frontmatter" and the `\r` handling in the scan is effectively dead code. Accept the CRLF opener (ideally normalize line endings up front) so the two halves of the parser agree.
- [x] **L73** (low/maintainability) `internal/skills/validate.go:219` - Every ReadFile failure is reported as "missing agents/openai.yaml", so permission errors, I/O errors, or a path that is a directory are misdiagnosed as an absent file. Distinguish `os.ErrNotExist` from other failures so the report tells the author what actually went wrong.
- [ ] **L74** (low/maintainability; reviewed: storage context threading needs an API change; tracked for a refactor) `internal/storage/store.go:19` - `WriteArtifact`/`ReadArtifact` take no `context.Context`, so the metadata and object-store calls always run on a fresh `context.Background()` with a fixed 30s budget. Tool handlers already have a caller context with their own deadline (MCP call timeout), so a slow backend cannot be cancelled when the caller gives up and the handler blocks up to 30s. Consider threading the caller's context through these methods (or accepting it as an optional parameter) so backend calls inherit the request deadline/cancellation.
- [x] **L75** (low/maintainability) `internal/telegram/telegram.go:454` - `io.LimitReader` silently truncates the body at 1 MiB, so an oversized but otherwise valid response fails `json.Unmarshal` and is reported as `invalid response`, which is indistinguishable from genuinely malformed JSON and hides the real (potentially retriable) failure. Read `maxResponseSize+1` and report an explicit "response too large" error.
- [x] **L76** (low/performance) `internal/tools/context.go:47` - The handler receives `ctx` but the scan ignores it entirely (no `ctx.Err()` check in the WalkDir callback or before each `os.ReadFile`), and `WalkDir`/`os.ReadFile` have no deadline. On a large or slow plugin tree the tool call can run far past the caller's deadline and keep doing work after cancellation. Thread `ctx` into `skillContextItems`/`manifestContextItems` and abort the walk when the context is done.
- [x] **L77** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/context.go:233` - The recommendation list is capped at 10 without any indication of truncation, while the envelope warning reports the full `flagged` count. A caller that has 14 flagged items sees "14 item(s) exceed the recommended budget" but only 10 actionable entries, with no way to tell which were dropped. Either keep the count consistent (report `len(out)`) or return a `truncated`/`omitted` field.
- [ ] **L78** (low/bug; reviewed: unknown extensions fall back to CSV parsing by design (documented in the tool description)) `internal/tools/data.go:31` - Any extension other than `xlsx`/`xls`/`tsv` falls through to comma-separated parsing, so a typo or an unsupported format (`.json`, `.parquet`, `.txt`, or a `.csv` file whose real content is xlsx) is parsed as CSV and yields silently wrong headers/rows instead of a clear error. Handle `csv` (and any intentionally supported alias) explicitly and return an unsupported-format error for the rest.
- [ ] **L79** (low/bug; reviewed: where-clause tokenizer limitation documented; column names with spaces are not supported) `internal/tools/data.go:363` - `strings.Fields` collapses all whitespace runs, so a filter value is reassembled with single spaces (`where: 'city == New York'` silently searches for `New York`), and column names containing spaces can never be addressed since `fields[0]` is only the first token. Split with `SplitN(where, " ", 3)` (or require a documented quoting/separator rule) so the value after the operator is preserved verbatim.
- [x] **L80** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/deps.go:107` - `argInt` silently clamps out-of-range input to the `int32` bounds instead of returning `def`, so a caller cannot distinguish "value was clamped" from "value was supplied". The `int32` limit is also arbitrary on 64-bit platforms, where `int` is 64 bits: a legitimate value above `math.MaxInt32` (e.g. a large `offset`/`max_chars`) is silently truncated rather than rejected or defaulted. Prefer falling back to `def` for out-of-range values, or document the clamping contract and the int32 bound explicitly.
- [x] **L81** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/deps.go:132` - The slice branches return a non-nil (possibly empty) slice while the `string` branch returns `nil` for an empty string and the default branch returns `nil`. Callers that distinguish "argument absent" from "argument present but empty" (e.g. `if v := argStringSlice(...); v != nil`) therefore get different results depending on how the client encoded the argument (`[]` vs `""` vs omitted). Return `nil` consistently when the normalised result is empty.
- [ ] **L82** (low/maintainability; reviewed: capability prefix filter is intentionally explicit; registry namespaces are stable) `internal/tools/deps.go:161` - The capability filter relies on a hard-coded prefix denylist that duplicates knowledge of the registry's namespaces held in the individual `registerX` functions. Any newly added or renamed tool family (for example a future `resources.*` or `hooks.*` namespace) is silently advertised as a model capability with no compile-time or test-time signal. Consider deriving the exposed set from a single explicit allowlist (or from per-tool metadata on `mcp.Tool`) so the contract cannot drift.
- [x] **L83** (low/bug) `internal/tools/documents.go:82` - A PDF that fails to parse (`pdf.Open`/`GetPlainText` error, including the recovered panic) is downgraded to an empty text plus a warning with a `nil` error. `documents.ingest` then happily stores an empty artifact and returns `ok`, and a later `documents.get` on that artifact id returns empty text with no warning at all, so the extraction failure becomes indistinguishable from a genuinely empty document. Consider returning the error for hard parse failures (only degrade to a warning for the documented scanned/OCR case), or at least preserving the warning in the stored metadata.
- [x] **L84** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/documents.go:227` - The error from the companion `.meta.json` write is discarded (`_, _ =`). A failed metadata write therefore leaves the ingest result reporting success with a text artifact that has no matching metadata, and no signal to the caller. Since `warnings` is already collected and surfaced through `WithWarnings`, propagate the failure there (or return `mcp.Fail`) instead of swallowing it.
- [x] **L85** (low/bug) `internal/tools/documents.go:264` - The `max_chars` fallback does not actually enforce the 10,000,000 cap: when the request is out of range the value is reassigned to `d.Cfg.Config().Tools.MaxTextChars`, which is itself unvalidated against 10M in `internal/config/config.go` (`dst.Tools.MaxTextChars = v` for any `v > 0`). With `max_text_chars: 50000000` the guard is a no-op and an arbitrarily large response can be returned, defeating the intended hard limit. The same pattern and issue exists in the `documents.get` handler below. Suggest re-clamping after the fallback.
- [x] **L86** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:55` - Persistence roots are inconsistent across the five tools: `eval.aggregate`/`eval.compare`/`eval.rank` write via `d.Store.Root()` while inputs are resolved against `d.Cfg.Root()` and `eval.calibrate` writes via `d.Cfg.Root()`. The two roots happen to be identical today (both are the repo root passed to `config.NewManager`/`storage.New`), but if they ever diverge the artifact is written into a different tree than the validated inputs. Pick one root for both reads and writes.
- [x] **L87** (low/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:236` - Same second-resolution collision as the ranking artifact: `evals/decisions/<sha>/<mode>-<ts>.json` is overwritten when two `eval.compare` calls with `persist:true` happen within the same second for the same snapshot/mode. Consider a nanosecond stamp or a uniqueness check before writing.
- [x] **L88** (low/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:387` - The ranking artifact path uses a second-resolution timestamp. Two `eval.rank` calls with `persist` enabled inside the same second resolve to the same `ranking-<ts>.json` and the second silently overwrites the first ranking record, losing evidence. Use a higher-resolution/nanosecond stamp or a collision-resistant suffix (e.g. random hex).
- [x] **L89** (low/bug) `internal/tools/extras.go:149` - Artifact names use a second-resolution timestamp (`20060102-150405`); `Store.WriteArtifact` writes with `os.WriteFile`, which silently overwrites an existing file. Two calls with the same title/base within the same second therefore clobber the earlier artifact (and its metadata record) instead of producing a distinct artifact. Add sub-second/uniqueness entropy (e.g. `time.Now().UTC().Format("...-150405.000000000")` or a short random/hash suffix) at this and the reports/audio/vision write sites.
- [x] **L90** (low/security) `internal/tools/files.go:29` - Returning the raw `os.Stat` error to the caller leaks the server's absolute filesystem path (and layout) in the tool response; the earlier `InputPath` error can do the same via `ensureWithin` ("resolved path escapes repository root: <abs path>"). `InputPath` has already verified the file exists and is not a directory, so surface a generic message here (e.g. "cannot read input file") while keeping the detailed error server-side.
- [x] **L91** (low/bug) `internal/tools/files.go:92` - `strings.Count(...,"\n") + 1` reports 1 line for an empty file and counts a trailing newline as an extra (empty) line, so "a\nb\n" is reported as 3 lines instead of 2. Either document this approximation or count lines properly (e.g. drop an empty trailing segment) so downstream planning doesn't over-estimate the input size.
- [x] **L92** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/knowledge.go:129` - Diagnostics gathered during extraction are dropped on the failure paths. `warnings` from `extractText` is only attached to the successful response; if chunking yields no text (or `WriteIndex` fails), the caller receives a bare error with no indication of the partial extraction issues, which are exactly the diagnostics needed to understand the failure. Surface `warnings` on these failure returns as well.
- [x] **L93** (low/bug) `internal/tools/knowledge.go:288` - `matched` is computed from `hits` after it was already truncated to `limit`, so it reports the number of *returned* hits, not the number of matching chunks. Callers that use `matched` to decide whether the query matched (or for paging) will be misled whenever the result set exceeds `limit`. Capture the pre-truncation count.
- [x] **L94** (low/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:35` - The slug guard infers "contains no letter or digit" by comparing `plans.Slug(rawSlug)` with the literal `"plan"`, which is the fallback `plans.Slug` returns for any input without valid characters. Inputs that do contain letters/digits but still normalize to `plan` (e.g. `"plan!"`, `"plan-"`, `"plan.json"` truncation edge cases) are therefore rejected with the misleading message "slug must contain at least one letter or digit". Validate the raw input directly (e.g. check for at least one `unicode.IsLetter`/`IsDigit` rune) instead of relying on the normalization fallback. The same expression is duplicated in `plan.update`.
- [x] **L95** (low/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:40` - Caller-supplied `stages` are silently ignored when the argument is present but not a JSON array (a single stage object, a string, or `null`): the type assertion fails, no entry is counted as skipped, and no warning is emitted while `plans.DefaultStages` is substituted. Distinguish "absent" from "present but wrong shape" and report a warning so the caller can tell that its stages were dropped. (The same silent substitution happens for an explicitly empty array.)
- [x] **L96** (low/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:48` - The emptiness check trims `id` but the untrimmed value is stored in `plans.Stage{ID: id, ...}`. A stage created with `" id ": " S3 "` is persisted as `" S3 "`, and `plan.update` (which matches with `argString(args, "stage")`, already trimmed) can then never address that stage and returns "unknown stage S3". Store the trimmed id. Note also that duplicate ids are accepted silently; on lookup only the first match is ever updated, so duplicates make later stages permanently unreachable.
- [x] **L97** (low/documentation) `internal/tools/resources.go:95` - The parameter documents a 5s timeout, but probeProvider applies no deadline of its own. The OpenAI/NVIDIA path enforces 5s inside `ProbeOpenAICompatible`, whereas the Telegram path falls back to the client's own default (15s, `TELEGRAM_TIMEOUT_SEC`) and can additionally wait for a 429 `retry_after` before retrying, so the documented budget does not hold for Telegram. Applying one explicit timeout in probeProvider (as suggested) makes the documented behaviour accurate for every live provider.
- [x] **L98** (low/bug) `internal/tools/resources.go:282` - `status.EnvKeys[0]` is indexed without a length check. Today `openai_api`/`nvidia_api` always carry exactly one env key from `resources.Defs()`, so this is not reachable, but a future provider definition without env keys would panic the handler here. A cheap guard (or reading the key by name) keeps the tool robust.
- [x] **L99** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/system.go:92` - This early return for the "no recommendations" case bypasses the fly-events.jsonl / fly-neuron-events.jsonl logging and the `RecordFlyPrediction` call that the normal path performs, even though `fly_route_requests_total` has already been incremented for it. As a result, the route="unknown" case is counted in metrics but invisible in the events/learning data, skewing downstream analysis. Consider emitting the event before returning or documenting that this case is intentionally not recorded.
- [x] **L100** (low/performance) `internal/tools/system.go:124` - This is a second, structurally identical `if d.Store != nil` guard in the same handler (the first opens at the fly-events block above); the two blocks can be merged under a single nil check. Also note `ActivationSnapshot` re-embeds the task (extra encoder/LLM work) immediately after `Recommend` already embedded the same task, so every recommend pays the embedding cost twice — consider reusing the vector/snapshot computed during `Recommend`.
- [x] **L101** (low/bug) `internal/tools/telegram.go:94` - A failing `getUpdates` is silently discarded: the error is not surfaced and `recordTelegramError` is not called, so the caller cannot distinguish "no recent chats" from "recent-chat discovery failed". This is also inconsistent with the `GetChat` branch just above, which records `chat_error`. Record the failure so the tool result and metrics stay truthful.
- [ ] **L102** (low/bug; reviewed: 1,5 decimal vs 1,234 thousands is documented behaviour with tests) `internal/tools/util.go:20` - The thousands/decimal heuristic is inconsistent for leading short groups: `1,5` becomes a decimal (1.5, as the test asserts) but `0,123` or `1,234` in a European-decimal file is lexed as a thousands separator and silently becomes 123 / 1234 instead of 0.123 / 1.234. Mixed formats such as `1.234,56` (both separators present) are rejected outright and the whole cell is skipped instead of parsed. Consider an explicit, documented precedence (e.g. treat 3-digit groups as thousands only when a leading group of 1–3 digits is followed by the same grouping for the entire string, and handle a trailing group of 1–2 digits as decimals), or require the caller to declare the locale.
- [x] **L103** (low/bug) `internal/tools/util.go:139` - The helper is not defensive about n: for n <= 0 the guard `len(s) <= n` is false, the loop never runs, and `s[:cut]` panics with a slice-bounds error (negative index) instead of returning an empty string. `snippet` has the same shape (`r[:limit]`). Current callers pass positive literals or values validated upstream, so this is latent, but these are generic helpers — add an early return to keep a bad limit from taking down the process.
- [x] **L104** (low/bug) `internal/tools/util.go:168` - The slug is dash-trimmed before the 60-rune cut, so a value whose 60th rune is a '-' (e.g. a long title with a hyphen at that position) is returned with a trailing dash, producing malformed artifact/file identifiers (`charts/my-title--<stamp>.svg`). Re-trim after truncation.
- [x] **L105** (low/bug) `internal/tools/xlsx.go:333` - `<is>` sets `inT = true` in the StartElement switch, but nothing clears it on `</is>`: the EndElement switch only handles `t` (and `v`). For an inline string cell without a `<t>` child (`<c t="inlineStr"><is/></c>`), `inT` stays true, so any character data following the cell (pretty-printer whitespace emitted between elements) is appended to `curVal` and can be committed as the cell value at `</c>`. Add a `case "is": inT = false` branch in the EndElement switch alongside the `t` case.
- [x] **L106** (low/bug) `internal/tools/xlsx.go:525` - Builtin format id 45 is `mm:ss` (a clock-style time) and 47 is `mmss.0`; only 46 is the true elapsed format `[h]:mm:ss`. Mapping all of 45-47 to `elapsedStyles` renders them as total `h:mm:ss`, contradicting the documented rule that elapsed formats are the ones using an `[h]`-style token. Classify 45/47 as time formats and keep only 46 as elapsed.
- [x] **L107** (low/documentation) `plugins/hackathonkit/.codex-plugin/plugin.json:12` - Stale/incorrect skill count in the interface metadata: the advertised "16 workflow skills" does not match the shipped artifacts. `plugins/hackathonkit/skills/` currently contains ~26 skill directories (each with SKILL.md + agents/openai.yaml), and README.md states "29 standard-compliant skills" / "skills/ 29 skills" (and internal/skills/catalog_test.go only guards a lower bound of 16, so it will not catch this drift). User-facing metadata should reflect the real number to avoid misleading users.
- [x] **L108** (low/maintainability) `plugins/hackathonkit/hooks/codex-hooks.json:23` - The top-level description guarantees every hook "never blocks a session", but nothing in the manifest enforces that declaratively. `PostToolUse` fires on *every* tool call (matcher `.*`) yet the command hook declares no `timeout`, so it falls back to the harness default and a slow/hung `node` invocation (or a blocked stdin read) would hold up each tool result rather than being bounded. Relying solely on the script's internal best-effort logic leaves the contract undocumented/unenforced in the manifest. Consider adding an explicit per-hook `timeout` (seconds) to make the best-effort guarantee concrete; apply the same to the `session-start` and `stop` hooks.
- [x] **L109** (low/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:11` - `-x` is true for directories (they are searchable), so if bin/hackathonkit-mcp is a directory rather than a file, the branch is taken and `exec` fails with a cryptic error instead of falling back to docker compose. Guard with `[ -f ... ] && [ -x ... ]` as the boundary condition.
- [x] **L110** (low/maintainability) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:26` - The fallback `exec`s unconditionally, so any failure inside docker compose (daemon unreachable, compose plugin broken, missing required .env variables such as POSTGRES_PASSWORD, image build failure) surfaces only as a raw docker error on the MCP stdio channel with no launcher-level context. Wrapping the invocation to emit a clear launcher message makes these failures diagnosable without changing the exit status.
- [ ] **L111** (low/maintainability; open: stop cleanup when no session id resolves) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:304` - When `stop` cannot resolve exactly one active session the session files are never cleaned up; they linger until the 12h TTL and keep influencing `lastActiveID()` for subsequent `tool-use` events. Conversely, when `id` *is* present the code logs `session_end` and calls `removeActive(id)` without checking that a file actually existed, so a bogus/unknown id is recorded as a completed session. Consider reconciling stale files (e.g. prune any active entry whose parent pid/`root` no longer matches the current hook invocation) and only logging `session_end` when a state file was actually removed.
- [x] **L112** (low/bug) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:36` - Coverage total parsing is unchecked and pipeline errors are masked (no `set -o pipefail`). `awk` coerces `$3` to a number, so a malformed/partial value on the `total:` line would be silently misread; and if `go tool cover` itself fails, only awk's status (0) surfaces, so an empty/garbage total is the only signal. Validate the parsed value's format before comparing, and consider failing fast when `go tool cover` errors.
- [x] **L113** (low/documentation) `scripts/docker-check.sh:22` - The header documents `2` as "docker unavailable", but this branch also exits `2` for a bad argument, and conversely the Docker Compose v2 plugin is never verified (`docker info` can succeed while `docker compose` is missing), in which case a missing plugin surfaces as a generic `1 failed`. Either differentiate the exit codes (e.g. keep 2 strictly for docker/unavailability and use a separate code or `2` documented as "usage/docker unavailable") or update the header comment so callers can rely on it.
- [x] **L114** (low/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:59` - Two problems on this line: (1) `sed -E` is a GNU/BSD extension and not POSIX; the shebang is `#!/usr/bin/env sh`, so on a strict POSIX `sed` the redaction step fails; (2) the trailing `|| true` then swallows that failure, and because the pipeline's stdout is what reaches stderr, a failed `redact_logs` yields *no* diagnostics at all instead of a redaction warning — the failure path degrades silently. Fall back to a plain BRE (`sed 's/\(...\).../')`) or verify sed support, and avoid masking a redaction failure without emitting a note.
- [x] **L115** (low/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:111` - Inconsistent TTY handling: the selftest run above uses `-T` to disable TTY allocation, but the quality run does not (`docker compose run --rm quality`). When the script is executed from CI/cron/captured output where stdin is not a TTY, `docker compose run` allocates a TTY and can fail with "the input device is not a TTY". Add `-T` here for parity and non-interactive safety.
- [ ] **L116** (low/performance; open: doctor.ps1 Test-NetConnection latency) `scripts/doctor.ps1:138` - `Test-NetConnection` performs a full ICMP + TCP probe and is notoriously slow (often seconds per call); running it twice adds noticeable latency to a diagnostic script. Prefer a bounded `System.Net.Sockets.TcpClient` connect (or `Test-Connection -TcpPort` on newer PowerShell).
- [x] **L117** (low/performance) `scripts/doctor.sh:73` - `docker info` is invoked up to three times in this script (here and again in the MCP-runtime branch). It can take hundreds of milliseconds — sometimes seconds when the daemon is starting — so probe once, cache the result, and reuse it for the engine and image checks.
- [ ] **L118** (low/bug; open: doctor mirror compares counts only) `scripts/doctor.sh:95` - The mirror check only compares counts, so a stale or wrong mirror with the same number of directories/SKILL.md files is reported as `[ok] skills mirror`. Compare the sorted directory names (or use `diff -r` / a checksum) so renamed or outdated skills are detected. Note also the numeric counters are compared with `=` (string equality), which should be `-eq`.
- [ ] **L119** (low/bug; open: doctor version regex greedy) `scripts/doctor.sh:128` - The greedy `.*"version"...` regex matches the *last* `"version"` occurrence on a line and `head -n 1` then takes the first such line, so a nested/embedded version field (or a one-line JSON with several `version` keys) yields a misleading version string — or an empty one if the value is not a plain string. Parse the top-level field with jq/python3 when available, or anchor the pattern to the start of the line (e.g. `^[[:space:]]*"version"`). The same pattern is duplicated for `resources` `"summary"` below.
- [x] **L120** (low/maintainability) `scripts/doctor.sh:174` - All smoke output is discarded, so a failing `mcp handshake` check prints only `scripts/smoke.sh` with no root cause; the plugin-runtime branch similarly reduces the whole stderr to `tail -n 1`. Capture the output and echo the last few lines on failure (as `scripts/plugin-check.sh` does) so the doctor report is actionable.
- [x] **L121** (low/bug) `scripts/env-set.sh:62` - The file is created (empty) before the content is produced, and `mv` would create it anyway. If the subsequent `awk` fails, `set -e` aborts but the freshly created empty `$FILE` is left behind, changing filesystem state on a failed run. Either drop this pre-creation or ensure the empty placeholder is removed on failure.
- [x] **L122** (low/maintainability) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:30` - Several assertions are invoked with only two arguments (e.g. this one and `new key is appended`, `literal key is replaced`, `lookalike key is untouched`), so `detail` is empty and a failure prints just `[fail] <name>` with no value to inspect. Pass a diagnostic detail (the actual file contents/mode) for these cases too, so failures are debuggable without re-running manually.
- [x] **L123** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:4` - The documented contract is an *absolute* root, but a caller-supplied `$1` is accepted verbatim; a relative value (e.g. `sh scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh .`) passes the `[ -d "$ROOT" ]` check and is then written into the manifest as a relative path, breaking the MCP launcher. Also note `[ -z "$ROOT" ]` on line 7 is effectively dead code: `${1:-...}` already substitutes the default for an empty `$1`. Validate that the resolved root is absolute, e.g.: ```sh case "$ROOT" in /*) ;; *) echo "[mcp-manifest] repository root must be an absolute path: '$ROOT'" >&2; exit 1 ;; esac ```
- [x] **L124** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:23` - The substitution loop is capped at 50 passes with hardcoded offsets (`i + 13`, `i + 16`). If `$ROOT` itself contains `{{REPO_ROOT}}`, each pass inserts a fresh occurrence, so the line grows on every iteration until the cap is hit and the run fails via the `grep` guard — a confusing failure mode. More generally, exceeding the cap silently leaves placeholders behind. Consider replacing only the first occurrence per line (single `index`/`substr` pass) or documenting/verifying the offsets against the placeholder constants instead of magic numbers.
- [x] **L125** (low/maintainability) `scripts/repair.sh:50` - The final message uses a relative path even though the script computes an absolute ROOT and may be invoked from any working directory; it also prints "done" unconditionally, including after the runtime-unavailable warning above. Reference the resolved path and avoid signalling success when a prerequisite failed.
- [x] **L126** (low/security) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:44` - When the user answers "no" or skips a prompt, the previous value is never removed from .env, so a provider can be recorded as `false` in the profile while a stale credential of the same name stays in .env indefinitely. At minimum warn the user that existing keys are kept; ideally offer to remove/unset them (env-set.sh would need an unset mode).
- [x] **L127** (low/bug) `scripts/setup.sh:16` - `--profile=` (attached form with an empty value) is accepted here with `PROFILE=""` and only later rejected by the `case` below with the message "invalid profile: (expected core or full)" — inconsistent with the separated form, which explicitly detects the empty value and prints a clearer "--profile requires a value". Reject the empty attached value here as well for a uniform error, and consider ignoring/rejecting a repeated `--profile` instead of silently letting the last one win.
- [ ] **L128** (low/maintainability; open: setup.sh hardcoded explicit-only skill list) `scripts/setup.sh:59` - The list of "explicit-only" skills is hardcoded inline and duplicated in the human-readable echo below. Any rename/addition in the skill catalog silently turns into a no-op here (the `rm -rf` succeeds without removing anything) or, worse, leaves stale mirrors, and nothing verifies the intended skills were actually pruned. Drive the list from a single declarative source (e.g. a manifest file or a `SKILLS_EXCLUDE` variable shared with the plugin definition) and fail/warn when an expected skill directory is absent.
- [ ] **L129** (low/bug; open: setup.sh fallback go guard) `scripts/setup.sh:81` - The fallback branch assumes the Go toolchain exists. If `go` is missing (a plausible state on the very machines that lack a working Docker engine), `go build` fails and `set -e` aborts the script after `.env` and the mirrors have already been mutated. Check `command -v go` first and print an actionable message (or make this branch a warning rather than a hard failure).
- [x] **L130** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.ps1:77` - Coupling the ordering assertion to `id == parsedCount` assumes contiguous, ascending ids starting at 1 and is redundant with the later explicit `($ids -join ",") -ne "1,2,3"` check. It is also brittle: a non-JSON line does not increment `$parsedCount`, so every subsequent valid response then reports a confusing "unexpected response order" error instead of the real parse failure. Rely on the collected-id comparison (or compare against the expected id array) and drop this per-line counter check.
- [x] **L131** (low/documentation) `scripts/smoke.sh:112` - This success message asserts "stdout is JSON-RPC only", but the grep fallback never verifies that: extra non-JSON lines are not detected there (the python3 branch does enforce exactly 3 lines). Either drop the purity claim from the fallback path or make the fallback check for unexpected extra output.
- [x] **L132** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:10` - The script declares `#!/usr/bin/env sh` (POSIX sh) but traps the non-POSIX signal name `EXIT`; POSIX specifies `0` for this trap. On stricter /bin/sh implementations the cleanup trap may not be installed, leaking the temp dir. Use `trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' 0 INT TERM` (or add `HUP`) for portability.
- [x] **L133** (low/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:6` - Only `set -e` is enabled (no `-u`, no pipefail) and the delegated `sync-skills.sh` is invoked with no error context: if it fails or does not exist, the whole script aborts with an opaque message from the child, making harness failures hard to diagnose. Verify the script exists and emit a harness-specific error on non-zero exit.
- [x] **L134** (low/maintainability) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:41` - The same `MISSING` counter is incremented for both missing files and invalid JSON, so the final message cannot distinguish the two and users get no accurate diagnosis. Track invalid manifests separately so the summary reports each condition correctly.
- [x] **L135** (low/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:25` - `Copy-Item -Recurse` follows reparse points found in the source: a directory symlink/junction under `plugins/hackathonkit/skills` is expanded (duplicating external content, or looping / blowing MAX_PATH on Windows PowerShell 5.1), while a dangling link makes the whole copy fail. Since this script mirrors trusted-but-inspectable repo content, either reject reparse points before copying (`$_.Attributes -band [IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint`) or use a copy method that preserves them as links.
- [x] **L136** (low/maintainability) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:12` - `find`'s `-mindepth`/`-maxdepth` are not POSIX; they are GNU/BSD extensions. On a strict/minimal POSIX `find` (the script is declared `#!/usr/bin/env sh`) these primaries fail, and with `set -e` the assignment fails too. If true POSIX portability is intended, restrict the match with a depth test, or document the GNU/BSD `find` requirement.
- [x] **L137** (low/documentation) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:62` - The final message claims `data/` was kept, but `data\control` is in the deletion target list, so the message is misleading. Update the wording to reflect exactly what was preserved/removed.
- [x] **L138** (low/maintainability) `scripts/uninstall.sh:26` - When `codex` is not on PATH the whole codex section is skipped silently, so the dry run does not preview the plugin/marketplace removal that a later `--yes` run (on a machine with codex installed) would perform. Print a note in the dry-run branch so the preview is complete, e.g. "would run: codex plugin remove ... (codex not found on PATH)".
- [x] **L139** (low/bug) `scripts/validate-skills.sh` - `dirname "$0"` is relative to the invocation path, not the script's real location: when the script is run through `PATH` (bare `validate-skills.sh`) or via a symlink, `$0` has no/incorrect directory component, so `$ROOT` resolves to the wrong tree and the validator silently checks the wrong catalog — a false "pass" for a validation gate. Consider resolving the script path (e.g. `command -v "$0"`, plus `readlink -f` when available) and failing loudly if the resolved root does not contain `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp` (or `bin/hackathonkit-mcp`), instead of validating an arbitrary directory.
- [x] **L140** (low/bug) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:6` - `[ -x path ]` is also true for a directory (or any executable-but-not-regular file) named `bin/hackathonkit-mcp`. In that case `exec` fails immediately with exit 126 and the script never reaches the `go run` fallback, hiding the real cause. Guard the exec path with a regular-file test.
- [ ] **L141** (low/bug; open: write-install-state.ps1 profile/runtime validation) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:3` - $Profile and $Runtime are free-form strings with no validation, so typos or unexpected values are written straight into install-state.json and may break consumers that expect a fixed set. Constrain them with ValidateSet (adjust the allowed values to the real domain).
- [ ] **L142** (low/bug; open: write-install-state.ps1 mirror name validation) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:17` - Validation only compares directory counts, so a partially copied/truncated mirror, a renamed/stale mirror with the same number of directories, or a source with zero skill directories all pass as complete. Additionally nested skill layouts are undercounted and access errors are swallowed into 0. Compare actual directory names or file-level content (relative paths/file counts or hashes) and fail when the source count is zero. Affects scripts/write-install-state.ps1, scripts/sync-skills.ps1, and scripts/doctor.ps1.

---

## Scan 3 (final verification pass, after v0.16.13)

Second full scan (147 files, 421 findings: 5 critical / 29 high / 203 medium / 184 low).
The 5 criticals and the 29 highs were fixed in v0.16.14 (the scan caught regressions
introduced by the earlier fix batches); mediums/lows are the next backlog.

- [x] **S3-C1** (critical/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:195` - On terminals narrower than 54 columns the layout sets `rightWidth = 0`, but the right pane is still built *before* the `if rightWidth > 0` guard, so `truncate` is invoked with negative limits (`rightWidth-6` = -6, `rightWidth-10` = -10) for the task, worktree and every event label. `truncate` (main.go) does `runes[:limit]`, and a negative slice bound panics ("slice bounds out of range"), so the TUI crashes whenever the window is narrow and any of these strings is non-empty. Clamp the widths to >= 0 (or move the right-pane construction inside an `if rightWidth > 0` block).
- [x] **S3-C2** (critical/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:53` - Infinite loop: the replacement keeps the marker itself (`text[:idx+len(marker)]` ends with e.g. `password=`), and the inner loop restarts scanning from offset 0, so `idx` is found at the same position forever and the `idx < 0` break can never be reached. Any error containing `password=`, `passwd=`, `secret=` or `token=` (exactly the Postgres/MinIO errors this helper exists for) makes the process spin at 100% CPU and never start the server. The scan must resume *after* the inserted replacement instead of restarting at 0 — e.g. keep a `searchFrom` offset (initialized to 0 before the inner `for`, used as `strings.Index(strings.ToLower(text[searchFrom:]), marker)` with `idx = searchFrom + rel`) and advance it past the replacement here.
- [x] **S3-C3** (critical/bug) `internal/evaluation/bundle.go:160` - Base-path mismatch during the walk: `WalkDir` is rooted at `rootResolved` (symlinks resolved) but `filepath.Rel` is computed against the unresolved `root`. When the repository path itself traverses a symlink (e.g. a macOS `/tmp`→`/private/tmp` workspace), `root != rootResolved`, so every yielded `path` is not under `root` and `Rel` returns a rel path with leading `..` components (e.g. `../private/tmp/proj/README.md`). Consequences: exclude and demo-profile matching silently fail (patterns no longer line up with the real relative path), the `outRel` skip check no longer matches, and `dst := filepath.Join(outAbs, relSlash)` escapes `outAbs`, writing copies (and thus the bundle hash) to unintended locations. Use the same base that was passed to `WalkDir`.
- [x] **S3-C4** (critical/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:46` - The retry loop silently falls through after 10 failed `mkdir` attempts and execution continues **without holding the lock**, so two concurrent runs can still interleave the `mv`-based swap. Note also that `sleep 0.2` is not portable POSIX (a strict `/bin/sh`, per the `#!/usr/bin/env sh` shebang, only guarantees integer seconds). Track acquisition explicitly and abort if the lock cannot be obtained; use an integer sleep for portability.
- [x] **S3-C5** (critical/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:52` - This single trap has three problems: (1) it is installed unconditionally even when `mkdir` never succeeded, so on exit it deletes a lock owned by another process, silently breaking the mutex; (2) it *replaces* the earlier `trap cleanup_tmp EXIT` and `on_signal`, so after this point a later failure (e.g. the fallback `mv` failing) leaves the `.tmp.$$` tree behind; (3) on INT/TERM it only removes the lock and does **not** exit, so the interrupted run resumes the swap without holding the lock. Keep TMP cleanup in the EXIT path and only remove the lock when it was actually acquired.
- [x] **S3-H1** (high/security) `.codex/agents/evaluator.toml:6` - `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"` grants write (and command execution) across the whole workspace while this agent ingests attacker-controllable artifacts (README/TASK.md/demo output/code). The "never edit source files" guarantee lives only in a TOML comment, which is not part of `developer_instructions` and is therefore never surfaced to the model — so it is not an enforced constraint. A prompt-injection payload in a scored artifact could drive destructive writes. Scope the write access to the report tree and state the no-edit rule as an explicit instruction line (e.g. "- Never create, modify or delete source files; the only artifacts you write are the judge report JSON under evals/judges/.").
- [x] **S3-H2** (high/bug) `.codex/config.toml:6` - `command` is repository-relative and points at a POSIX `.sh`, so it only works when Codex is launched from the repo root on POSIX: subdirectory/temp cwd launches cannot spawn the server, and Windows users get a hard startup failure. Use an absolute `REPO_ROOT` path (as the generated `plugins/hackathonkit/.mcp.json` does) and either a platform-neutral dispatcher or a per-platform generated file (`.cmd` on Windows) instead of relying on the executable bit or a hand-edited version-controlled file.
- [x] **S3-H3** (high/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:225` - Residual of H5: `active_kc` is still taken from `lastNeuron` (the newest neuron record of any type). Per `internal/tools/system.go` the `type: "recommend"` record is the only one carrying `active_kc`; once a feedback is logged, the newest record is `type: "feedback"` (fields: ts/type/trace_id/source/reward/pam/ppl1/chosen) which has no `active_kc`, so the highlight is silently emptied and, when it does resolve, it can belong to a different trace. Derive `active_kc` from the newest recommend record.
- [x] **S3-H4** (high/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:226` - Double-applied feedback. When the newest neuron record *is* the feedback record (the normal case, since feedback is appended last), this line already runs the `ev.type === 'feedback'` branch of applyNeuronEvent; the synthesized call below (guarded only by `fbKey`) then runs the same branch again for the same logical event. Result: dopamine is incremented twice (+44 instead of +22), hunger drops twice, and the verdict is written twice per single reward/punish. Deduplicate the two paths (e.g. skip the synthesized event when the neuron-path feedback was already applied, keyed by trace_id/ts).
- [x] **S3-H5** (high/bug) `cmd/fly-bench/main.go:154` - A run in which the model-backed signals never engage still exits 0 and writes a SHA-keyed report under evals/fly-ab/, so invalid runs are indistinguishable from real A/B evidence. `fly.Router.Recommend` returns no error and swallows every dependency failure: `embedTask`/`embeddingCategoryScores`/`mbCategoryScores`/`llmCategoryScores` all collapse a failed embed/LLM call (or an unloadable brain/memory file) into `ok=false`, and `ensureCentroids` does not cache a failed build, so it retries the network per row. The result is a report full of zero recall for the Fly side that is still persisted and reported as evidence (`evidence: <path>`) with exit code 0 — easy to commit from CI. Add a validity gate before `save`, e.g. fail (non-zero exit and/or `Valid: false` in the report) when no row produced a Fly recommendation, when the encoder never contains a model signal (`embedding`/`knn`/`mb`/`llm`) for any row, or when the warmup `full.Recommend` produced no model signal. The warmup result is currently discarded, so an unreachable embedder is also invisible there.
- [x] **S3-H6** (high/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:174` - Deferred call ordering makes `daemonForeground` hang on any `ListenAndServe` failure. Deferred calls run LIFO, and `defer ingestWG.Wait()` is registered *after* `defer stop()`, so on the error path (e.g. port already in use / bind failure) the context is never cancelled before the wait: the ticker goroutine is still blocked in its `select` on `ctx.Done()`, so `ingestWG.Wait()` blocks forever. The deferred `store.Close()`, pid-file removal and `stop()` never run — the daemon stays alive holding a stale pid file, and subsequent `daemon start` reports "already running". Normal signal shutdown works only because the signal cancels ctx first. Stop the context before waiting: Note: the earlier `defer stop()` can stay (cancel is idempotent) or be removed once this defer handles it.
- [x] **S3-H7** (high/bug) `internal/control/handoff.go:56` - `PendingDecisions` can never reflect real data: `store.Events` filters with `WHERE session_id=?` (store.go:327), but every `eval_decision` event is produced by `ingestDecisions`, which appends `Event{TS:..., Type: "eval_decision", Payload: payload}` with **no SessionID** (ingest.go:243-247), i.e. it is stored with `session_id=''`. For any real session id this query therefore counts 0 decisions (the tests only pass because they hand-roll `AppendEvent` with `SessionID: "s-1"`). Secondary, semantic issue: `eval_decision` marks a *recorded* decision (ingest.go:245, discriminated by risk.go:42) and has no unresolved state, so counting it as "pending" is misleading even if the query were fixed. Suggest either attributing decision events to a session at ingest time, or counting over a session-agnostic/dedicated query (a `COUNT(*)` would also avoid materializing and JSON-unmarshalling up to 500 full events just to derive an integer).
- [x] **S3-H8** (high/bug) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:624` - `Unresolved` is a flat list of every tied snapshot sha, but `ApplyCommission` treats it as one contiguous tie group: it inserts all unresolved entries at the position of the first unresolved entry and skips the rest. With two independent ties (e.g. A/B tied at 80 and D/E tied at 60, with C alone at 70) the commission must vote on all four, and the lower tier D/E is re-inserted before C — a strictly lower-scoring entry ends up ranked above C. Preserve tie tiers (e.g. keep groups as `[][]string` / resolve one contiguous group per call and require votes only for that group).
- [x] **S3-H9** (high/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:324` - `Promote` performs the same load-modify-save cycle as `Record` but never takes the cross-process `.lock` file that `lockFile` exists for (`Record` does). The store path is shared between the long-running MCP server and the `hackathonkit-ctl` CLI, and `save` replaces the whole file via `os.Rename`, so a concurrent Promote in one process silently discards learnings recorded/updated by the other (last writer wins). Wrap the critical section in `lockFile()` exactly as `Record` does.
- [x] **S3-H10** (high/bug) `internal/quality/quality.go:127` - The substring test `strings.Contains(existing, "-buildvcs")` also matches an inherited `-buildvcs=true`. In that case the documented workaround is silently skipped, Go keeps VCS-stamping the build, and the container/root-owned-.git scenario described in the comment (exit status 128) reappears — exactly the failure this function exists to prevent. Only skip when the flag is already set to `false`; appending `-buildvcs=false` after an earlier `-buildvcs=true` is safe because the last flag wins.
- [x] **S3-H11** (high/bug) `internal/quality/quality.go:142` - `Write` truncates `p` to the remaining capacity and then returns `len(p)` of the *truncated* slice. When the buffer is not yet full but the incoming chunk is larger than the remaining space, the returned count is smaller than the number of bytes the caller passed, with a nil error. `os/exec` feeds `Stdout`/`Stderr` writers through `io.Copy` from a pipe goroutine, and `io.Copy` converts such a short write with nil error into `io.ErrShortWrite`, which `cmd.Wait` then returns. Consequence: any check whose combined output exceeds `maxCheckOutput` (256 KiB) is reported as `failed` even when the command exited 0 — the gate spuriously fails on chatty-but-successful runs. Return the original length (the buffer already silently caps, which is the intended behaviour).
