- [x] **S3-H12** (high/bug) `internal/tools/context.go:217` - `manifestTokenWarn` is advertised in `limits.manifest_tokens_warn`, but no manifest ever receives a `Flag`, so a 50k-token manifest is reported as within budget and yields no recommendation. `total` is accumulated here and then discarded (written, never read) — exactly the accumulator the missing check would have used. Flag oversized manifests (or an aggregate) and drop the dead accumulator (H27 is still not actually fixed).
- [x] **S3-H13** (high/security) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:106` - `repoRoot()` still falls back to the unvalidated working directory. Every downstream consumer (CONTROL_DIR, SESSIONS_DIR, SESSIONS_LOG, EVENTS_LOG) is derived from `ROOT`, so in an untrusted cwd the hook writes session state/logs into that directory — and, worse, it spawns processes from it: `binaryContext()` will happily execute `<cwd>/bin/hackathonkit-mcp`, and the fallback path runs `docker compose -f <ROOT>/docker-compose.yml run hackathonkit-mcp`, so an attacker-influenced working directory could ship a `docker-compose.yml` (or a `bin/hackathonkit-mcp`) defining an arbitrary image/command, giving code execution during `session-start`. The comment above only rules out the plugin-cache path, not arbitrary cwd. Return an empty/no root and let the entrypoint exit without touching the filesystem or spawning anything when no candidate validated; additionally gate the docker fallback on `isHackathonKitRepo(ROOT)` (and verify the compose file is inside a validated repo) before spawning docker.
- [x] **S3-H14** (high/bug) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:13` - The value `.` (or `.5`) passes both the character-class and dot-count checks, and `awk` then coerces it to `0`, silently turning the coverage floor off instead of rejecting the malformed target. Replace the ad-hoc dot-count test with a full decimal-format check that requires at least one digit.
- [x] **S3-H15** (high/security) `scripts/docker-check.sh:53` - Silent unredacted fallback: if the probe for `sed -E` fails (BSD/macOS sed before -E support, BusyBox/minimal images, etc.), `redact_logs` degrades to plain `cat`, so the full `docker compose logs`/`ps` output — including DB passwords, MinIO root credentials, DSNs and tokens — is dumped verbatim into CI output. A redaction feature must fail safe, not fail open. Try `-r` as a second option and, if neither works, print a loud warning (and prefer dropping/truncating the dump) instead of echoing secrets.
- [x] **S3-H16** (high/bug) `scripts/doctor.sh:35` - This assignment is not guarded, unlike the jq branch above it. The embedded Python only catches exceptions from `json.load`; if codex emits valid JSON that is not an object (e.g. a list), `data.get(...)` raises `AttributeError`, the interpreter exits non-zero, and because the script runs with `set -e` the whole doctor run aborts with no `[doctor] ...` summary instead of degrading gracefully. Wrap it (e.g. `if ! PLUGIN_STATE=$(...); then PLUGIN_STATE=unknown; fi`) or make the Python defensive (`data = json.load(...); if not isinstance(data, dict): sys.exit(0)`), so a malformed/unexpected payload is reported rather than killing the script.
- [x] **S3-H17** (high/bug) `scripts/doctor.sh:60` - The awk fallback never emits `disabled`: once a `"name": "hackathonkit"` entry is seen, `found=1` is set and this line prints `enabled` even when the entry says `"enabled": false` (or has no `enabled` key). A disabled plugin is therefore reported as installed+enabled, `PLUGIN_OK=1`, and the plugin-runtime check is executed against a plugin that is not actually enabled. It should print `disabled` here (and let the `enabled` case above print `enabled`), matching the jq/python3 branches.
- [x] **S3-H18** (high/bug) `scripts/doctor.sh:117` - This command substitution is unguarded: when `.agents/skills` does not exist the `cd` fails, the substitution returns non-zero and, under `set -e`, the entire doctor script aborts before the `skills mirror` check is ever printed — precisely in the state that check is meant to report ("run scripts/sync-harness.sh"). `SRC_NAMES` above has the same exposure when `plugins/hackathonkit/skills` is absent. Append `|| true` (or compute the listing without `cd`) so the failure surfaces as a failed check rather than an aborted run.
- [x] **S3-H19** (high/bug) `scripts/env-set.sh:75` - Lock acquisition failure is not fatal. If all 10 `mkdir` attempts fail (e.g. a stale lock left by a crashed run, or a concurrent writer holding it), the loop simply falls through and the rewrite proceeds **without** the lock, so concurrent invocations can interleave and silently lose updates. The script must abort when the lock cannot be acquired. Also note `sleep 0.1` is not guaranteed by POSIX (only integer seconds are), so use a portable delay.
- [x] **S3-H20** (high/bug) `scripts/env-set.sh:81` - This second `trap` overwrites the earlier `trap 'rm -f "$TMP"' EXIT INT TERM`, so the temp file (which contains the secret) is no longer removed on EXIT/INT/TERM. A signal arriving after this point but before `mv` leaves the secret on disk. Additionally, the INT/TERM handlers do not `exit`, so the shell resumes the script after a signal with the lock already deleted. Use a single cleanup trap and make signal handlers terminate.
- [x] **S3-H21** (high/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.ps1:63` - The MCP server subprocess is launched with a plain pipeline and awaited with no timeout. If the server hangs, ignores stdin, or never exits (or a broken-pipe error is swallowed because `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"`), this check blocks forever, hanging CI. Use a bounded wait, e.g. start the process with redirected stdin/stdout and `WaitForExit($timeout)`, or run the pipeline inside a job/`Start-Process` with a timeout and kill the process tree on expiry.
- [x] **S3-H22** (high/bug) `scripts/repair.sh:61` - Non-idempotent plugin step that fails the whole repair. `codex plugin add` errors out when the plugin is already installed, so re-running `repair.sh` on an otherwise healthy install hits the `else` branch and `exit 1` — after the harness, directories, binaries and install state were all successfully repaired, making a successful repair look failed. `setup.sh` handles this by removing the plugin first and only warning on failure. Mirror that: run `codex plugin remove hackathonkit` before the add (ignore its status) and downgrade the failure to a warning with the manual command.
- [x] **S3-H23** (high/bug) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:44` - Silent no-op on an empty secret breaks the stated invariant. `SPEC` (openai_api=true, nvidia_api=true, ...) is computed from the y/N answers *before* the secrets are collected, but `set_key` returns 0 without writing anything when the user answers yes and then leaves the key prompt empty. `mcp --resources-set` is still called with the `true` flags, so `configs/resources.json` advertises a provider whose credential was never stored — exactly what the comment at line 88 claims cannot happen. The same applies to a partial set (e.g. Telegram token stored but chat id skipped, or MinIO endpoint stored without access/secret key). Fix: have `set_key` fail on an empty value and derive the booleans from whether the write actually succeeded (i.e. build `SPEC` after the secret-collection block, downgrading any flag whose key was skipped), or at minimum warn the user that the resource will be recorded as available without a key.
- [x] **S3-H24** (high/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:21` - Signal handling breaks the atomic-swap guarantee. `on_signal` only removes the staging dir; if INT/TERM arrives after `.claude/skills` has been moved to `$CLAUDE_OLD` (lines 35-37) but before the new tmp is moved in, the script exits 130 leaving `.claude/skills` **absent** and an orphaned `.claude/skills.old.$$` behind, with no automatic restore on the next run. Extend cleanup to roll the old mirror back (and install the same handler on EXIT, since `set -e` failures in that window have the same effect). `CLAUDE_OLD` must be defined before the trap is installed.
- [x] **S3-H25** (high/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:28` - The integrity guard is too shallow to justify the swap: it counts only *top-level* directories, ignores files and nested content entirely, and omits hidden entries (no `-Force`, while the copy above uses `-Force`). A truncated/partial copy, a missing nested file, or an empty skill directory all pass. It also under/over-counts asymmetrically: a top-level directory symlink is excluded from the copy but still counted by `Get-ChildItem -Directory` in `$srcCount`, so the check fails spuriously; and when `$src` contains zero skill directories both counts are 0 and the script happily publishes an empty mirror over the existing one. Compare recursive file and `SKILL.md` counts (with `-Force`) and fail when `$srcCount` is 0, as `sync-skills.sh` does.
- [x] **S3-H26** (high/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:34` - The swap is not atomic and has no rollback, contradicting the comment above (`consumers never observe a missing or half-populated mirror`). `Remove-Item $dst` destroys the live mirror before `Move-Item` recreates it, so any failure in between (locked file/AV, permission, crash) or a `Move-Item` failure leaves `.agents/skills` absent or partially deleted — and the `finally` block then deletes `$tmp`, so the freshly built mirror is lost too, with no way back. Rename the old mirror aside, move the new one in, restore the backup if the move fails, then delete the backup. The shell twin (`sync-skills.sh`) already does this.
- [x] **S3-H27** (high/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:63` - `trap - EXIT INT TERM` clears the EXIT trap *before* the lock is released, and the trap was the only place `$LOCK` was ever removed. As a result every successful run leaves `.agents/skills.lock` behind permanently; the next run then waits ~2s and (because of the fall-through above) proceeds anyway. Release the lock explicitly before resetting the traps.
- [x] **S3-H28** (high/bug) `scripts/uninstall.sh:55` - `rm -rf` on the whole `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` trees does not match the parallel PowerShell implementation. `scripts/uninstall.ps1` (lines 48-62) treats these as *shared* skill directories and removes only the sub-directories that this plugin ships (`plugins/hackathonkit/skills/*`), explicitly so that "user-authored skills survive". The shell version deletes the entire mirror, so any skill the user added to those directories is destroyed irreversibly. This also contradicts the header contract ("committed source stays in place"). Mirror the ps1 behaviour: iterate over the plugin's own skill directories and remove only `$ROOT/.agents/skills/<skill>` / `$ROOT/.claude/skills/<skill>`.
- [x] **S3-H29** (high/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:49` - `configs\` is never ensured to exist, and `Set-Content` has no error handling. When `$Root\configs` is absent, `Set-Content` raises a **non-terminating** error, so the script falls through to the `Write-Host` success message and exits 0 while nothing was written — a false success. Create the directory first and treat write failures as terminating.
- [x] **S3-M1** (medium/bug; reviewed: Claude Code requires a POSIX shell; Windows users run under WSL) `.claude-plugin/plugin.json:22` - Residual of M1 (marked fixed in docs/REVIEW-BACKLOG.md:66, but still present here): the static manifest hardcodes the POSIX launcher `hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh`. The repo ships a Windows counterpart (`plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.cmd`) and selects the platform-correct launcher when generating the runtime manifest (`scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.ps1:32` substitutes `{{MCP_LAUNCHER}}` with `hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.cmd`). Because this plugin manifest is static, a Windows Claude Code install will try to spawn the `.sh` directly and fail to start the `hackathonkit` MCP server even though a working `.cmd` launcher exists. Make the entrypoint platform-neutral (e.g. a small node/sh dispatcher that picks `.cmd` on Windows), or point `mcpServers` at a file generated the same way as `plugins/hackathonkit/.mcp.json` (`.mcp.json.template` + `gen-mcp-manifest.*`).
- [x] **S3-M2** (medium/maintainability) `.codex/agents/evaluator.toml:15` - The instruction chain depends entirely on the Hackathon Agent Kit MCP tools (`eval.aggregate`, `eval.compare`) and on `$self-improvement-controller`, but no fallback is defined if the MCP server or that skill is unavailable — unlike `researcher.toml`, which degrades explicitly. In that state the agent is told to call tools that do not exist and may emit nothing parseable. Add a degradation clause (e.g. still emit the validated per-judge JSON and explicitly report the missing aggregate/compare step) so the failure mode is not silent.
- [x] **S3-M3** (medium/bug) `.codex/agents/researcher.toml:6` - Residual of backlog item M2 (marked fixed, but not visible here): `sandbox_mode = "read-only"` is declared while the instructions require state-persisting MCP calls (`reports.create`, `learning.record`, and in the skill `knowledge.index`/`documents.ingest`). `reviewer.toml` (lines 4-5) documents its read-only semantics explicitly; this file says nothing, so it is undefined whether the MCP server runs outside the sandbox. If read-only also covers MCP writes, the promised `theme-research-<slug>` artifact id can never be produced and the failure is silent. State the intended behaviour (MCP runs outside the sandbox, or the orchestrator persists the returned brief).
- [x] **S3-M4** (medium/security) `.codex/agents/researcher.toml:15` - The redaction rule only names "the brief", but the same source text is also written to the `reports.create` artifact and to `learning.record` evidence, which persist beyond the session. The secret classes are also incomplete (no private keys, session cookies, bearer headers or personal data), and there is no rule for the case where a passage cannot be quoted without exposing a secret.
- [x] **S3-M5** (medium/bug) `.codex/agents/researcher.toml:16` - Residual of backlog item M5 (marked fixed, only partially): the availability check still enumerates an incomplete tool set — `files.describe` (skill step 3) and `learning.recall` (skill step 2) are covered by neither wildcard nor by name, so their absence is never detected. It also still assumes web search works, yet `.codex/config.toml` ships `web_search = "disabled"`, in which case this branch degrades to "provided documents" with no instruction left, and the skill's "no web access -> corpus only, list external gaps, do not guess" rule is not restated.
- [x] **S3-M6** (medium/bug) `.codex/agents/researcher.toml:17` - Residual of backlog item M6 (marked fixed, only partially): "Return the $theme-research contract" delegates correctly, but the enumerated summary that follows omits three mandatory elements of the skill's Output Contract (theme-research/SKILL.md lines 98-107): a confidence level per claim and per question answer, the closing "What would change our mind" falsifiers section, and the coverage statement (questions settled vs. open, sources unavailable). Since the list reads as the authoritative shape, an agent may emit a brief that fails the skill's own Verification checklist.
- [x] **S3-M7** (medium/security) `.codex/agents/reviewer.toml:6` - `sandbox_mode` alone does not contain untrusted evidence: `read-only` still permits process execution and network access (reviewer), and `workspace-write` with no `approval_policy`/`network_access` leaves destructive writes and exfiltration to prompt compliance (evaluator). Since these agents ingest attacker-controllable artifacts, constrain execution/network by configuration (e.g. disable network access and set an explicit approval policy) or document the residual risk explicitly.
- [x] **S3-M8** (medium/bug) `.codex/config.toml:8` - Residual of M11: 120s startup is still tight for this launcher. When `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` is absent the script falls back to `docker compose run --rm -T hackathonkit-mcp`, which on a cold machine pulls/builds images and starts dependencies — routinely more than 120s — so Codex aborts the server before it answers `initialize`. The recommended budget was 180s. `tool_timeout_sec = 120` is likewise still undocumented against the slowest tools (ingestion, `quality.gate`, research passes) and there is no retry path in the config; either raise/justify these values or state the required overrides.
- [x] **S3-M9** (medium/bug) `.gitattributes:1` - Residual of backlog item M14 (marked fixed, but the fix is not present): `* text=auto` only normalizes content *inside* the repository, it does not pin the EOL used on checkout. Every path without an explicit rule below — `Dockerfile`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `Makefile`, `LICENSE`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitattributes` itself, `*.txt`/`*.sql` data, extensionless scripts — is therefore checked out with the platform-native ending (CRLF on Windows), so the Windows working tree diverges from Linux CI and a CRLF `Dockerfile` (copied into the build via `COPY . .`) is fragile with line continuations. Pin LF globally and keep CRLF as an explicit override for the Windows script types only.
- [x] **S3-M10** (medium/bug) `.gitattributes:17` - Residual of backlog item M15 (marked fixed, but no exclusion rules exist here): nothing declares binary content, so images, archives, compiled artifacts and fixtures rely solely on Git's heuristic text detection and a misclassified file can be line-ending-translated/corrupted during normalization. Add explicit `binary` (`-text -diff`) markers for the binary types the repo/tooling produces, including the artifacts built into `bin/` and any test fixtures.
- [x] **S3-M11** (medium/other) `.github/workflows/ci.yml:3` - The workflow triggers on `push` to main, every `pull_request` and manual dispatch but has no `concurrency` group, so superseded runs are queued instead of cancelled. With the heavy docker-stack job this wastes runner minutes on commits that are already outdated. Add a concurrency group with `cancel-in-progress` (guarded so pushes to main are not cancelled).
- [x] **S3-M12** (medium/other) `.github/workflows/ci.yml:14` - None of the three jobs (`quality`, `docker`, `windows`) declares `timeout-minutes`, so they inherit the 6-hour default. The docker job runs `docker-check.sh --full` (compose config, image builds, readiness probes) and the quality job runs a full clean-checkout rehearsal that clones + rebuilds the repo — either can hang on a stuck build/probe and pin a runner for hours before the workflow is killed. Add an explicit timeout to each job (tighten for the docker job).
- [x] **S3-M13** (medium/security) `.github/workflows/ci.yml:50` - The failure diagnostics dump full container logs (`docker compose logs ... --tail=200`) with no redaction or masking. Container stdout can include environment variables, tokens or credentials injected by the compose stack / local `.env`, and on a public repo those logs become permanently visible. Prefer targeted `docker compose ps`/inspect output, or filter/redact before printing (e.g. pipe through a mask step) rather than dumping raw service logs.
- [x] **S3-M14** (medium/bug) `.gitignore:1` - `bin/` (like `logs/`) is unanchored, so it matches at any depth and can unintentionally exclude a same-named source, fixture, or `testdata/bin/` directory. Since the executables above are root-anchored, anchor this one too unless nested `bin/` directories are intentionally ignored.
- [x] **S3-M15** (medium/maintainability) `.gitignore:20` - `configs/install-state.json` and `configs/resources.json` are ignored wholesale. If either is a non-secret template required for a fresh clone to run, ignoring it breaks setup; and if it is generated state, ignoring it silently hides schema changes from review. Confirm these are truly machine-local, otherwise commit a checked-in template (e.g. `configs/resources.example.json`) or document the expected schema/regeneration path.
- [x] **S3-M16** (medium/security; reviewed: patch tag pinned to 1.27.0; digest pinning needs a registry lookup) `Dockerfile:1` - Base images are not pinned and the two builder stages drift from the runtime stage: the builders use the floating tag `golang:1.27-alpine3.24` (Go patch + Alpine 3.24 patch both move upstream) while the runtime uses `alpine:3.24.2`. This makes builds non-reproducible and exposes the pipeline to silent upstream tag mutation/supply-chain substitution; the builder's Alpine patch level can also differ from the runtime's. Pin every `FROM` by digest (`@sha256:...`) and keep a single Alpine minor/patch across stages (as already done for `postgres:16.15-alpine3.24` in docker-compose.yml).
- [x] **S3-M17** (medium/bug) `Dockerfile:24` - The image exposes 8787 but never sets `HACKATHONKIT_DEMO_HOST`; `cmd/demo-api/main.go` defaults the bind host to `127.0.0.1`, so `docker run -p 8787:8787 hackathonkit-demo-api` is unreachable from the host, while the HEALTHCHECK below still reports healthy because it probes loopback too (false-positive health signal that hides the misconfiguration). docker-compose sets `HACKATHONKIT_DEMO_HOST: 0.0.0.0` explicitly, which masks the problem only for compose users. Set the bind host in the image so the declared port is actually served.
- [x] **S3-M18** (medium/security; reviewed: the quality stage runs as root on purpose to write bind-mounted evidence) `Dockerfile:34` - The `quality` stage has no `USER` directive, so it runs as root while executing `scripts/quality-gate.sh` from a host-controlled bind mount (`./:/workspace`). Arbitrary content in the mounted repo is therefore executed with full in-container root privileges and can create root-owned files in the working tree (e.g. `evals/quality/...`), which then break later host-side operations. Note docker-compose.yml also mounts the Go build cache at `/root/.cache/go-build`, which only works because of this. Add a dedicated non-root user (and update the compose cache paths accordingly), or explicitly document this as an accepted risk in the stage comment.
- [x] **S3-M19** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:101` - When a session disappears from the poll response its row is removed, but `selected` is never cleared. Subsequent polls keep calling `loadDetail(selected)` against a session that no longer exists, repeatedly erroring/leaving a stale detail pane and a ghost selection. Clear `selected` (and the detail pane) when the selected id is no longer in `seen`.
- [x] **S3-M20** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:109` - `loadSessions` guards concurrent polls via `sessionsLoading`, but `loadDetail` is fired here without any guard. A poll tick and a user click (or two rapid clicks) can issue overlapping detail requests whose responses may resolve out of order, overwriting the pane with the wrong session. Track an in-flight request id/sequence (or AbortController) and only render the response whose id still matches the latest requested `selected`.
- [x] **S3-M21** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:119` - `loadDetail` has no try/catch and is invoked as a floating promise (see `if (selected) loadDetail(selected);`). A network failure, a non-JSON error body (`res.json()` throws), or a missing `data.session` on a `res.ok` response will produce an unhandled promise rejection and leave the detail pane showing stale data with no user feedback. Wrap the body in try/catch and surface errors into the detail/status pane; also guard `data.session` before dereferencing `data.session.status`.
- [x] **S3-M22** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:138` - `setInterval` is never cleared and the `if (sessionsLoading) return;` guard means one hung request permanently stops all future polling while the banner stays stuck; the timer also keeps firing on hidden tabs. Clear/restart the interval on unload, or add a timeout/abort to in-flight fetches so a stuck request cannot wedge the poll loop, and pause polling when `document.hidden`.
- [x] **S3-M23** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:138` - `/fly-model-list` is consumed without an `r.ok` check: a 404/500 HTML response rejects in `.json()` and is reported as `model assets unavailable`, hiding the actual status. Validate the response before trusting `list.files`.
- [x] **S3-M24** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:148` - `encodeURI` leaves `/`, `#` and `?` unescaped, so a file name containing `#` truncates the URL (the loader then silently no-ops) and `..` / path separators from the server-supplied list are passed through verbatim, letting the browser resolve outside `/fly-model/`. Encode each path segment and validate/skip suspicious entries; also note the error callback `resolve()`s unconditionally, so missing assets are reported as success (the `model-note` still claims the STL mesh was loaded).
- [x] **S3-M25** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:217` - The `polling` re-entrancy guard has no escape hatch: if a request never settles (no AbortController/timeout), `polling` stays `true` forever and every subsequent tick returns immediately, permanently freezing the visualization with no user-visible error. Add an AbortController with a timeout and reset `polling` in a `finally` that is guaranteed to run.
- [x] **S3-M26** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:221` - None of the three fetches validate `r.ok` or the JSON shape before calling `r.json()`. A 500/HTML error body makes `r.json()` reject, which is swallowed by the outer catch into the generic `offline: ...` status — masking a real server failure and making a partially-broken endpoint look like a transient network problem. Check `r.ok` (and that the payload is an object with an `events` array) and report per-endpoint failures.
- [x] **S3-M27** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:232` - Fallback dedupe key is lossy. When `fb.ts` is missing, `task + '|' + source` repeats for every identical task/source pair, so a second (genuinely different) reward is dropped; conversely a record without ts/task/source yields `undefined|undefined` and freezes dedupe forever. Include a monotonically increasing identifier (e.g. trace_id or the array index/last element ts) in the key.
- [x] **S3-M28** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:234` - `active_kc: lastNeuron?.active_kc || []` keeps the KC highlight tied to an unrelated record (see comment above); after the fix it should read from the newest recommend record. As written the reward/punish KC coloring frequently receives an empty array, so no KC is ever highlighted for the event being rendered.
- [x] **S3-M29** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:188` - Feedback is bound to the displayed event by `task` text only. Both `logs/fly-events.jsonl` and `logs/fly-feedback.jsonl` carry `trace_id` (see `internal/tools/system.go`), and `task` is only a 200-char snippet that repeats across runs — so re-running the same prompt binds a *previous* trace's decision (verdict, PAM/PPL1, added/dropped sets) to the newest event. This is the residual of backlog item M28, which asked for `trace_id` matching with a `task` fallback. Prefer matching the newest feedback whose `trace_id` equals `ev.trace_id`, falling back to `task` only when the ids are unavailable.
- [ ] **S3-M30** (medium/bug; reviewed: decide() sends the revision rendered at click time) `cmd/demo-api/static/plan.html:108` - `current` is replaced on every 5s poll (`current = data.plan;` in `load()`), but `decide()` submits whatever `current` holds at click time. A user can open the page on revision N, have the poll swap in revision N+1 (or even a different slug), and then approve a plan version they never saw. Snapshot the reviewed slug/revision when rendering and send that snapshot, or detect a revision change and force a reload before allowing a decision.
- [x] **S3-M31** (medium/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/plan.html:123` - The success path dereferences `data.approval.decision`/`data.approval.revision` without validating the payload shape, while the error path does guard with `data.error`. A 2xx response with an unexpected body (e.g. `{}`) throws a TypeError that the outer catch reports as "request failed", falsely signalling a failure for a decision that was actually recorded. Guard the response body like the error branch does.
- [x] **S3-M32** (medium/bug) `cmd/fly-bench/main.go:138` - The determinism/agreement metrics conflate "no recommendations" with "not deterministic"/"disagreement", so the reported rates do not mean what the summary claims. - `Deterministic` requires `len(flyRes.Recommendations) > 0`, so any task where the full router returns nothing (the exact silent-failure case above) is counted as non-deterministic even though two empty results are trivially identical. The same applies to `Agreement`, which requires both sides non-empty. - The probe `again := full.Recommend(...)` runs immediately after the first call and shares the router's one-entry embedding cache (`embedCacheTask`), so the embedding path is memoized rather than re-executed; the report's `Determinism: "warm (caches primed before the loop)"` string therefore overstates what was actually re-run. Consider recording `FlyTop1Again` in `rowResult`, treating two empty results as deterministic, and documenting that the probe measures LLM/reranker variance only.
- [x] **S3-M33** (medium/bug) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:173` - `scanner.Err()` is never checked after the loop. A mid-file read error (or a line exceeding the 4 MB buffer, which yields `bufio.ErrTooLong`) silently ends iteration and the function still returns nil, reporting success with only partially ingested feedback — the rebuilt memory/dopamine state is then incomplete with no indication to the user.
- [x] **S3-M34** (medium/bug) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:180` - `runEval` does not guard against an empty eval set. `readDatasetWithWarnings` returns an empty slice with a nil error when every eval line is blank/malformed/missing fields, so the main path happily calls this function and the metrics divide by `n == 0`, printing `top-1 NaN%, top-3 NaN%` instead of failing or clearly reporting an empty evaluation.
- [x] **S3-M35** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:261` - `store.Session` returns `sql.ErrNoRows` for a missing session, but it also returns genuine failures (DB errors, `session <id> has malformed risk reasons`, context cancellation). Mapping every error to 404 "session not found" masks real internal/database failures as not-found responses, and discards the cause. Distinguish the not-found sentinel from other errors (import `database/sql` / `errors`).
- [x] **S3-M36** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:240` - The retry is unconditional and the original error is discarded. Any failure of `worktree add -b <branch> <dir>` (permission error, dirty index, disk error, etc. — not just an already-existing branch) triggers a second `worktree add <dir> <branch>`, and only the retry's error is returned. When the branch genuinely does not exist, the reported error comes from the fallback and can be misleading. Only retry when the failure is actually due to an existing branch, and preserve the original error otherwise (`fmt.Errorf("worktree add -b: %w; retry: %v", firstErr, retryErr)`).
- [ ] **S3-M37** (medium/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:259` - `runGit` writes directly to the global `os.Stdout`/`os.Stderr`, ignoring the `io.Writer`s that `run()` carefully threads through for testability. The same bypass exists in `cmdWorktree` (`fmt.Println(dir)`) and `cmdRun` (`fmt.Printf(...)`, `cmd.Stdin/Stdout/Stderr = os.Stdin/Stdout/Stderr`). Any subcommand output or subprocess error therefore escapes the caller's writers, defeating the injected-writer contract and making these paths untestable/uncapturable. Pass the writers into these helpers instead of using os.Stdout/os.Stderr directly.
- [x] **S3-M38** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:358` - Session bookkeeping failures are silently downgraded: `AppendEvent`/`EndSession` errors only print a warning and flip the local `status` string, but the function still returns the original `runErr` (nil when codex succeeded). If the codex run succeeds while the output event or session-end timestamp fails to persist, the process exits 0 even though the session record is incomplete and `RecomputeRisk` runs on inconsistent data. Consider surfacing a non-nil error (or a distinct non-zero exit) when bookkeeping fails, or documenting that it is deliberately best-effort.
- [x] **S3-M39** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:81` - `loadedMsg.err` conflates failures of `store.Sessions` and `store.Events` (see loadCmd). When only the Events query fails, `msg.sessions`/`msg.selected` are still valid, but this early return drops them and `View` returns immediately on `m.err`, hiding the freshly loaded session list and leaving the pane stale until the next successful load. Consider carrying a separate `eventsErr` so the session list renders with a degraded right pane (and only treat a Sessions error as fatal).
- [x] **S3-M40** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:45` - The offset is computed on the lowercased copy but applied to the original string. `strings.ToLower` is not byte-length preserving for all Unicode input (e.g. 'İ' U+0130 lowers to a 3-byte sequence), so `idx` can point past the intended marker in `text` and the subsequent `text[:idx+len(marker)]` / `text[end:]` slicing can cut mid-rune or panic with a slice-bounds error. Match case-insensitively on the original bytes instead (e.g. a precompiled `regexp.MustCompile("(?i)(password|passwd|secret|token)=[^\\s&;\\n]*")` with `ReplaceAllString(err.Error(), "${1}=[redacted]")`), which also removes the termination bug above.
- [x] **S3-M41** (medium/security) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:194` - The MinIO failure is logged with the raw `err.Error()`, while the Postgres path right above is passed through `redactBackendError`. The helper's own doc comment states MinIO errors can carry DSNs/credentials, so this path can write secrets to the log stream. Wrap it the same way (`redactBackendError(err)`) for consistency.
- [x] **S3-M42** (medium/security) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:202` - Same leak class as above: storage reports object-store errors through this callback (`store.report(s.objects.Put(...))` in internal/storage/store.go), i.e. MinIO errors whose text the helper's comment says may embed DSN/credentials, yet the message is logged unredacted. Route it through `redactBackendError(err)`.
- [x] **S3-M43** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:363` - Returning nil on `err != nil` swallows the walk error, so `filepath.WalkDir` returns nil and the outer `if err := ...; err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err)` warning never fires. An unreadable/nonexistent-with-permission-error decisions directory therefore reports zero pending decisions with no diagnostic — the opposite of the comment below and of the documented guarantee. Propagate the error (the outer `os.IsNotExist` check already suppresses the expected "directory not created yet" case).
- [x] **S3-M44** (medium/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:342` - The skip guard only fires for messages that *carry* an id different from the in-flight request. A message with no `id` field (`len(probe.ID) == 0`) or an explicit `"id":null` (which `Response.MarshalJSON` emits when a frame has no id, e.g. parse-error frames) falls through and is parsed/returned as the response for our request: `resp.Result` is nil, so `call()` fails with a misleading `invalid tool result: unexpected end of JSON input` instead of the notification being ignored. Skip anything that is not exactly our reply. Note `json.RawMessage` of a number is the raw bytes, so the `strconv.Itoa` comparison stays correct.
- [ ] **S3-M45** (medium/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:716` - The accumulated `ok` is discarded here, so the whole accumulator is dead code. Worse, the pattern `ok = r.step(...) && ok` does not short-circuit: Go evaluates the left operand first, so `r.step` runs unconditionally and a failure never prevents the following steps from executing. Either gate dependent steps explicitly or drop the accumulator entirely to avoid implying sequencing that does not exist.
- [x] **S3-M46** (medium/bug; reviewed: variant ordering is a tuned heuristic; changing it needs an A/B run) `configs/resource-matrix.yaml:15` - Variant ordering still defeats the documented "provider-backed variants when ready" contract for `tabular` (same pattern in `timeseries`, `classification` and `retrieval`). The provider-less `deterministic` entry is listed first, and `resources.Plan` (`internal/resources/matrix.go:108-114`) selects the first variant whose provider is empty or ready, so the `openai` variant is never chosen even when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is ready and the capability is never reported as degraded (the `missing[]` upgrade hint is therefore lost). This contradicts this file's own header ("variants without a provider ... act as deterministic fallbacks"), the `routing.variant` tool description, and README lines 224/235. It also breaks the implicit fallback invariant in `Plan` (`variants[len(variants)-1]` is the fallback when nothing is ready): for these four capabilities the last entry is provider-backed. Either list the provider variant first and `deterministic` last (as `reasoning`/`embeddings`/`asr`/`vision`/`tts`/`notifications`/`metadata`/`artifacts` already do) or explicitly document the deterministic-first choice, and in either case update `resources.DefaultMatrix()` and `internal/resources/matrix_test.go` in lockstep since the regression test compares them with `reflect.DeepEqual`.
- [x] **S3-M47** (medium/bug) `docker-compose.yml:27` - Client and server MinIO credentials are decoupled. The MCP client reads `MINIO_ACCESS_KEY` (overridable via `.env`), but the `minio` service hardcodes `MINIO_ROOT_USER: hackathonkit` (line 107). Since `scripts/setup-resources.sh` and the README both direct users to set `MINIO_ACCESS_KEY`, anyone overriding it in `.env` will authenticate with a key the server does not know → `SignatureDoesNotMatch` and silent artifact-mirroring failure. The secret side has the same asymmetry: the client uses `${MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD}` while the tooling writes `MINIO_SECRET_KEY`, so a `.env` containing only `MINIO_SECRET_KEY` makes compose abort with a misleading "set MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD" error. Drive both sides from one variable pair (e.g. `${MINIO_ROOT_USER:-hackathonkit}` / `${MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD}`).
- [x] **S3-M48** (medium/bug) `internal/atomicfile/atomicfile.go:33` - `filepath.Base(path)` is interpolated unescaped into a glob pattern, so a base name containing glob metacharacters changes the match set. With `*`/`?` the sweep can match and delete unrelated `*.tmp` files older than an hour (e.g. base `foo.json` also matches a stale temp of `foo.json.bak`); with `[`/`]` the pattern silently stops matching its own temp files (or returns `ErrBadPattern`, which is swallowed), so leftover temps accumulate. The package contract does not require callers to avoid metacharacters, and the utility is shared. Prefer a plain directory scan with literal prefix/suffix checks (add `strings` to the imports).
- [x] **S3-M49** (medium/performance) `internal/config/config.go:372` - The shared `m.mu` is held across blocking file I/O here (`os.ReadFile` and `atomicfile.Write` below), whereas `Routing()` and `Evaluation()` were deliberately changed to read the file *before* taking the lock so a slow/bind-mounted filesystem cannot serialize every config access. Since the same mutex guards `Config()`, `Routing()` and `Evaluation()` on request paths, a stalled write serializes all config reads. Read and parse the existing file before locking, and keep the lock only for the version bump + write.
- [x] **S3-M50** (medium/bug) `internal/config/config.go:377` - The `os.ReadFile` error is swallowed (`err` is only used as a success guard, never logged), so a transient read failure or an unparsable `routing.yaml` silently falls back to `current := RoutingState{Mode: "normal", Version: 1}`. The next write then persists `Version: current.Version + 1` (= 2) over a file that may already be at version N, regressing the version that `internal/tools/system.go` (`routing_config_version`) and `metadata.RoutingRun` expose to callers. Log the failure and return an error instead of clobbering the stored version when the file exists but cannot be read/parsed.
- [x] **S3-M51** (medium/bug) `internal/config/dotenv.go:74` - Residual of the M58 escape fix: the double-quoted branch recognizes only `\n`, `\t`, `\r` and silently drops the backslash for every other `\X` sequence (`default: b.WriteRune(r)`). A legitimate quoted value such as `KEY="C:\path\to\dir"` is loaded as `C:pathtodir`, and `KEY="a\qb"` becomes `aqb`, i.e. configuration/secrets are corrupted silently. This is also inconsistent with the unquoted and single-quoted branches of this same function, which preserve backslashes, and with mainstream dotenv parsers (JS dotenv, shell `"..."`) that only treat `\n`,`\t`,`\r`,`\\`,`\"` (optionally `\$`) as escapes and keep the backslash otherwise. Suggest preserving the backslash in the unknown-escape case.
- [x] **S3-M52** (medium/bug) `internal/control/handoff.go:41` - Degradation warnings are collected into `handoff.Notes` but never surfaced: `RenderHandoff` does not render `h.Notes` (and the only caller, `sessions resume`, prints just `RenderHandoff(handoff)` — cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:179). So when the learning store or decision history is unreadable, the paste-ready block looks complete and the operator has no signal that the handoff is degraded. Either render the notes in the block (e.g. a `- warnings:` section) or return/print them.
- [x] **S3-M53** (medium/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:89` - Rotation is detected solely by comparing the stored offset with the current file size. If the log is truncated/recreated and then regrows past the previous offset before Ingest runs (a realistic window for an unbounded, frequently-written log), `offset > info.Size()` is false, so Seek(offset) skips the leading records of the new file and they are silently lost forever. Consider also tracking inode/creation time or a head fingerprint (e.g. first N bytes / modtime) to distinguish append-only growth from replacement.
- [x] **S3-M54** (medium/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:128` - The cursor write on the error path is explicitly discarded with `_ =`, and the function returns `false` for `rotated` even when rotation was detected. If SetCursor fails here, all records handled before the failing line are replayed on the next cycle (duplicates) and the caller has no indication; the rotation flag is also silently lost, so `IngestResult.Rotated` will under-report. Propagate the SetCursor error and preserve the rotated value.
- [x] **S3-M55** (medium/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:136` - Cursor persistence is not atomic with event insertion. All records in the chunk are appended first, and the cursor is written only once at the end. If this final SetCursor fails (or the process crashes between appends and the write), the next Ingest starts from the stale offset and re-appends every record in the chunk, directly contradicting the doc comment's promise that "repeated runs never duplicate records". Consider persisting the cursor in the same transaction as the event append, or advancing it per line/batch so the exposure window is bounded.
- [x] **S3-M56** (medium/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:227` - The modtime-based rewrite detection resets `offset = 0` and then appends *another* `eval_decision` event for the same file. A decision source is described above as "immutable"; a touch or in-place rewrite therefore produces duplicate (and potentially conflicting) events rather than replacing the prior one. If rewrites are expected, deduplicate on a stable identity (content hash or file identity) or upsert the event keyed by the decision file instead of blind-appending.
- [x] **S3-M57** (medium/bug) `internal/control/risk.go:82` - The panic heuristic only scrubs the exact literal "no panic", so negated prose such as "no errors or panic"/"no panic or rollback" still matches `panicWord` and is counted as a panic event, inflating the score. It also scans the whole marshaled payload (including keys/tool names/arguments), so unrelated strings like `panic_free` or a tool named `panic-recover` produce false positives, while unusual spacing/key names can produce false negatives. Prefer matching a structured panic indicator (e.g. a dedicated payload field/status) rather than free-text substring matching.
- [x] **S3-M58** (medium/performance) `internal/control/risk.go:104` - `latestGlobalTS` runs inside the per-session loop even though its result is independent of the session. That issues N identical `SELECT MAX(ts) ... WHERE session_id=''` queries (a full scan without a session filter) for N sessions. Compute it once before the loop and reuse the value.
- [x] **S3-M59** (medium/bug) `internal/control/risk.go:108` - The window end is first clamped to `sess.EndedAt` for ended sessions, but this block immediately overrides it with the newest global event timestamp. As a result, global MCP events that occurred *after* the session ended are attributed to it (and every ended session ends up sharing the same `end` = latest global ts), inflating/deforming the risk score. `latestGlobalTS` depends only on global state, so it should not influence a per-session window either.
- [x] **S3-M60** (medium/bug) `internal/control/store.go` - The `ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE` branch is inconsistent with the INSERT branch and can corrupt session state. Two problems: 1) `status=excluded.status` sets the status unconditionally and `ended_at=excluded.ended_at` blindly overwrites the terminal timestamp, so any caller that re-upserts an existing row with an active `Session` (e.g. a replayed `session_start`/heartbeat, or a colliding `s-<ts>-<pid>` id) fully resurrects an ended session (`status=active`, `ended_at=NULL`). This contradicts the no-resurrect invariant documented on `StartSession`, whose own `UPDATE`→`COUNT`→`Upsert` guard is a non-atomic check-then-act that a concurrent writer (the daemon and CLI open the same `control.DefaultPath` DB) can interleave, so the invariant is not actually guaranteed. 2) The branch never writes `risk_score`/`risk_reasons`, while the INSERT branch does. A caller that upserts an already-existing row with a scored `Session` silently loses the new risk values and must know to call `UpdateRisk` separately. Guard terminal state/monotonic timestamps and propagate the risk columns.
- [x] **S3-M61** (medium/security) `internal/evaluation/bundle.go:137` - The guard before this destructive `os.RemoveAll(outAbs)` only checks that the target directory contains a `bundle.json`, and the check/target are separate operations (TOCTOU). Pointing `OutDir` at any directory that happens to contain a `bundle.json` (or swapping the path between the `ReadDir`/`Stat` check and `RemoveAll`) causes the entire tree to be recursively deleted. The `os.ReadDir` error is also discarded, so an unreadable directory silently skips the guard. Consider tying deletion to a stronger ownership marker (e.g. a tool-specific marker file that also records the target dir), re-stat'ing immediately before removal, or refusing when the directory holds entries that are not part of a prior bundle's manifest.
- [x] **S3-M62** (medium/bug) `internal/evaluation/bundle.go:286` - `isExcluded` discards the error from `path.Match`. A malformed glob pattern (e.g. an unclosed `[`) returns `ErrBadPattern` with `ok == false`, so the rule silently matches nothing and files it was meant to exclude (secrets such as `.env*`, runtime state, generated mirrors) can leak into the bundle instead of failing loudly. Since exclude patterns come from user-supplied `opts.Exclude`, validate them once at compile time and propagate an error, or at minimum log/return failure when `path.Match` reports a bad pattern.
- [x] **S3-M63** (medium/bug) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:430` - The same-snapshot requirement is skipped whenever either run has an empty `SnapshotSHA`, and the returned calibration always reports `run1.SnapshotSHA`. A run that never recorded a sha can therefore be calibrated against a run over a different snapshot, producing a meaningless noise floor. Require the shas to be identical (both empty or both equal) instead of skipping the check.
- [x] **S3-M64** (medium/bug) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:523` - `CompareInput.NoiseFloor` (and `Target`) are used unchecked. If `NoiseFloor` is NaN, every comparison against it is false, so in judge mode `regression > in.NoiseFloor+epsilon` never triggers and a large regression falls through to `DecisionAccept`. A negative floor similarly disables the regression guard, and `in.Target > 0` silently disables target detection when the caller legitimately passes a 0 target. Validate the inputs (finite, non-negative floor; explicit "target set" flag) before deciding.
- [x] **S3-M65** (medium/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:143` - The embedding error is discarded (`err == nil` is the only condition checked), so there is no way for a caller to learn that semantic recall is degraded for this entry. A transient or persistent embedder outage (bad credentials, network failure) results in a learning being persisted permanently without a vector, silently degrading all future `Recall` calls to keyword matching. Either propagate the error or at least surface/log it so the degradation is observable.
- [x] **S3-M66** (medium/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:392` - The lock file is created with `O_EXCL` and only removed on the normal unlock path, with no staleness detection. If the owning process is killed (or the machine reboots) between create and remove, the `.lock` file stays behind and *every* subsequent `Record` fails after the ~1s (50 x 20ms) retry loop with "learning store is locked by another process" — permanently, until an operator deletes the file by hand. Note the inconsistency this creates: `Promote` (which does not take the file lock) keeps writing while `Record` is wedged. Consider recording the owner pid/creation time and breaking a stale lock, or using an advisory OS lock (`syscall.Flock`/`LockFileEx`) that the kernel releases on process exit.
- [x] **S3-M67** (medium/bug) `internal/mcp/contracts.go:44` - `WithWarnings` appends directly to the receiver's slice, unlike `WithSources`/`WithMetrics` which defensively copy first (the comment in `WithSources` explicitly notes that appending to a shared backing array would mutate the caller). Because `Envelope` is passed by value, `e.Warnings` still shares the backing array with the caller's slice; if that slice has spare capacity (`cap > len`), the append writes into the shared array and can silently clobber elements observed through another `Envelope`/slice that aliases the same storage. Make it consistent with the other `With*` methods by copying before appending.
- [x] **S3-M68** (medium/bug) `internal/mcp/registry.go:63` - `cloneMap`/`cloneSlice` only recurse into `map[string]any` and `[]any`; every other concrete type is copied by reference. Tool schemas in this repo do contain concrete composite types — `deps.go` builds `schema["required"] = required` with a `[]string` (e.g. `[]string{"path"}`). Because the `[]string` backing array is shared, the `cloneTool` guarantee ("callers cannot mutate registry state") does not hold: a caller can do `tool.InputSchema["required"].([]string)[0] = "x"` on the value returned by `Get`/`List` and silently corrupt the schema stored in the registry for all subsequent requests (and vice versa, the caller's original slice is aliased into registry state at `Register` time). The same gap exists in `cloneSlice`. Add explicit handling for the concrete types actually used (e.g. `[]string`, `map[string]string`) or switch to a reflective deep copy.
- [x] **S3-M69** (medium/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:67` - Residual of M74: this reader goroutine blocks inside `scanner.Scan()`/`reader.Read` and only observes cancellation when it next sends to `lines`. When `ServeStreams` returns early (an `enc.Encode` failure, an output write error, or parent-ctx cancellation) the goroutine stays parked on `in` until the reader produces data or is closed, retaining the reader and a scanner buffer that can grow to 32 MB. That is harmless for `Serve()`/`os.Stdin` (the process exits) but leaks per call for the exported `ServeStreams` used with a long-lived pipe/socket. Unblock it on the return paths — close `in` when it implements `io.Closer`, or document that the caller owns closing `in`.
- [x] **S3-M70** (medium/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:97` - Oversize-frame recovery turns real read errors into a successful shutdown. In this discard loop any error from `ReadSlice` that is not `ErrBufferFull` — including a genuine I/O failure (EIO, broken pipe, reset connection) — is reported as `scanErr <- nil`, so `ServeStreams` returns `nil` and the underlying read failure is silently swallowed (the L52 fix is present, but this new path leaks the error contract). Only `io.EOF` is a legitimate clean terminator here; every other error should be propagated.
- [x] **S3-M71** (medium/bug) `internal/metadata/metadata.go:225` - `Close()` writes `p.pool = nil` without synchronization, while `Enabled()` (line 164) and all `Record*` methods read `p.pool`. This is a data race whenever shutdown overlaps in-flight recording — `cmd/demo-api/main.go:68` calls `meta.Close()` from the `ListenAndServe` error path while request goroutines can still be executing `meta.Enabled()` / `meta.RecordRoutingRun` (and `cmd/demo-api/main.go:47` defers a second `Close()`). A torn interface read (type word vs. data word) can even yield a non-nil interface with a nil data pointer, turning the intended no-op into a panic inside `Exec` instead of a clean error. The fix for M75 introduced this write, so please make `pool` access synchronized (mutex or atomic) and have `Enabled`/`Record*` read the field under the same lock.
- [x] **S3-M72** (medium/security) `internal/objectstore/minio.go:33` - TLS is enabled only for the exact literal "true"; any other non-empty spelling ("TRUE", "1", "yes", "on") silently leaves `Secure` false, so the static access/secret keys are sent to the endpoint in cleartext with no warning. Strict parsing itself is intentional (asserted by `TestSettingsFromEnvUseSSLOnlyTrue`), but a non-empty, unrecognized value should fail fast (or at least log a warning) instead of silently downgrading to plaintext. Consider parsing with `strconv.ParseBool` and surfacing a configuration error for unparseable values.
- [x] **S3-M73** (medium/bug) `internal/objectstore/minio.go:72` - `Put` accepts arbitrarily large payloads, while `Get` rejects anything above `maxObjectBytes` (64 MiB). Because `storage.Store.ReadArtifact` falls back to `Get` only when the local file is missing, an object larger than the cap can be written successfully, yet becomes permanently unrehydratable (the read path returns "remote fetch failed" instead of the data). Enforce the same limit on write, or make the limit shared/configurable so write and read agree.
- [x] **S3-M74** (medium/bug) `internal/observability/obs.go:97` - The recovery path writes directly to the underlying writer, bypassing `log.Logger`'s internal mutex (`Output` holds `l.mu` around its write). `log.Logger` is documented safe for concurrent use, and this file's callers log from request handlers (e.g. `internal/mcp/server.go` `tool_call`/`tool_panic`), so a concurrent `Output` can be interleaved with this raw `"\n"`, splitting/corrupting another JSON record. The write error is also discarded. Prefer emitting the newline through the logger itself (which serializes writes) and/or a logger-owned mutex rather than reaching into `l.Writer()`.
- [x] **S3-M75** (medium/bug) `internal/observability/obs.go:127` - Silent over-limit downgrade: any `limit > 1000` is replaced with 50, i.e. a caller asking for 1001+ gets *fewer* records than one asking for 1000. This is reachable from user input (`cmd/demo-api/main.go` `queryLimit(r, ...)` passes the raw `?limit=` value straight into `TailJSONL`), so `?limit=2000` silently returns 50 entries with no error, truncating results in a way callers cannot detect. Clamp to the actual maximum instead of resetting to the default.
- [x] **S3-M76** (medium/bug) `internal/openai/client.go:79` - `io.LimitReader` caps the body at 8MB but the truncation is undetectable afterwards: `io.ReadAll` returns a clean `nil` error at the limit, so `json.Unmarshal` fails with the misleading `unexpected end of JSON input`. This is realistically reachable — callers such as `internal/routing/fly/embed.go` pass an unbounded exemplar slice to `Embed`, and a 100+ vector response at 3072 dims already exceeds 8MB — and the caller cannot tell a size overrun from a malformed provider response. Read one extra byte and report an explicit size error.
- [x] **S3-M77** (medium/bug) `internal/plans/plans.go:173` - `Latest` silently discards every read and JSON-parse error (`continue`) and keeps scanning, so when the newest plan file is corrupt or unreadable the caller gets a *stale* older plan (or `ok=false`) with no signal that the intended newest plan failed. This is reachable from user-visible paths: the `plan.status` default branch (`internal/tools/plan.go:197`), the session-context hook (`internal/control/handoff.go:34`), the CLI (`cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:297`) and demo-api (`cmd/demo-api/main.go:195,227`) all use `Latest`. The package already added `LatestErr` for exactly this, but it has no callers anywhere in the repo, so the error surfacing is not actually wired up (docs/SESSION-HANDOFF.md still lists L59 `plans.Latest error surfacing` as open). Either delegate and surface the error, or migrate the callers to `LatestErr`.
- [x] **S3-M78** (medium/bug) `internal/quality/proc_unix.go:1` - The `!windows` build constraint is broader than the code it guards. `syscall.SysProcAttr` on plan9, js/wasm and wasip1 is an empty struct (no `Setpgid` field), so `cmd.SysProcAttr = &syscall.SysProcAttr{Setpgid: true}` fails to compile on those targets. Since the helper is inherently POSIX (process groups + negative-PID kill), use the standard `unix` build tag instead of `!windows` (or add a per-platform file).
- [x] **S3-M79** (medium/bug) `internal/quality/quality.go:231` - This pre-filter compares the *directory* mtime against `bestTime`, but `bestTime` is later assigned the *gate.json* mtime. The two are different clocks: `atomicfile.Write` renames a temp file into place, so a normally written gate.json has file mtime <= dir mtime, but any in-place update (external tooling, manual edit, `touch`, a restored backup) can make gate.json newer than its directory. In that case, if a newer directory was visited first, this stale dir-mtime test skips the directory holding the actually newest evidence and `LatestReport` returns outdated evidence as the current gate result. Compare like with like — drop the directory-level pre-filter and rely on the gate.json mtime check below.
- [x] **S3-M80** (medium/security) `internal/quality/quality.go:281` - The ref read from `.git/HEAD` is joined into a filesystem path with no validation. A crafted HEAD such as `ref: ../../../../etc/passwd` escapes `.git` and makes this read an arbitrary file, whose contents are returned as the SHA. That value flows into `Report.SHA` and is persisted to `evals/quality/<sha>/gate.json` (the `unknown` fallback only affects the path, not the field), leaking file contents into stored evidence when the gate runs over an untrusted repository. Constrain the ref to the `refs/` namespace and reject `..` components (also note a worktree-style `.git` *file* is not handled at all here, yielding "unknown").
- [x] **S3-M81** (medium/bug) `internal/resources/matrix.go:115` - The degraded fallback blindly takes the *last* variant. Nothing enforces that the last variant is provider-less: `Validate` (lines 72-87) only checks track/capability names, and the tracked `configs/resource-matrix.yaml` is user-editable. For a matrix whose terminal variant is provider-backed (e.g. `reasoning: [openai, nvidia]`), this picks an unavailable provider as `Chosen` and marks it `Degraded` even though an earlier provider-less deterministic variant would have been usable. The result is a plan that names an unusable provider as the fallback, and callers present it as such (`internal/tools/resources.go` reports these as "capabilities on deterministic fallbacks"), so the mislabel is user-visible. Suggest either enforcing in `Validate` that every capability used by a track contains a provider-less variant, or selecting the last provider-less variant here and only falling through to an unavailable provider when none exists.
- [x] **S3-M82** (medium/bug) `internal/resources/probe.go:57` - Residual M90 (marked fixed in docs/REVIEW-BACKLOG.md, but the ignored error is still here). The `io.ReadAll` error is discarded and the reader is capped at 1 MiB; on a mid-read failure (or a body larger than 1 MiB) the JSON parse silently fails, `Models` stays 0 and the function still returns `Reachable: true` with an empty `Error`. Capture the read failure so a truncated/aborted response is not reported as a healthy probe with zero models.
- [x] **S3-M83** (medium/bug) `internal/resources/resources.go:82` - `copy` only duplicates the outer `[]Def`; each copied `Def` still shares the package-level `EnvKeys` backing array. A caller that writes `Defs()[i].EnvKeys[j]` (or hands it to a helper that normalizes/redacts keys) silently corrupts `defs` for every later `Defs`/`Statuses` call, and races with concurrent readers. Note `Statuses` already deep-copies via `append([]string{}, d.EnvKeys...)`, so this is inconsistent — the M91 fix recorded in the backlog does not appear to be present here. Deep-copy `EnvKeys`.
- [x] **S3-M84** (medium/bug) `internal/resources/resources.go:109` - The documented "leading number" shorthand does not work for unpadded refs: `number` is "1"/"9" while `t.ID[:2]` is "01"/"09", so `LookupTrack("1")` fails and `Save` then returns `unknown track "1"` for a value the CLI/MCP accepts. Tests only exercise zero-padded forms ("08", "10"), so this is uncovered. Normalize the numeric part before comparing. Also note the `strings.Contains(label, ref)` fallback is first-match-wins, so ambiguous refs (e.g. "assistant", which appears in tracks 02/03/05) resolve arbitrarily to the earliest track.
- [x] **S3-M85** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/embed.go:239` - The disk cache is accepted on hash+model-tag alone, but it carries no record of the vector dimension. In production the embedder is a `ChainEmbedder{Primary: openai, Fallback: HashEncoder(512)}` with one shared tag (`"<model>+hash512"`), so if the first request ever falls back to the hash encoder, `buildCentroids` persists 512-dim centroids under the primary's tag. Once the primary recovers, the task vector is 1536-dim, every centroid is skipped in `embeddingCategoryScores` (len(sims)==0 -> nil,false) and — because this cache hit is never invalidated — the embedding signal stays silently disabled for the whole process lifetime (only an exemplar/model-tag change or manual cache deletion recovers it). Please persist the dimension in `embeddingCache` and reject/rebuild a cache whose dims cannot match the current vectors, or, when all centroids are skipped for dimension mismatch, invalidate `r.centroids` and force a rebuild.
- [x] **S3-M86** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/hash_encoder.go:24` - `HashEncoder` is exported with a mutable exported `Dims` field, so callers outside the package can build the zero-value `HashEncoder{}` (or set a negative `Dims`) and bypass the constructor guard. `make([]float64, h.Dims)` then panics on a negative length, and `value % uint64(h.Dims)` divides by zero when `Dims == 0`. A library panic instead of a returned error is a real failure mode for this public API. Validate once before the loop and return an error (or normalize to the default) instead of relying solely on the constructor.
- [x] **S3-M87** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/hash_encoder.go:91` - The primary embedder's error is discarded. If the primary fails (transient provider outage, timeout) and `Fallback` is nil or also returns fewer vectors than requested, the caller only sees the generic `"no embedder available"`, which misdiagnoses an operational outage as a nil-configuration problem, and the original cause is lost for `errors.Is`/logging. Capture the primary error and preserve it with `%w` (e.g. `fmt.Errorf("primary embedder failed: %w", primaryErr)`), including in the fallback path.
- [x] **S3-M88** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:123` - saveBrain overwrites the brain file with a plain, non-atomic os.WriteFile. A crash, full disk, or an interleaved writer can leave a truncated/partial JSON file; loadBrain then fails json.Unmarshal and silently returns nil (`brain == nil`), so MB scoring silently disappears until a full retrain. memory.go already uses the temp-file + rename pattern for this reason — use it here too (write to a temp file in the same dir, fsync/close, then os.Rename).
- [x] **S3-M89** (medium/performance) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:263` - brainMu is process-global, and this deferred unlock holds it across brain.scores (O(KC log KC) sort + map writes) and, at the end of the function, r.saveBrain's MkdirAll/MarshalIndent/WriteFile. Consequently every other Router's mbCategoryScores / ActivationSnapshot / Dopamine must block behind a single disk write, which directly contradicts loadBrain's own comment that "a slow disk must not serialize every Router in the process" (Dopamine -> loadBrainLocked also does os.ReadFile under this same lock on the cold path). Apply the update under the lock, then release it and persist the snapshot outside the critical section.
- [x] **S3-M90** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:298` - The readout/bias/counters were already mutated in place before this call, so when saveBrain fails applyDopamine returns false while the in-memory brain stays updated: memory and disk diverge and a caller treating `false` as "nothing was learned" is misled (AddFeedback currently discards the bool entirely, so the failure is invisible). Note also that `r.brain = brain` is a no-op — loadBrain already returned the cached pointer, so no private copy is ever made. Either build the update on a copy and publish it only after a successful save, or roll back / propagate the error to the caller.
- [x] **S3-M91** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:327` - The `||` conflates the two maps: if only one is nil the other is discarded. A brain whose JSON has a populated "readout" but no "bias" key (Bias == nil) has all learned weights wiped on the first Train call. Split the nil checks so each map is initialized independently.
- [x] **S3-M92** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/memory.go:154` - `saveMemory` re-implements the temp-file+rename pattern by hand but never flushes the data: after `tmp.Write(data)` there is no `tmp.Sync()` before `os.Rename`, and the parent directory is never synced after the rename. On a crash/power loss the rename can be durable while the file contents are not, leaving an empty or truncated `fly-memory.json` behind a valid name — exactly the failure the package's own `atomicfile.Write` (internal/atomicfile/atomicfile.go, which does `tmp.Sync()` + dir sync) was written to prevent. This copy also skips the stale `*.tmp` reclamation that helper performs. Prefer reusing `atomicfile.Write` instead of duplicating the sequence.
- [x] **S3-M93** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/memory.go:185` - `mem.Model` is a file-level tag, but it is unconditionally overwritten with the current `r.modelTag` here. After a model change, existing examples whose `Vector` was produced by the old model are relabelled as the new model, so the cross-model guard in `memoryCategoryScores` (`mem.Model != r.modelTag`) can never fire again and stale vectors are scored against the new task vectors, producing meaningless similarities. Either tag the model per example, or drop examples that were embedded under a different model before rewriting the file tag.
- [x] **S3-M94** (medium/bug) `internal/routing/fly/router.go:191` - The "has signal" gate uses map presence (`len(scores) > 0`) instead of a positive score. `embeddingCategoryScores` inserts an entry for every centroid even when the similarity is zero/clamped to 0 (see the `len(sims) == 1` branch in embed.go), so an out-of-domain task that matched no keyword still leaves zero-valued entries in `scores`. That makes `keywordSignal || len(scores) > 0` true, `boost(scores, "reports", 0.15)` fires, and the task is routed to `reports` with non-empty recommendations instead of the documented `unknown`/empty result (see TestRecommendZeroSignalIsUnknown). Gate the reports prior (and the resulting route) on an actual positive score.
- [x] **S3-M95** (medium/bug) `internal/skills/validate.go:187` - The explicit-only enforcement is gated on the literal, case-sensitive substring `"Explicit-only:"` (with a colon). The catalog uses two variants: `judge`, `output-self-check`, `project-cost`, `qa-expert`, `skill-authoring` write `"Explicit-only: never invoke implicitly."`, but `cp-expert-refactoring`, `roast-project`, `self-improvement-controller` and `system-design-expert` write `"Explicit-only; never invoke implicitly."` (semicolon). For those four skills the check never fires, so removing `policy.allow_implicit_invocation: false` from their `agents/openai.yaml` would silently pass validation — exactly the policy regression this guard exists to catch. Accept both punctuation variants (a lowercase `"explicit-only"` match is not safe here: `task-router`'s description contains `"explicit-only skills such as $judge"` but is not an explicit-only skill).
- [x] **S3-M96** (medium/security) `internal/storage/store.go:164` - Check-then-use race (TOCTOU) around the containment guard: `ensureWithin` resolves `full` (and its ancestors) at line 193, but the write happens later via a separate `os.MkdirAll` + `os.WriteFile` on the same name. Any process with write access to the artifact/index tree (exactly the threat model of H25/H26) can swap a directory component for a symlink in that window, so `os.WriteFile` follows it and creates the file outside `s.root` even though the guard passed. The same pattern applies to `ArtifactPath`, `WriteIndex` and the rehydrate write in `ReadArtifact`. Since the check is name-based and the use is separate, containment cannot be guaranteed this way. Consider opening the final file with `O_CREATE|O_NOFOLLOW` (so a leaf symlink cannot redirect the write) plus re-resolving/verifying the parent immediately before the write, or resolving the whole path through an fd-relative openat/securejoin helper.
- [x] **S3-M97** (medium/bug) `internal/telegram/telegram.go:235` - `parse_mode` is caller/model-controlled (`internal/tools/telegram.go:18,29` forwards `argString(args, "parse_mode")` verbatim), but only `HTML` gets a markup-aware chunker. Any other mode (`Markdown`, `MarkdownV2`) falls through to `ChunkText`, which cuts blindly at rune/newline boundaries and will split an entity in half (e.g. `**bold**`, `[text](url)`, `_italic_`). Telegram then rejects the whole send with `400 can't parse entities`, so long Markdown messages fail where short ones succeed. Either validate the parse mode locally and reject/escape unsupported markup, or document that only `HTML` is chunk-safe.
- [x] **S3-M98** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/context.go:67` - `truncated` compares `len(recommendations)` with `flagged`, but `contextRecommendations` first appends an aggregate `tools` entry and only then caps the slice at 10. When `flagged == 10` and the aggregate is appended (11 entries capped to 10), truncation is reported as `false`; and whenever more than 10 entries exist the aggregate recommendation is silently dropped. Compute truncation before the cap, accounting for the aggregate (e.g. have the helper return whether it dropped entries). Fixing L77 correctly requires this.
- [x] **S3-M99** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/context.go:182` - If the walk aborts because `ctx` is cancelled, `walkErr` is `ctx.Err()` and this records a synthetic `Kind: "skill"` item with a Flag. Since `contextRecommendations` treats any flagged `skill` item as an oversized SKILL.md, this inflates `flagged` and emits the misleading 'move depth into references/' recommendation, and the handler still returns a success envelope rather than the cancellation error. Propagate cancellation as an error and do not synthesize a skill-flagged item for it.
- [x] **S3-M100** (medium/performance) `internal/tools/data.go:52` - `ReadAll` materialises the entire file in memory and no size cap is applied anywhere on this path; the `ctx` accepted by `loadTable` is never used to bound or abort the read (nor do the row/column-pair loops in `profileColumns`, `applyWhere` and the correlation pass check it). An agent-supplied large CSV/XLSX under data/input can therefore exhaust memory or keep a request busy well past its deadline/cancellation. Consider a max input size / max row count and periodic `ctx.Err()` checks in the scanning loops.
- [x] **S3-M101** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:180` - `parseNumber` delegates to `strconv.ParseFloat`, which accepts `NaN`, `Inf` and `-Inf`. Such cells make the column qualify as `numeric` and flow into Min/Max/Mean/Std, producing non-finite float64 values. The tool result is serialized with `json.MarshalIndent` (internal/mcp/server.go:267), which fails on NaN/Inf, so a table containing e.g. `NaN` or `Inf` makes data.inspect/data.analyze return the opaque error "result marshal failed" instead of data. Exclude non-finite values from the numeric aggregation.
- [x] **S3-M102** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:305` - Output records are keyed by the trimmed header text. Two headers that trim to the same string (e.g. `id` and `id `, or two columns literally named `id`) collapse into one key, so the later column silently overwrites the earlier one and the returned row loses a column without any warning. An empty header also produces an unusable `""` key that `columnIndex` can never select. Detect duplicate/blank headers when loading the table and either warn or disambiguate the output keys.
- [x] **S3-M103** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:356` - Column names are re-resolved with the fuzzy `columnIndex` instead of reusing the index that produced the profile. With duplicated/whitespace-colliding headers (e.g. two columns named `a`), `numeric` contains the same name twice and both lookups return the same index, so the analysis reports a self-correlation `{"columns":["a","a"],"r":1}` (and the profile of the other column is never analysed). Carry the profiled index through, or at least skip identical pairs.
- [x] **S3-M104** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/documents.go:160` - `docxText` wraps `word/document.xml` in `io.LimitReader(rc, maxDocxXMLBytes+1)` but never checks whether the limit was reached. A document.xml larger than 64MB is read only partially: the decoder then either stops at the cut (returning silently truncated text as a success) or fails mid-token with a confusing `unexpected EOF` XML error, and in neither case is the caller warned that the content was truncated. Use a countable limiter and report truncation (e.g. return a distinct error or propagate a `truncated` flag so `extractText` can surface a warning).
- [x] **S3-M105** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/documents.go:319` - `documents.get` never re-clamps the fallback value against the intended 10,000,000 hard cap: when the requested `max_chars` (or the config default) exceeds 10M, `maxChars` is reassigned to `Tools.MaxTextChars`, which `internal/config` accepts for any `v > 0` (config.go:239-243). With `max_text_chars: 50000000`, the guard is a no-op and this handler can return arbitrarily large `text`. `documents.extract` above already has the extra `if maxChars > 10_000_000 { maxChars = 10_000_000 }` clamp, so the two handlers also behave inconsistently. Add the same clamp here (and note that in both handlers an out-of-range *client-supplied* value is silently downgraded to the config value rather than clamped to 10M, which can return far less than the requested/likely-expected volume).
- [x] **S3-M106** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:54` - Persistence paths use non-unique components, so evidence files are silently overwritten. An omitted/empty `snapshot_sha` is copied verbatim and `pathSegment` maps it to the literal "unknown", making every sha-less aggregate (and calibration) write to `evals/<kind>/unknown/aggregate.json` and letting `eval.rank` consume a stale artifact; millisecond-resolution timestamps similarly make `evals/rankings/ranking-<ts>.json` and decision files collide for calls within the same millisecond. Use a unique/content-derived discriminator (reject/warn on empty snapshot sha and/or append a nanosecond/random component) before persisting.
- [x] **S3-M107** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/extras.go:242` - Malformed points are silently accepted with Y defaulting to 0. When `y` is missing, non-numeric (e.g. a string), or the fallback `v` key is absent/not a float64, the point is still appended with `Y: 0`. This injects a fabricated zero datum that distorts the axis range and the plotted line/bar with no warning, unlike the series-level warnings produced in parseChartSeries. Skip (or warn about) points that have no usable numeric Y.
- [x] **S3-M108** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/extras.go:394` - X-axis labels can be drawn from a different series than the one actually plotted, and their positions ignore value-based spacing. - Bar charts render `series[0]`, but the label loop below derives both the text and the slot position from `longest`. When another series has more points, labels describe `longest` while bars come from `series[0]`, so text/positions are wrong. - Line charts with numeric/timestamp X values plot points via `xPosValue(xVals[...])`, yet the labels are placed with index-based `xPos(idx, len(longest.Points))`, so labels no longer sit under the points they name (e.g. irregular timestamps collapse to evenly spaced ticks). Use `series[0]` consistently for label values/positions and, when `useX` is true, place labels with `xPosValue`.
- [x] **S3-M109** (medium/performance) `internal/tools/files.go:54` - Unlike the text branch, which caps reads at `maxTextInspectBytes` (8 MiB) via `readCapped`, these table/XLSX branches call `readAnyTable`/`readXLSXSheets`, both of which load the entire file into memory (`csv.Reader.ReadAll`, full sheet parsing) just to report rows/columns. A multi-GB .csv/.tsv/.xlsx input under data/input will be fully materialized here, so `files.describe` can OOM the process. Consider enforcing a size guard (e.g. skip full parsing or emit a warning when `st.Size()` exceeds the cap) before calling these helpers.
