- [x] **S3-M110** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:46` - Slug validation is duplicated and inconsistent between `plan.create` and `plan.update`. `plan.create` validates the raw input with `containsLetterOrDigit`, but `plans.Slug` only retains ASCII a-z/0-9 and Cyrillic, so a raw slug like `日本語` passes while `plans.Slug` falls back to the stem `plan`, silently creating/clobbering an unrelated plan. `plan.update` uses an ad-hoc condition encoding the same rule differently (so the two tools can drift) and its error message "slug must contain at least one letter or digit" does not match what is checked. Extract one shared helper that validates the normalized slug (e.g. `validPlanSlug`) and use it in both tools.
- [x] **S3-M111** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:49` - `plans.Load` failures other than "file does not exist" (e.g. a corrupt or unreadable JSON file) make this guard fall through, so `plans.Save` silently overwrites the existing plan and the caller never learns the read failed. Only `os.IsNotExist(err)` should be treated as "no existing plan"; any other read error must be surfaced (add the `os` import).
- [x] **S3-M112** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/resources.go:135` - In live mode the completeness check (`reachable < configured`) drops unconfigured providers from the decision entirely. Because `configured` only counts `status.Ready` entries, a profile with no keys at all (or a claimed provider whose env keys are missing) yields `configured == 0, reachable == 0` and therefore an `ok` envelope, while the same call without `live` correctly returns `partial`. The tool can report success for a profile that is not usable at all; the caller (agent) loses the signal. Keep the configuration gap in the check and only additionally require reachability when live: `incomplete = configured < len(results) || (live && reachable < configured)`.
- [x] **S3-M113** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/security.go:122` - Residual from the M129 fix: reading the artifact through `ArtifactPath` + `readCapped` bypasses `Store.ReadArtifact`, which is the only read path that falls back to the attached object store (MinIO/Postgres) and rehydrates the object into `data/artifacts` (see `store.go:187-213`, and `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:202` which always attaches the backends). With a remote backend, an `artifact_id` that has not been materialized in this container/node is now reported as an error, although `documents.get`/`knowledge.search` read the same id successfully via `ReadArtifact`. Prefer a size-limited read on the store (try the local file, then `objects.Get` with a cap) instead of opening the path directly, so the memory bound from M129 is kept without losing the remote fallback.
- [x] **S3-M114** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/system.go:228` - `"learned": true` is hardcoded, and with the source allowlist now validated this is the remaining half of M131: the handler still asserts the router learned even when nothing was applied. `Router.AddFeedback` discards the boolean returned by `applyDopamine` (internal/routing/fly/memory.go:203) and returns `(size, nil)` on that path, so this branch is reached with `err == nil` even when `applyDopamine` bailed out. That happens in reachable cases: `loadBrain()` returns nil when `data/index/fly-brain.json` is absent/invalid (e.g. a fresh checkout before a brain is built), when the embedder is nil, when `embedTask` fails, or when `saveBrain` fails — in all of these PAM/PPL1 and the readout are untouched, yet the caller is told `learned: true`. Surface the real outcome (have `AddFeedback` propagate whether dopamine was applied) instead of unconditionally reporting success.
- [x] **S3-M115** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/util.go:17` - `strconv.ParseFloat` accepts non-finite literals (`NaN`, `Inf`, `+Inf`, `-Inf`, `Infinity`), so `parseNumber` returns `ok == true` for them (both call sites in util.go). This injects NaN/±Inf into downstream consumers: aggregates (`mean`/`stddev` in data.go, `seriesSlopePerHour`/min/max in timeseries.go) become NaN or arbitrary, and `json.MarshalIndent` then fails with `result marshal failed`, destroying the whole tool result; in xlsx.go a non-finite serial combined with a date/time style reaches `excelSerialToTime`/`formatExcelElapsed`/`formatExcelTime`, where `math.Floor(NaN)`/`int(NaN)`/`time.Duration(NaN)` produce garbage dates. Reject non-finite values in `parseNumber` (return `ok=false` after `math.IsNaN`/`math.IsInf`) before they are appended or converted.
- [x] **S3-M116** (medium/bug) `internal/tools/xlsx.go:325` - Cell state machine leaks `inT`. `<is>` start sets `inT = true`, but `</is>` is not handled and `inT`/`inV` are never reset when a new `<c>` begins (only `curVal` is). For a cell like `<c t="inlineStr"><is/></c>`, or any malformed cell where `inT` is set without a matching `</t>`, `inT` stays true; subsequent CharData (inter-element whitespace, formula text, or the next cell's text) is then appended to `curVal`, producing wrong cell values that propagate across cells. Reset both flags at cell start for defensiveness against untrusted input.
- [x] **S3-M117** (medium/security) `internal/tools/xlsx.go:501` - Unbounded XML decoding: unlike worksheets/sharedStrings (which are wrapped in `maxBytesReader`), `xl/styles.xml` here (and `xl/workbook.xml` / `xl/_rels/workbook.xml.rels` in `workbookSheets`) are decoded with a plain `xml.NewDecoder(rc)`. An untrusted archive can therefore decompress a huge styles/workbook part without triggering any size cap, causing unbounded CPU/memory use (zip-bomb style DoS). Wrap these readers with `&maxBytesReader{r: rc, remaining: maxXLSXStylesBytes}` (or reuse an existing limit) so all workbook parts are capped consistently. Note also that `readXLSXSheets` accumulates every sheet with no total cap, so a workbook with very many sheets is likewise unbounded.
- [x] **S3-M118** (medium/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:12` - Arguments passed to the launcher are silently dropped: neither the local-binary branch nor the docker compose branch forwards `"$@"`. Any user/integration-supplied flag (e.g. extra `--repo-root`, verbosity, or future MCP options) is lost without warning. Forward the positional parameters to the exec'd process (and to `docker compose run` after the service name).
- [x] **S3-M119** (medium/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:31` - Wrapping the run in `if ! ...; then exit 1` discards docker compose's real exit status, so callers (MCP harness / integration scripts) always see 1 regardless of whether the failure was a build error, an unreachable daemon, or a missing `.env` value. Capture and propagate the status while still emitting the diagnostic message.
- [x] **S3-M120** (medium/security) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:54` - The permission check only rejects world-writable binaries (`0o002`). A group-writable (`0o020`) file, or one owned by an unprivileged/foreign uid inside the resolved ROOT, still passes and is then executed via `execFileSync`. Reject any group/world-writable mode and, on POSIX, require the binary to be owned by the current user (or root).
- [x] **S3-M121** (medium/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:221` - Activity timestamps are never persisted: `writeActive()` only stores `started_at`, the `tool-use` path never touches the session file, and the session file's mtime is set once at creation and never updated on activity. As a result `updated_at` is referenced but never written, `Date.parse(entry.updated_at || "")` is always 0, `lastActivity` collapses to `stat.mtimeMs` (session start), and TTL/expiry silently degrades to start-based expiry. Likewise the `entries.sort(...)` comment "Latest activity first" is really "latest started last", which misattributes events to whichever session started most recently when `lastActiveID()` is used by `tool-use` without an id. Either drop `started`/`updated_at` and use mtime explicitly (fixing the comment to match), or have `writeActive`/the tool-use path persist real activity timestamps (e.g. `utimesSync` the session file on each tool-use).
- [x] **S3-M122** (medium/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:351` - When the payload supplies an id, `stop` removes that session file and logs `session_end` without verifying the id corresponds to a session this hook actually started/owns. A malformed or hostile payload can therefore delete another session's state file (or emit an `session_end` for a non-existent id), while the genuinely active session lingers and keeps receiving events via `lastActiveID()`. Consider only removing when the file exists and was created by this root, and log/ignore unknown ids.
- [x] **S3-M123** (medium/bug) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:22` - The INT/TERM trap only deletes the profile; after the handler runs, a non-interactive POSIX sh resumes at the interrupted command. The script can therefore continue past an interrupt and then run `go tool cover` against a profile that was already removed, producing a confusing failure (and a non-standard exit status). Re-raise the signal (or exit) so the cleanup is the last thing that happens, and keep the EXIT trap for normal cleanup.
- [x] **S3-M124** (medium/security) `scripts/docker-check.sh` - The keyword list is case-sensitive, so lowercase/`snake_case` keys commonly printed by tooling (`password`, `token`, `secret`, `api_key`, `dsn=...`) are not matched and leak verbatim. The value class `[^ " ]+` also stops at the first space, so a quoted secret containing spaces (e.g. `PGPASSWORD="my secret"`) is only partially redacted and the remainder is printed. Consider matching case-insensitively and handling quoted values (or replacing the whole rest-of-line after a credential-looking key).
- [x] **S3-M125** (medium/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:130` - `docker compose exec -T minio sh -c 'exit 0'` is not a health check: it only proves the container is up and that `exec` works. If MinIO's server fails to bind/serve but the container stays alive, this reports success. It can also produce a false negative on images without a usable `sh`. Since the compose file intentionally ships no healthcheck for MinIO, probe the actual HTTP endpoint from inside the container (or inspect a declared health status), and keep a `seq`-free loop.
- [x] **S3-M126** (medium/maintainability) `scripts/docker-check.sh:153` - Infrastructure lifecycle is inconsistent: on failure `stop_infra` stops postgres/redis/minio, but on success `trap - EXIT` is removed before the infra is ever stopped, so the containers (and bound ports 5432/6379/9000) keep running. This inverts the usual expectation (keep things up to debug a failure, clean up after success) and leads to leaked containers/ports across repeat runs. Please make the intent explicit — e.g. stop infra on the success path too, or gate retention behind an opt-in flag such as `--keep-infra`.
- [x] **S3-M127** (medium/bug) `scripts/doctor.ps1:9` - `TcpClient` is only closed on the timeout branch. Every other failure path — `BeginConnect` throwing, or (the common case) `WaitOne` returning true for a refused/unreachable port and `EndConnect` throwing — leaves the socket and its `AsyncWaitHandle` undisposed. Under repeated probes (this runs on every doctor invocation) that leaks handles. Move the cleanup into a `finally` and dispose the client/async handle on all paths.
- [x] **S3-M128** (medium/maintainability) `scripts/doctor.ps1:61` - The "skills mirror" check only compares top-level directory counts, so it passes when a skill directory exists on both sides but its files are missing, stale or divergent — the integrity guarantee the check implies is not actually enforced. Compare the mirrored file set (relative paths, or content hashes) instead of the directory count.
- [x] **S3-M129** (medium/maintainability) `scripts/doctor.ps1:111` - Routing the probe through `cmd.exe` hides both the exit code and stderr (`2>nul`), so a failing `codex plugin list` is indistinguishable from a well-formed but empty response and is reported as the misleading "codex plugin list returned no output". `scripts/plugin-check.ps1` invokes codex directly (`& codex plugin list --json 2>$null`) and checks `$LASTEXITCODE` — keep the same pattern here and report the exit code when it is non-zero.
- [x] **S3-M130** (medium/bug) `scripts/doctor.ps1:118` - If the response has no `installed` property (schema change, error payload, `null`), `foreach` over `$null` iterates zero times and the check silently reports "install: scripts/setup.ps1 or codex plugin add ..." instead of surfacing the shape problem. `plugin-check.ps1` explicitly guards this case (`if (-not $parsed.installed) { ... }`); the inline copy here should do the same so a broken payload is not reported as "not installed".
- [x] **S3-M131** (medium/bug) `scripts/doctor.ps1:158` - `smoke.ps1` is invoked without first resetting `$LASTEXITCODE` and without verifying the file exists. If the invocation never runs (script missing/renamed, or it errors before its own `exit`), `$LASTEXITCODE` keeps the value left by an earlier native command (e.g. `docker info`/`docker images`) and the check can pass or fail on a stale value. Note the plugin-check branch has the opposite hazard: it sets `$global:LASTEXITCODE = 0` beforehand, so a missing `plugin-check.ps1` false-passes as "plugin runtime". Guard both with `Test-Path`/`$?` and reset the exit code before invoking. Also, since smoke output is discarded (`-Quiet`), the hardcoded detail "initialize / tools/list / system.context" is shown even when the real cause is "no MCP runtime available", hiding the failure reason.
- [ ] **S3-M132** (medium/maintainability) `scripts/doctor.sh:71` - `PLUGIN_STATE=unknown` is never assigned anywhere in the script, so this branch is dead code, while genuinely unparsable output (non-JSON text from `codex plugin list --json`, or a JSON payload that the python3 branch bails out of) is silently reported as `missing` via the `*)` default with the misleading hint "run scripts/setup.sh". Either drop this branch or set `PLUGIN_STATE=unknown` when `PLUGIN_JSON` is non-empty but no parser could interpret it, and add an explicit `missing)` branch so the setup hint is unambiguous.
- [ ] **S3-M133** (medium/bug) `scripts/doctor.sh:175` - Presence of the resources profile is inferred by grepping for `"present": true` anywhere in the binary's JSON output; any nested or unrelated object containing that key (now or after a schema change) produces a false positive, and a differently-shaped payload (e.g. `"present":true` on a line with other formatting) can produce a false negative. Prefer parsing with `jq -e` when available (with the current grep only as a last-resort fallback), and target the exact path rather than a global text match.
- [ ] **S3-M134** (medium/security) `scripts/env-set.sh:50` - `readlink -f` is GNU-specific (BSD/macOS readlink does not accept `-f`), and resolving the symlink here is a TOCTOU: the target can be swapped between resolution and the later `mv`, redirecting the secret write to an unintended path. Also, when `readlink` is unavailable or returns nothing, `FILE` stays as the symlink and `mv "$TMP" "$FILE"` replaces the symlink itself with a regular file, silently changing semantics. Consider not dereferencing symlinks at all, or re-checking the resolved path before the write.
- [ ] **S3-M135** (medium/security) `scripts/env-set.sh:64` - When `mktemp` fails the code falls back to a predictable, PID-derived path (`$FILE.tmp.$$`). An attacker able to write to the target directory can pre-create a symlink at that name so the subsequent `awk ... > "$TMP"` follows it and overwrites an arbitrary file with the secret value. Prefer failing loudly instead of using a guessable temp name.
- [ ] **S3-M136** (medium/bug) `scripts/env-set.sh:87` - The value is emitted verbatim after `k=`. Values containing spaces, quotes, `#`, or embedded newlines produce a syntactically broken or misleading `.env` line — a newline in particular injects additional `KEY=value` lines. Multiline secrets (e.g. PEM keys) are therefore corrupted, and there is no way to recover the exact value on read-back. Encode/quote the value (single-quote it and escape embedded single quotes) or reject values that contain newlines.
- [ ] **S3-M137** (medium/security) `scripts/env-set.sh:97` - Permission/ownership preservation fails silently: the `stat` fallback defaults `MODE` to 600 when probing fails, and both `chmod`/`chown` swallow errors with `|| true`. For a credentials file this means a silent failure can leave the file at an unintended mode or owner (e.g. `chown` fails for a non-root user and ownership is silently changed to the caller). At minimum emit a warning when the mode/owner cannot be preserved, and validate that `$MODE` is a sane octal value before applying it.
- [ ] **S3-M138** (medium/test) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:48` - The helper contains a dedicated symlink-resolution branch (`[ -L "$FILE" ]` + `readlink`) that rewrites the link target instead of replacing the link, but this suite never creates a symlinked .env, so that branch (and the risk of clobbering the symlink) is unverified. Add a case that creates `real.env` (0600), symlinks `link.env -> real.env`, runs the helper on `link.env`, and asserts `real.env` holds the new key, is still 0600, and `link.env` is still a symlink.
- [ ] **S3-M139** (medium/test) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:69` - The stdin path is only exercised with a newline-free value, so the helper's documented "a single trailing newline is a line terminator" behavior (`VALUE=$(cat)` followed by the trailing-newline strip) is never verified, nor is empty stdin. The comment also claims "secrets stay out of argv", but nothing asserts that. Please add cases such as `printf 'from-stdin\n' | sh "$HELPER" ... S` (expect `^S=from-stdin$`), an empty stdin (expect `^S=$`), and a check that the value does not appear in the helper's argv.
- [ ] **S3-M140** (medium/test) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:74` - This block is the only value-fidelity test, and it stops at backslashes. A value containing an embedded newline (e.g. a multi-line secret/PEM key) is emitted by the helper as `print k "=" v`, which produces a second, unparsable line in the .env and corrupts the file — a plausible data-integrity regression that this suite would not catch. Add cases that round-trip a value with an embedded newline and an explicitly empty value (`sh "$HELPER" f K ''` → `^K=$`).
- [ ] **S3-M141** (medium/test) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:78` - This case claims to prove that keys are matched literally rather than as regexes, but `A_B` / `A_XB` contain no BRE metacharacters, so a regex-based implementation (e.g. `grep -q "^$KEY="` or `sed "s/^$KEY=//"`) would pass it just as well — `^A_B=` still only matches the first line. Use a key that actually contains a metacharacter so literal vs. regex matching is distinguishable, and escape it in the assertions (`. ` is the only metachar that needs escaping here).
- [x] **S3-M142** (medium/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.ps1:7` - With `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"`, `Write-Error` raises a terminating error, so **every** `exit 1` in this script is unreachable dead code (this applies to all six `Write-Error`/`exit 1` pairs, not just this one). The failure escapes as an unhandled exception, so the caller observes whatever exit code/stack the host happens to produce instead of the explicit `1`, and the intended diagnostic is buried in an exception dump. Either keep the explicit exit by marking the diagnostic non-terminating (`-ErrorAction Continue`) or drop the `exit 1` lines and `throw` consistently.
- [x] **S3-M143** (medium/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.ps1:49` - `Move-Item -Force` is not guaranteed to be an atomic replace on Windows: the FileSystem provider may delete the existing destination and then rename the temp file into place. A crash (or a failure such as a lock/handle on `.mcp.json`) between the delete and the rename leaves the previous manifest lost and no new manifest published — the `finally` only removes the temp file. Also note two concurrent runs both write to the same destination with no lock (only the temp names are PID-distinct), so last-writer-wins applies to the whole read/validate/replace sequence. Prefer an atomic replace primitive, e.g. `[System.IO.File]::Replace` (destination exists) falling back to `[System.IO.File]::Move`.
- [ ] **S3-M144** (medium/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:18` - The comment overstates what is done. The `tr '\r\n\t\f\v' ' '` stage maps CR/LF/TAB/FF/VT to spaces, so the trailing `sed -e 's/<TAB>/\\t/g'` stage can never match (dead code, and it does not emit the `\t` escape it appears to intend). Worse, other C0 controls such as `\b` (0x08) and the remaining bytes < 0x20 are still passed through unescaped, so a root path containing them produces invalid JSON (this is the residual of backlog M163, which was marked fixed). Either escape every control character (e.g. emit `\uXXXX` sequences) or reject values containing them so the generated manifest is always valid JSON.
- [x] **S3-M145** (medium/security) `scripts/plugin-check.ps1:45` - `$cmd`/`$argList` are taken verbatim from the plugin-controlled `.mcp.json` and executed directly. A tampered/malicious installed plugin can therefore cause arbitrary command execution during the check. Validate that the resolved command path exists and, ideally, that `$sourcePath` resolves under the expected repository/plugin location before invoking it.
- [x] **S3-M146** (medium/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.ps1:84` - Parsing each stdout line independently assumes one compact JSON object per line. If the server pretty-prints its responses (multi-line JSON) or interleaves log lines, every line fails `ConvertFrom-Json` and is silently skipped, so the check reports a misleading "missing JSON-RPC response" instead of the real cause. Consider accumulating the full output and extracting complete JSON objects (e.g. track brace depth) or at least report parse failures to stderr so this is diagnosable.
- [ ] **S3-M147** (medium/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:19` - `status=$?` is read inside the `then` branch of `if ! codex ...`, i.e. it captures the exit status of the *negated* condition, which is always 0 when the branch is taken. The diagnostic therefore always prints `failed (exit 0)` and hides the real failure code. Capture the status before negating (the `|| status=$?` form is also safe under `set -e`).
- [ ] **S3-M148** (medium/security) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:65` - The execution guard is too shallow for a tree whose manifest is about to be executed: `os.stat` follows symlinks, only the directory's `S_IWOTH` bit is checked (group-writable `S_IWGRP` passes), and neither `.mcp.json` nor the referenced command/launcher is inspected. A symlinked or group-writable plugin tree therefore bypasses the intended mitigation. Resolve the real path and check ownership/writability of the directory, the manifest and the command (accepting that a residual TOCTOU window remains).
- [ ] **S3-M149** (medium/bug) `scripts/quality-gate.sh:13` - Staleness detection is incomplete: only `*.go` files under `cmd/` and `internal/` are compared with the prebuilt binary. A `go.mod`/`go.sum` bump (dependency behaviour change with no `.go` edit), any non-`.go` compile input (the repo already uses `//go:embed`, e.g. `cmd/demo-api/main.go`), or a package added outside those two trees will not trigger a rebuild, so a stale gate binary can store evidence for the *current* SHA that was produced by old gate logic — exactly the "a stale green is not evidence" case the docs forbid. Additionally, when no toolchain is present the check is skipped entirely and the stale binary is exec'd without any warning. Include the module files in the freshness test and/or provide an explicit force-build override.
- [ ] **S3-M150** (medium/bug) `scripts/quality-gate.sh:25` - `mktemp` returns the template path as given, so a relative `TMPDIR` (e.g. `TMPDIR=tmp`) yields a relative `$TMP_BIN`. After the later `cd "$ROOT"`, `go build -o "$TMP_BIN"` writes to a different (usually non-existent) path and `rm -f "$TMP_BIN"` cleans the wrong file, leaving stray artifacts and a failing build. Normalise the temp directory to an absolute path before creating the file.
- [ ] **S3-M151** (medium/bug) `scripts/quality-gate.sh:27` - The signal handler hardcodes 130 for both signals (TERM should be 143 = 128+15), so callers cannot distinguish an interrupt from a termination, and it neither terminates nor waits for the in-flight `go build`/`$TMP_BIN` child — an externally delivered TERM to the shell leaves the gate process (or a long `go build`) orphaned while its temp file is unlinked underneath it. Note also that `exit` from the trap re-enters the EXIT trap, so `cleanup_tmp` runs twice (harmless with `rm -f`, but worth being explicit about).
- [x] **S3-M152** (medium/bug) `scripts/repair.ps1:20` - With `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"` the result of `New-Item` is never checked, so a failure to create `logs/`, `evals/` or the `data/*` directories (existing file with the same name, permission denied, locked path) only writes to the error stream and the script continues and prints "done". Check the outcome and fail loudly, e.g. with `-ErrorAction Stop` plus a targeted message, so a partially prepared environment is not reported as repaired.
- [x] **S3-M153** (medium/bug) `scripts/repair.ps1:65` - `$LASTEXITCODE` is not reset before the two `codex` invocations, and both calls discard stdout/stderr with `*> $null`. If `codex` resolves to a non-native shim (function/alias) the variable keeps the stale `0` from the preceding `write-install-state.ps1` call, so a failed marketplace add/reinstall can be reported as "plugin reinstalled" (the H38 pattern that was fixed for the other calls). Capture the exit code explicitly and keep the error text for the warning so failures stay actionable.
- [ ] **S3-M154** (medium/bug) `scripts/repair.sh:14` - Fragile profile parsing with a silent fallback that then *overwrites* the recorded state. The sed pattern only matches when `"profile"` begins its own line, so compact or reordered JSON (e.g. a single-line `{"profile":"core",...}`) yields an empty `DETECTED`; the script then falls back to `full` and `write-install-state.sh` permanently rewrites `configs/install-state.json` with profile `full`, silently downgrading a core install. Prefer parsing the JSON with python3/node (as `sync-harness.sh` does) or, when the value cannot be read, abort instead of guessing (the state file exists, so it clearly has a profile).
- [ ] **S3-M155** (medium/bug) `scripts/repair.sh:32` - Runtime/build guard is inconsistent with what gets recorded. When Docker is detected, `RUNTIME=docker` and missing `bin/*` binaries are never rebuilt, so a host that later loses its Docker engine has no working fallback. Worse, `docker info` succeeding does not prove the MCP image/containers exist: `setup.sh` only records `docker` after a successful `docker compose build ... && up -d`, whereas here the state is written as `docker` unconditionally, so `configs/install-state.json` can claim a runtime that is not actually available. Consider verifying the image/containers (or at least warning + rebuilding binaries when `bin/*` is missing regardless of runtime).
- [ ] **S3-M156** (medium/bug) `scripts/repair.sh:37` - Fixed `.tmp` file names are not concurrency-safe and the publish step is not atomic. Two concurrent repair runs write the same `bin/hackathonkit-mcp.tmp` etc., so one run can `mv` a half-written binary produced by the other; a single stale/partial `.tmp` is also left behind if the run is interrupted. Additionally, under `set -e` a failure in the second or third `mv` leaves `bin/` with a mix of new and old binaries (no cleanup of the remaining `.tmp` files). Use a per-process suffix (e.g. `mktemp`/`$$`) and install all three artifacts under a trap that cleans up incomplete temps.
- [ ] **S3-M157** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:71` - The track filter is still far looser than the documented `01-10` range / backend contract (see REVIEW-BACKLOG M184, which this pattern does not actually implement). It accepts single digits (`8`), out-of-range two-digit ids (`00`, `99`), trailing-hyphen slugs (`12-`) and arbitrary lowercase words. A value such as `99` or `99-anything` passes this `case`, is then rejected by `mcp --resources-set` with "unknown track", and because of `set -e` the whole questionnaire aborts *after* the secrets were already written to `.env` — the user loses every answer and gets an inconsistent state instead of the intended "ignoring invalid track" path. Tighten the numeric alternatives to `0[1-9]|10` (optionally with a non-empty `-slug` suffix) and reject slugs with a trailing hyphen; additionally, do not let an `mcp --resources-set` failure abort silently — report it explicitly.
- [ ] **S3-M158** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:103` - No atomicity/rollback and the failure is invisible to the user. Secrets are written to `.env` one at a time before this call; under `set -e` any failure here (or in a mid-run `set_key`, unwritable `.env`, `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` missing and `go run` failing) aborts the script with `.env` already modified and `configs/resources.json` not updated — the two stores are left inconsistent and there is no message telling the user which parts were applied. Consider collecting all secret values first, writing them, and only then persisting the profile, and wrap the profile write so a failure prints a clear diagnostic plus the fact that the `.env` keys were already saved.
- [x] **S3-M159** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:4` - `Continue` means every cmdlet failure in this bootstrap (Copy-Item of `.env`, New-Item of the working directories, Set-Location, Remove-Item) is a non-terminating error that only lands on the error stream while the script keeps going and finally prints "[setup] done.". Because the guard checks below only look at `$LASTEXITCODE` (native/child-process commands), a partially applied setup is reported as success. The sibling scripts (`sync-harness.ps1`, `gen-mcp-manifest.ps1`, `repair.ps1`) all use `"Stop"` for this reason. Suggest `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` plus try/catch around the individual steps that must degrade gracefully (or explicit `-ErrorAction Stop` on the critical cmdlets).
- [x] **S3-M160** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:15` - Two problems with this guard. (1) The comparison is case-sensitive (`-ne` on strings) while `$root` is built by string manipulation from `$PSCommandPath` (which preserves the case used on the command line) and `Get-Location` returns the provider-canonicalized path, so on Windows a differently-cased invocation (e.g. `./Scripts/setup.ps1`) can falsely abort with "could not enter the repository root". (2) `Set-Location` runs under `Continue`, so the failure it is meant to detect is only inferred afterwards; it is cleaner to make the chdir itself fatal. Also note `$PSCommandPath` is populated for dot-sourced scripts too, so the earlier guard does not actually detect dot-sourcing as its message claims. Suggest canonicalizing (`Resolve-Path`) and comparing with an explicit StringComparison, and/or catching the Set-Location error.
- [x] **S3-M161** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:22` - This copy is not validated: if `.env.example` is missing or `.env` is locked, `Copy-Item` raises a non-terminating error (masked by `Continue`) and the script continues as if `.env` existed, then announces success. Guard the source and fail loudly, e.g. verify `.env.example` exists and pass `-ErrorAction Stop` (wrapped in try/catch).
- [x] **S3-M162** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:27` - `$LASTEXITCODE` is only updated by native executables and by PowerShell scripts that explicitly call `exit`; `sync-harness.ps1` never calls `exit 0` on success and `gen-mcp-manifest.ps1` (checked at line 40) does not either, so these guards can read a stale value inherited from the caller's session (or from an earlier command). `repair.ps1` avoids this by resetting `$global:LASTEXITCODE = 0` before every child-script invocation — apply the same pattern here (and prefer checking `$?`/`$Error` as well).
- [x] **S3-M163** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:32` - Destructive recursive deletion with no containment check and no failure reporting: if `$dir` were ever mis-resolved (or a junction/symlink inside `.agents\skills` pointed outside the repo), `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force` would delete outside the repository, and `-ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` swallows any failure while the script still prints that the skills were excluded. Constrain the deletion to the repo root and report failures. The hardcoded list is also duplicated in `scripts/setup.sh`, `AGENTS.md` and `README.md`, so drift is easy — consider a single source of truth. Note as well that `write-install-state.ps1` compares the `.agents\skills` mirror against the plugin's full skill set, so in the `core` profile it will warn about "missing" skills and record the pruned count; that mismatch should be handled or documented.
- [x] **S3-M164** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:76` - The result of `write-install-state.ps1` is never checked, unlike every other step. That script inherits `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"` from this scope, does not call `exit` on success, and writes `configs/install-state.json` with `Set-Content`, so a failed write is completely silent while setup reports success (`doctor.ps1` then reports a missing/stale install state). Check the exit code and/or assert the file was written. `repair.ps1` performs this check; setup.ps1 should too.
- [x] **S3-M165** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.ps1:81` - The plugin registration sequence hides evidence of a partially installed state: the exit code of `codex plugin remove` is discarded, and both `marketplace add` and `plugin add` redirect all streams to `$null`, so a failure is only a warning with no diagnostics. Worse, after `marketplace add` fails the script still runs `plugin add`, which can then succeed against a stale marketplace registration, leaving the user with a plugin pointing at old sources. Track the failures, surface the captured output on error, and skip `plugin add` when the marketplace could not be (re)registered.
- [ ] **S3-M166** (medium/security) `scripts/setup.sh:51` - The `stat` fallback masks its own failure: when neither `stat -c` nor `stat -f` works, `ENV_MODE` becomes the literal `600`, the `*00` branch matches, and the remediation is skipped even though the real mode is unknown. Since `.env` holds credentials, an unreadable `stat` must not be treated as "already safe". Use a sentinel that forces the `chmod`, and do not swallow a failed `chmod` silently.
- [ ] **S3-M167** (medium/security) `scripts/setup.sh:59` - When `od` is unavailable or fails, `TOKEN` is empty and the script silently continues without a demo token. Per the project's own backlog (M55/H4), an empty `HACKATHONKIT_DEMO_TOKEN` disables demo-API authentication, so this failure should not be silent — warn (or fail) so the operator knows the demo API is unauthenticated.
- [ ] **S3-M168** (medium/security) `scripts/setup.sh:61` - The secret is passed as a command-line argument, so it is visible in `ps`/`/proc/<pid>/cmdline` to any local user while the call runs. `scripts/env-set.sh` explicitly supports reading the value from stdin (`value read from stdin when omitted` ... "secrets never appear in the process argv"); use that path instead.
- [ ] **S3-M169** (medium/bug) `scripts/setup.sh:94` - Partial-failure handling is inconsistent: if `build` succeeds but `up -d postgres redis minio` fails (e.g. one service comes up, another does not), `RUNTIME` falls back to `binary` and that value is written to install-state, while half-started containers are left running in the background. Subsequent runs then operate on a stale/inconsistent state. Consider tearing the partial stack down before falling back (or recording the actual state).
- [x] **S3-M170** (medium/bug) `scripts/smoke.ps1:2` - `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"` lets non-terminating failures pass silently. If `Start-Process` fails, `$proc` stays `$null` and the next statement throws an unhandled "cannot call a method on a null-valued expression" instead of a clear diagnostic; if `Get-Content -LiteralPath $outFile` fails, `$output` stays `$null` and the script falls through to the parsing block, reporting a misleading "MCP handshake FAILED" rather than the real cause. Prefer `-ErrorAction Stop` (or an explicit try/catch with the underlying error message) around Start-Process/Get-Content so infrastructure failures abort loudly.
- [x] **S3-M171** (medium/bug) `scripts/smoke.ps1:51` - On timeout only the `docker` CLI process is killed; the container started by `docker compose run` is not. `--rm` removes the container only after it exits, so a hung MCP server (exactly the case this timeout guards against) keeps running and leaks a container plus its `depends_on` services. Kill the container explicitly in the timeout path (e.g. run it with a known `--name` and `docker rm -f`, or `docker compose ... down`) instead of relying on `--rm`. While here, `$proc` is never disposed — add `$proc.Dispose()` after the wait to release the process handle.
- [ ] **S3-M172** (medium/bug) `scripts/smoke.sh:41` - The compose path sends stderr to `/dev/null` instead of the shared `$ERR_FILE`. As a result `fail_with_stderr` can never print any diagnostic for compose runs (ERR_FILE stays empty), and genuine Docker/compose errors (image pull failures, missing daemon, container startup errors) are silently swallowed — unlike the binary path which captures stderr. Redirect to `$ERR_FILE` so failures are diagnosable.
- [ ] **S3-M173** (medium/test) `scripts/smoke.sh:94` - The python validator hard-codes request order (`expected = [1, 2, 3]` consumed via `zip`) and the shell fallback compares `GOT_IDS` to the literal `1,2,3`. JSON-RPC/MCP permits responses to arrive in any order for concurrently handled requests, so a compliant server that replies 3,1,2 will be reported as a handshake failure. Validate the *set* of ids (and reject duplicates) instead of positional order.
- [ ] **S3-M174** (medium/bug) `scripts/smoke.sh:117` - The grep fallback assumes compact JSON with no space before the colon (`"id": *[0-9]*`). Any pretty-printed/spaced output such as `"id" : 1` (or `"id": 1` handled, but not `"id" :`) yields an empty match, so `GOT_IDS` becomes wrong and the smoke test falsely fails. Allow optional whitespace around the colon.
- [ ] **S3-M175** (medium/bug) `scripts/smoke.sh:126` - `grep -q '"isError":true'` (and the sibling `grep -q '"error"'`) are substring matches against the raw line, so they also fire on tool payload content: `system.context` results that legitimately contain the text `"error"` or `"isError": true` (spaced) will be reported as handshake failures, while a spaced `"isError": true` is missed. Prefer a JSON-aware check (python is already the primary path) or at least tolerate whitespace: `grep -qE '"isError"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*true'`.
- [ ] **S3-M176** (medium/test) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:60` - The stub records the full argv (`$*`) but this assertion only matches the marker prefix and the stdin count, never the arguments. Because the fake docker ignores `"$@"`, a regression in `run_compose` (e.g. dropping the `compose` subcommand, using `docker run`, or losing `-f .../docker-compose.yml run --rm -T hackathonkit-mcp`) still passes every transport check. `grep -q` also cannot detect docker being invoked more than once. This leaves the H46 fix (assert on the recorded invocation) incomplete.
- [ ] **S3-M177** (medium/test) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:73` - `$REPO/bin/hackathonkit-mcp` is created once and never removed, so only the branch where the local binary exists is exercised. The key `--auto` fallback path (no local binary -> must fall through to `docker compose`, smoke.sh:72-79) is untested, and the default no-argument invocation (`smoke.sh` treats `""` as `--auto`) is never run either. Add a case that deletes the binary and asserts docker is used.
- [x] **S3-M178** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.ps1:36` - The copy into the Claude mirror does not exclude reparse points (symlinks/junctions), unlike the sibling `sync-skills.ps1` which filters them with `Where-Object { -not ($_.Attributes -band [System.IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint) }`. `Copy-Item -Recurse -Force` on a directory symlink can throw ("cannot be followed because its type is disabled") or silently dereference and duplicate/copy unintended trees, so the two mirrors can diverge and the run can fail after the source-side sync already succeeded. Apply the same reparse-point filter here for parity.
- [x] **S3-M179** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.ps1:56` - The `finally` block only cleans `$ClaudeTmp`; it never removes `$ClaudeOld`. If the post-swap `Remove-Item -LiteralPath $ClaudeOld` fails (e.g. a file is locked on Windows, and it runs under `Stop` so it throws), or the process is interrupted between `Move-Item $ClaudeSkills -> $ClaudeOld` and the swap, `.claude/skills` is left absent and a permanent `.old.$PID` directory accumulates with no cleanup path. The sibling shell script limits the same exposure via `trap cleanup_tmp EXIT`, but here nothing recovers the stale `.old` directory. Also note the swap is not atomic under concurrent invocations: a second run sees `$ClaudeSkills` already moved aside (skips the move), then `Move-Item -Force` onto an existing non-empty directory fails, producing spurious failures. Initialize `$ClaudeOld = $null` before the `try` and clean it in `finally` (guarding against `$null`).
- [x] **S3-M180** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.ps1:87` - `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"` makes `Write-Error` a terminating error, so it throws out of the `catch` block and `$Missing++` (and therefore the subsequent `if ($Missing -ne 0) { exit 1 }`) is unreachable dead code. The intended "count invalid manifests and exit" logic never runs. `$Missing` is also reused as both the missing-file counter and the invalid-manifest counter, which conflates two distinct failure modes (the shell version keeps separate `MISSING`/`INVALID` counters). Use `-ErrorAction Continue` for the diagnostic and a distinct counter.
- [ ] **S3-M181** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:7` - If `command -v` fails, `SELF` stays a bare filename, `dirname` returns `.`, and `ROOT` is resolved against the current working directory — every later path (including `rm -rf "$CLAUDE_TMP"`/`$CLAUDE_OLD`) then targets the wrong tree. After resolution, verify that `SELF` contains a `/` (and is not empty) and abort with a clear message otherwise.
- [ ] **S3-M182** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:19` - No mutual exclusion between concurrent runs. Two invocations interleave badly: process B can recreate `$CLAUDE_DST` while process A is still in its window, so A's `mv "$CLAUDE_TMP" "$CLAUDE_DST"` moves the staging dir *inside* the freshly installed mirror (creating `.claude/skills/skills.tmp.<pid>`), and A then `rm -rf`s its `.old` — silently destroying the previous mirror. Consider a lock (e.g. `mkdir`-based lock dir next to `$CLAUDE_DST`) or re-checking/using `mv -T`-style semantics where available.
- [ ] **S3-M183** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:31` - The rollback path is silent and incomplete: no diagnostic is printed to stderr, and if `$CLAUDE_OLD` does not exist (nothing was moved because the mirror was already missing) the failed `mv` just exits 1. Worse, `[ -d ... ] && mv ...` followed by `exit 1` — if the restoring `mv` itself fails, `set -e` aborts before the `exit 1` with no message either. Emit an explicit error so CI logs show why the mirror was not updated.
- [ ] **S3-M184** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:65` - The no-validator fallback gives a false sense of security: any file whose first byte is `{` passes, so truncated or malformed manifests (the exact failure this check exists to catch) are reported as valid. It also rejects valid JSON that starts with whitespace or a UTF-8 BOM. Either fail loudly when no validator is available, or at least check that the first non-space byte is `{` **and** the last non-space byte is `}`.
- [x] **S3-M185** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:3` - `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` turns `Write-Error` into a *terminating* error, so this call (and the identical one for the repo-root check) aborts the script before the following `exit 1` — that line is dead code and the caller gets a propagated exception rather than a deterministic exit code. It is also inconsistent with the third call below, which explicitly passes `-ErrorAction Continue`. Mark every diagnostic `Write-Error` as `-ErrorAction Continue`, or drop `Write-Error` and rely on `exit 1`/`throw` consistently.
- [x] **S3-M186** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:11` - Backslash-separated path segments only resolve on Windows. On PowerShell Core (Linux/macOS) `\` is a literal filename character, so `plugins\hackathonkit\skills` never matches, the script always fails the `Test-Path $src` check with "skills source not found", and `$dst` would point at a bogus directory. Use forward slashes (accepted on all platforms) or nested `Join-Path` calls.
- [x] **S3-M187** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:21` - The PID-suffixed temp directory only prevents collisions on `$tmp`, not races on the shared `$dst`. Two concurrent runs (e.g. `sync-harness.ps1` and a manual invocation) can interleave their `Remove-Item`/`Move-Item` steps: run A removes the mirror, run B publishes its copy, then A's `Move-Item -Force` either fails or — worse — nests its temp directory *inside* the re-created `$dst` as `.agents/skills/skills.tmp.<pid>`. Serialize access to `$dst` with a lock (e.g. a `$dst.lock` directory created with `New-Item -ErrorAction Stop` and retried, as `sync-skills.sh` does) and release it in the `finally` block.
- [x] **S3-M188** (medium/security) `scripts/sync-skills.ps1:26` - The reparse-point filter only applies to the direct children of `$src`. `Copy-Item -Recurse` will still follow symlinks/junctions nested inside a skill directory, which can pull content from outside the repository into the published mirror, duplicate external trees, or recurse indefinitely / blow MAX_PATH on Windows PowerShell 5.1 (and a dangling link makes the whole copy fail). Reject reparse points recursively (scan `$src` with `-Recurse` before copying) or use a copy method that preserves them as links.
- [ ] **S3-M189** (medium/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:55` - The comment claims consumers "never observe a missing or half-populated mirror", but moving `$DST` to `$OLD` and then `$TMP` to `$DST` is two renames, leaving a window in which `.agents/skills` does not exist at all. Concurrent readers during that gap see an absent directory rather than the old or new mirror. Either correct the comment or perform an atomic replacement (e.g. swap a symlink, or use `renameat2(RENAME_EXCHANGE)` where available).
- [x] **S3-M190** (medium/bug) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:17` - Failure detection is only valid when `codex` resolves to a native executable. If it resolves to an alias/function or a `.ps1` shim, `$LASTEXITCODE` is never updated, so the pre-set `$global:LASTEXITCODE = 0` makes a failed removal look successful; the `*> $null` redirection also discards the diagnostic output needed to understand the failure. Prefer a `try { & codex ... ; if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {...} } catch {...}` shape (or check `$?`), and surface the captured output when the command fails.
- [x] **S3-M191** (medium/security) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:43` - The containment check is purely lexical on the joined string. `Remove-Item -LiteralPath ... -Recurse -Force` on a reparse point (junction/symlink) placed inside the repository — e.g. `bin`, `data\control`, or a skill folder pointing at an external directory — can delete content outside `$root` (notably on Windows PowerShell 5.1, where -Recurse follows the link). Consider resolving the final target and refusing to recurse into reparse points (e.g. inspect `(Get-Item -LiteralPath $target).LinkType` / `Attributes -band ReparsePoint`) before deleting. The same applies to the per-skill `$skillTarget` built below, which is not re-validated at all.
- [x] **S3-M192** (medium/bug) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:80` - With `$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"`, `Write-Error` raises a terminating error, so `exit 1` here (and the same pattern at lines 5 and 10) is dead code: the script aborts with an unhandled exception instead of an explicit exit code, and the resulting exit status depends on how the script was invoked (`pwsh -File` vs. dot-sourcing/`&` in an existing session). Additionally the message omits `$codexFail`, so a codex-only failure reports "0 path failure(s)" while still exiting non-zero. Emit the warning with a non-terminating stream and then exit explicitly.
- [ ] **S3-M193** (medium/security) `scripts/uninstall.sh:10` - M208 residual: the script's real path is still not resolved. `dirname -- "$SELF"` follows the *location of the invocation*, so a symlink/copied launcher (e.g. `/usr/local/bin/uninstall-hackathonkit -> .../scripts/uninstall.sh`) makes ROOT the launcher's parent, and the only guard is the presence of `docker-compose.yml` + `go.mod` — satisfied by any Go project that also uses compose. In that case `rm -rf "$ROOT/bin"`, `$ROOT/.claude/skills`, etc. run against an unrelated tree. The `command -v` fallback has the same weakness for a bare-name invocation (ROOT is then derived from the current working directory). Resolve symlinks before deriving ROOT (e.g. a small `while [ -L "$SELF" ]; do ... done` loop or `readlink -f` when available) and assert a hackathonkit-specific marker in addition to the generic two files. Also consider porting the path-containment check from `scripts/uninstall.ps1:42-47` (`$target.StartsWith($rootPrefix)`) as defence in depth before each `rm -rf`.
- [ ] **S3-M194** (medium/bug) `scripts/uninstall.sh:45` - This success line is printed unconditionally after the two `codex ... remove` attempts, even when both of them failed and `CODEX_FAIL` was set to 1. The user sees "removed hackathonkit plugin and marketplace" immediately after the two failure warnings, which reports success for an operation that did not happen. Print it only when both commands succeeded (or qualify it, e.g. "codex plugin/marketplace removal completed with errors").
- [ ] **S3-M195** (medium/bug) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:9` - ROOT resolution is still fragile (the L139 fix is only partial): 1) when `command -v` finds nothing, SELF stays a bare name, `dirname` yields `.` and ROOT silently becomes the *parent of the caller's cwd* — a validation gate then reports a false "pass" for the wrong tree; 2) symlinks are not resolved, so invoking the script through a link still mis-resolves ROOT; 3) nothing asserts that the resolved ROOT is really this repo. At minimum resolve symlinks where possible and fail loudly when the resolved root does not look like this checkout:
- [ ] **S3-M196** (medium/bug) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:18` - `$?` inside the `then` body of `if ! cmd; then` is the status of the `!` negation (always 0), not of `go run` — so the label always prints `(go run exit 0)` on every failure. This defeats the exact diagnostic M211 introduced (distinguishing "validator failed" from "validator could not be built"). Capture the real status before printing it:
- [x] **S3-M197** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:16` - `$Root` is used unvalidated to build every path, and its existence is never checked. A typo or a non-install directory still produces a state file (or a bogus `unknown`/`skills=0` record), which is hard to distinguish from a genuinely broken install. Validate that `$Root` is an existing container before proceeding.
- [x] **S3-M198** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:19` - Version is scraped with a regex instead of parsing the JSON, so a `"version"` occurrence in a comment, dependency block, or nested object can be picked up first and yield a wrong value; when nothing matches the script silently falls back to `unknown` with no warning. Prefer `ConvertFrom-Json` and warn when the version cannot be determined.
- [x] **S3-M199** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:23` - The mirror check is one-sided and silently degrades: if `plugins\hackathonkit\skills` is missing, `Get-ChildItem -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` yields an empty `$srcNames`, so no mismatch is ever reported and `skills` is just set to the mirror count; extra directories in the mirror are also ignored. A broken install therefore looks healthy. Warn when the source skills directory is absent and consider reporting mirror-only extras.
- [x] **S3-M200** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.ps1:42` - `hooks`, `agents`, `learning` are hardcoded to `$true` and `adapters` is a fixed list, regardless of what was actually installed. The emitted `install-state.json` therefore misrepresents the real install (e.g. a core-only install reports all components present). Derive these flags from actual filesystem evidence or clearly mark them as unsupported/not-detected rather than always `true`.
- [ ] **S3-M201** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:14` - Escaping is incomplete: only `\` and `"` are escaped, and CR/LF are silently *deleted* rather than escaped (which merges lines), while TAB and other control characters (e.g. \x01) pass through verbatim, producing invalid JSON. Since `$PROFILE`/`$RUNTIME` are caller-supplied, escape all control characters (or reject values containing them) instead of dropping them.
- [ ] **S3-M202** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:26` - `find ... 2>/dev/null | wc -l | tr -d ' '` masks failures: the pipeline status is `tr`'s, so `set -e` never fires and an unreadable/permission-denied skills directory silently reports `skills: 0`, which consumers read as "no skills installed". Capture `find` output separately and fail (or warn) on a non-zero status.
- [ ] **S3-M203** (medium/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:37` - The INT/TERM trap only deletes the temp file; in POSIX sh the handler returns and execution resumes, so an interrupted run can still fall through to `mv` (which then fails, or, if the file was recreated, publishes a truncated/partial state). Make the signal handlers terminate the script. Note the EXIT trap must stay separate from the INT/TERM ones to avoid re-entrancy.
- [x] **S3-L1** (low/maintainability) `.codex/agents/evaluator.toml:13` - The five criterion maxima (25/20/15/20/20) and the rubric path are duplicated from `plugins/hackathonkit/skills/judge/references/rubric.md`. If the rubric weights or its location change, this prompt silently enforces stale maxima and the validation step checks against the wrong bounds. Treat the rubric as the single source of truth (e.g. "scores within the maxima defined in `plugins/hackathonkit/skills/judge/references/rubric.md`") rather than restating the numbers, or assert the rubric version matches before emitting.
- [x] **S3-L2** (low/performance) `.codex/agents/researcher.toml:3` - Residual of backlog item L1 (marked fixed): the bounding intent lives only in a TOML comment, which is not part of the model's context, and `model` is still unpinned. The only bound the agent actually sees is "3-6 questions, one isolated pass per question". Move the stop condition into `developer_instructions` (e.g. stop after the scoped questions, no extra passes) so the cost/latency claim is enforced by the prompt and not just asserted here.
- [x] **S3-L3** (low/maintainability) `.codex/agents/reviewer.toml:3` - Only `model_reasoning_effort` is set; no `model` (or provider) identifier is pinned (the same gap exists in `.codex/agents/evaluator.toml`). Review/judge output then depends on an implicit default that can change between runs/environments, making results non-reproducible and judge totals non-comparable. Pin the model explicitly in both agent configs.
- [x] **S3-L4** (low/maintainability) `.codex/agents/reviewer.toml:14` - The claim that this agent is read-only does not follow from the sandbox for command execution — read-only still permits read commands and builds/tests that write only to blocked paths will fail non-deterministically. Stating "You are read-only: do not run tests or builds" merges the sandbox capability with a behavioral instruction, which can mislead the agent about what is physically prevented. Keep the guarantee phrased purely as an instruction (as the file header comment already does) so the two are not conflated.
- [x] **S3-L5** (low/maintainability) `.codex/agents/reviewer.toml:17` - The output contract (severity ranking, file:line, repro, smallest fix, unverified-claims list) is prose-only with no schema or validator, so compliance is unenforceable and unverifiable — the same properties this agent is told to demand of others. Consider backing it with a machine-checkable output schema/validator, or at minimum note it as best-effort guidance so downstream consumers don't assume structural guarantees.
- [x] **S3-L6** (low/documentation) `.codex/config.toml:13` - The comment says to switch `web_search` to "live" when `$theme-research` must verify external facts, but the committed value is `"disabled"` while `persistent_instructions` below require that nothing be asserted without a tool result or file behind it. With search disabled there is no tool that can confirm external facts, so research skills can silently degrade into unverifiable claims. Make the contract explicit, e.g. add to the comment "when disabled, external claims must be reported as unverified" (the offline-safe default from H2 itself is fine).
- [x] **S3-L7** (low/documentation) `.codex/config.toml:16` - The sibling agent files pin `https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-schema.json` (see `#:schema` in `.codex/agents/*.toml`), and M7 already showed that an undocumented key here is silently ignored. `persistent_instructions` appears nowhere else in the repo and is not referenced by any doc or test, so if the schema does not recognize it the entire evidence discipline (call `system.context`, never claim a result without evidence) never reaches the model. Confirm the key name against the schema and, if it is unsupported, use the documented `model_instructions_file`/AGENTS.md mechanism instead.
- [x] **S3-L8** (low/bug) `.dockerignore:5` - The `.env*` glob excludes every env file, but `!.env.example` only re-includes the root template. Any env template added in a subdirectory (e.g. `configs/.env.example`) or with an alternative suffix (`.env.example.local`) stays silently excluded, so a build/doc step that reads it would fail. If templates may live outside the repo root, re-include them by depth.
- [x] **S3-L9** (low/maintainability) `.dockerignore:13` - `!README.md` and `!**/README.md` overlap: the second re-includes README files in *every* subdirectory, not just the root, partially undoing `**/*.md`. Since the Dockerfile only compiles Go sources (no markdown is `go:embed`-ed), these negations add ambiguity without benefit — keep only `!README.md` if the root file is required, or drop both.
- [x] **S3-L10** (low/maintainability) `.github/workflows/ci.yml:34` - `--json` writes the rehearsal report to stdout and `> /dev/null` discards it, so when the rehearsal fails the per-step JSON diagnostics are lost from the CI log (the on-disk report under `evals/` is on the ephemeral runner). Capture the output or drop `--json` so failures are debuggable, e.g. `go run ./cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse --json > rehearsal.json` and upload it, or simply remove the redirect.
- [x] **S3-L11** (low/security) `.gitignore:9` - The `!.env.example` negation is unanchored, so it re-includes a file named `.env.example` at *any* depth (e.g. a nested component's `.env.example` that could carry real credentials), and its correctness depends on being ordered after `.env.*` without any later re-ignore. Anchor it to the repo root to make the intent explicit and depth-independent.
- [x] **S3-L12** (low/maintainability) `.gitignore:10` - Standard generated/OS artifacts are not covered by either ignore file: `node_modules/`, `__pycache__/`, `*.pyc`, coverage output (`coverage/`, `htmlcov/`, `.coverage`), virtualenvs (`.venv/`), `tmp/`, root-level `*.log` (only `logs/` is ignored), and editor/OS files (`.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`, `*.swp`, `.idea/`, `.vscode/`). These can still be committed, bloating the repo and leaking local paths, and because the Dockerfile uses `COPY . .` any of them present in a working tree also bloat the build context and can leak local files into the image. Add them explicitly to both `.gitignore` and `.dockerignore`.
- [x] **S3-L13** (low/maintainability) `.gitignore:34` - `data/judge-bundles/` and the standalone `judge-bundle/` are ignored separately, which is confusing and ambiguous about where judge bundles actually live. Also, enumerating `data/` subdirectories one-by-one is fragile: a newly generated `data/<new>/` output directory will be silently tracked and may commit large generated content. Consider consolidating (e.g. ignore a single parent or add a convention note) so new generated dirs are covered.
- [x] **S3-L14** (low/maintainability) `Dockerfile:10` - Hardcoded 1000:1000 collides with the first regular host user on most Linux installs, and the `chown -R` of `/workspace` only helps when the bind-mounted host directories are owned by exactly these IDs — otherwise the container user cannot write to configs/data/logs/evals. Additionally, `adduser -u` aborts the whole build if the requested UID already exists in the base image. This looks intentional (README documents HACKATHONKIT_UID/HACKATHONKIT_GID), but consider documenting the requirement next to the ARGs or failing early with a clearer message so users know to align host ownership.
- [ ] **S3-L15** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/main.go:60` - The server sets only `ReadHeaderTimeout`; there is no `WriteTimeout`/`IdleTimeout` and no graceful `Shutdown`. A slow-reading client can hold a connection (and its handler goroutine) indefinitely, and there is no drain on shutdown. Add write/idle timeouts and shut down via `server.Shutdown(ctx)` on SIGINT/SIGTERM.
- [ ] **S3-L16** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/main.go:240` - Check-then-act race: the plan is read (Latest/Load), `body.Revision` is compared, and the approval is written in a separate step. Two concurrent decision requests carrying the same revision can both pass this comparison and the second `writeApproval` silently overwrites the first, producing a lost update / conflicting approval. Serialize decisions (e.g. an exclusive lock or an `O_EXCL`/compare-and-swap on the approval file keyed by slug+revision) so the revision check and write are atomic.
- [ ] **S3-L17** (low/security) `cmd/demo-api/main.go:441` - When HACKATHONKIT_DEMO_TOKEN is unset, `authorizeMutation` degrades to trusting the client-supplied `Origin` header. Any non-browser local process can trivially send `Origin: http://localhost`, and any page served from another loopback origin (e.g. a different local dev server on `http://localhost:<port>`) also satisfies the check. The comment acknowledges this is CSRF-only protection, but the 401 message ("set HACKATHONKIT_DEMO_TOKEN or send a loopback Origin header") advertises an authorization boundary that does not exist. Consider requiring a token for mutations, or validating the request `Host`/same-origin in addition to `Origin`, and document explicitly that token-less mode only mitigates CSRF.
- [ ] **S3-L18** (low/maintainability) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:59` - `esc()` is dead code — all rendering paths use `textContent`, so this helper is never referenced. Remove it to avoid confusion (and to prevent a future caller from assuming HTML interpolation is the intended render path).
- [ ] **S3-L19** (low/maintainability) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:89` - A click on a row calls `loadSessions()` to refresh the highlight, but if a poll is already in flight the early return silently drops this call, so the selection highlight lags until the next tick. Consider updating the row class synchronously in the click handler (or queue a refresh) rather than relying on `loadSessions`.
- [ ] **S3-L20** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/control.html:97` - Inconsistent risk value handling: the cell text coerces with `Number(s.risk_score) || 0` while `riskClass` uses the raw value. A null/undefined/non-numeric score renders as `0.0` and falls into `risk-low`, silently misrepresenting an unknown severity as benign. Normalize the score once (and fall back to a neutral class/label such as `n/a`) before both the display and the class computation.
- [ ] **S3-L21** (low/maintainability) `cmd/demo-api/static/fly-viz.html:269` - Per-frame exponential decay is frame-rate dependent: at 144 Hz the dopamine flash fades ~2.4x faster than at 60 Hz, and the factor can never reach exactly 0. Use a delta-based decay (e.g. `intensity *= Math.exp(-k * delta)`) or a time-based lerp toward 0 so the effect is consistent across refresh rates.
- [ ] **S3-L22** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:161` - The empty-events early return clears the four panels but not `#fly-meta` (only written at the end of the function) and does not reset `lastFlyTrace`. After a log rotation/truncation the meta line keeps showing the previous route/encoder/score, and when the same `trace_id` reappears the `isNew` check is false so the fly animation is skipped. Clear `fly-meta` here and reset `lastFlyTrace = null`.
- [ ] **S3-L23** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:256` - The in-flight guard has no timeout or abort: `fetch` has no default timeout, so a single hung request keeps `refreshing === true` forever (the `finally` only runs when the promise settles) and silently disables all subsequent polling — the dashboard freezes on stale data with no indication. Add an `AbortController` timeout to `j()` (or reset the flag defensively) so polling always recovers.
- [ ] **S3-L24** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:265` - The render functions assume a fixed response shape (`calls.value.calls`, `inputs.value.files`, `fly.value.events`) without any guard. Because `j()` only rejects on a non-2xx status, a 200 response with an unexpected/missing key (proxy rewrite, partial backend error, future API change) makes these lines throw a TypeError, which aborts the remaining panel renders for that tick and is then reported as `offline: …`. Guard the payloads, e.g. `renderCalls(calls.value.calls || [])`, `renderFiles('inputs', inputs.value.files || [])`, and `renderFly(fly.value.events || [], …)`.
- [ ] **S3-L25** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/index.html:270` - `Promise.allSettled` never rejects, so this `catch` can only be reached by a synchronous throw from one of the render helpers — in that case the status is labelled `offline: …` even though the server is reachable, which is misleading. The rejected panels themselves are silently dropped and the status still ends up as `updated <time>`, so a persistently failing endpoint is invisible in the UI. Consider logging/collecting the `rejected` reasons per panel (and only using the `offline` wording for genuine network failures).
- [ ] **S3-L26** (low/bug) `cmd/demo-api/static/plan.html:59` - The re-entrancy guard silently drops refreshes: the `load()` invoked after a successful decision can return immediately if a poll is already in flight, so the table/approval banner stays stale (and buttons are re-enabled) until the next 5s tick. Consider recording a pending refresh and running it in `finally` instead of returning, or awaiting the in-flight promise.
- [ ] **S3-L27** (low/maintainability) `cmd/fly-bench/main.go:268` - The evidence filename is derived solely from the commit SHA, so re-running the benchmark at the same commit silently replaces the previous report (including its `generated_at` and latency numbers) — the last run wins and earlier evidence is unrecoverable. When `quality.GitSHA` cannot resolve HEAD it returns the literal `"unknown"`, so every non-git/`unknown` run anywhere also collides on `unknown.json`. The write is also not durable: the temp file is renamed without an `fsync`, so a crash can lose the report. Consider appending a timestamp/run id to the filename (or refusing to overwrite) and syncing the file before the rename.
- [ ] **S3-L28** (low/bug) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:49` - `--epochs` is not validated. A value <= 0 skips the training loop entirely yet still saves the (untrained, all-zero readout) brain over any existing `data/index/fly-brain.json` and prints a success message claiming it "trained" the examples — silent model corruption. Reject non-positive values early.
- [ ] **S3-L29** (low/bug) `cmd/fly-train/main.go:59` - `--model` is only consulted when `--dataset` is empty, so `--model knn` combined with `--dataset` (or any unrecognized `--model` value) silently takes the MB training path and overwrites `data/index/fly-brain.json`, contradicting the documented flag semantics. Validate the value and reject the conflicting combination.
- [ ] **S3-L30** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:231` - Two weaknesses in the process-identity check: (1) when `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` cannot be read (non-Linux platforms, /proc not mounted, restricted containers) the function returns `true`, accepting any process that merely passes the signal-0 probe, so a recycled PID can be mistaken for the daemon and get SIGTERM from `daemonStop`; (2) the hardcoded `"hackathonkit-ctl"` string must appear in the cmdline, so if the binary is installed/renamed (e.g. built as `bin/hackathonkit`) `readPID` never recognizes the running daemon — `daemonStatus` reports "stopped" and `daemonStart` launches a duplicate (and then times out). Consider a marker that is independent of the file name (e.g. an explicit `--daemon-marker` argument, or comparing against the basename of `os.Executable()`), and fail closed (return false) when identity cannot be verified.
- [ ] **S3-L31** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/daemon.go:269` - When only one of the two errors is non-nil, the `errors` field renders the other as the literal `<nil>` (e.g. `"boom <nil>"` / `"<nil> boom"`), which is confusing for API consumers. Join only the non-nil errors.
- [ ] **S3-L32** (low/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/detach_unix.go:1` - Build constraint is broader than the file's stated POSIX intent (file name `detach_unix.go`, comment says "on POSIX systems"), but `!windows` also selects non-POSIX `!windows` targets (plan9, js/wasip1), where `syscall.SysProcAttr` is a different struct without a `Setsid` field, so this file would not compile there. The sibling `detach_windows.go` is constrained with `//go:build windows`, so the pairing is asymmetric. If only POSIX + Windows are supported, tighten this to `//go:build unix` (and add a no-op stub for other targets so `detachSysProcAttr` stays defined); if the current breadth is intentional, document that plan9/js/wasip1 are unsupported.
- [ ] **S3-L33** (low/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/detach_windows.go:10` - The magic literal `0x00000008` duplicates Windows' `DETACHED_PROCESS` creation flag. The standard `syscall` package does not export this constant (only `golang.org/x/sys/windows` does, and that module is currently an indirect dependency), so a future edit could silently typo this into an unrelated flag (e.g. `CREATE_SUSPENDED` is `0x00000004`). Extract it into a documented local constant so the intent and its source are explicit.
- [ ] **S3-L34** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:310` - `slugify` returns the sentinel "session" both for an empty/omitted `--slug` and for a value that normalizes to "session" (e.g. `--slug session`, `--slug Session`, `--slug "session!"`). In the latter case the user-supplied slug is silently overwritten with a task-derived name, so two distinct runs can collide on the same worktree path (`data/worktrees/session`). Distinguish the absent-slug case (check `strings.TrimSpace(*slug) == ""`) from a user value that merely normalizes to the sentinel.
- [ ] **S3-L35** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/main.go:354` - On a successful run `runErr` is a nil `error`, so `fmt.Sprint(runErr)` evaluates to the string "<nil>" and the stored payload always contains `"error":"<nil>"`. Only include the error field when `runErr != nil` (or store `nil` directly instead of stringifying) to avoid polluting the event data.
- [ ] **S3-L36** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-ctl/tui.go:87` - Every returned `tea.Cmd` runs in its own goroutine, so the 2s tick, keypress loads and manual refresh can have several loads in flight at once. Because responses are applied unconditionally here, a slow earlier load can land after a newer one and reset `m.selected`/`m.events` to a stale selection, snapping the cursor back. Consider tagging each load with a monotonically increasing sequence number and ignoring responses older than the last applied one.
- [ ] **S3-L37** (low/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:373` - This helper writes diagnostics straight to the process-wide `os.Stderr` even though it is only reached from `printSessionContext`/`run`, which take an injected writer for output and testability. The warning bypasses the caller's stream (and any buffer used in tests). Consider passing the diagnostic writer in (or having `countFiles` return the error for the caller to report via its `out`/`stderr` writer).
- [ ] **S3-L38** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:112` - A walk failure (e.g. an unreadable `data/artifacts` subdirectory) is silently dropped, so the report shows zero artifacts and is indistinguishable from a run that legitimately produced none. Append the error to `rep.Notes` (or fail the run) when `err != nil` instead of ignoring it.
- [ ] **S3-L39** (low/maintainability) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:143` - Wrapping with `%v` discards the error identity, so a `context.DeadlineExceeded` (the 5-minute clone budget) or a `*exec.ExitError` cannot be distinguished from an arbitrary failure by any caller. Use `%w` (same applies to the `go build` wrap in `buildMCP`) if the failure kind is ever to be inspected or reported to the user.
- [ ] **S3-L40** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:365` - The timeout is re-armed on every loop iteration, so each skipped/stale/notification frame restarts the full 60s budget: a server that keeps emitting unmatched lines can keep this read loop alive indefinitely, and every skipped frame also leaves an unreferenced `time.After` timer behind until it fires. Compute the request deadline once and use the remaining budget per iteration.
- [ ] **S3-L41** (low/bug) `cmd/hackathonkit-rehearse/main.go:511` - `r.series` is produced by the earlier `timeseries.analyze` step but is consumed here without checking that the step succeeded. Because the step chain never actually short-circuits (see the `_ = ok` finding), a failed `timeseries.analyze` leaves `r.series` nil and this call still runs with an empty `points` array; if the server accepts it, the step reports `OK` with a bogus artifact instead of surfacing the missing prerequisite. Guard on `len(r.series) > 0` before issuing the chart request.
- [x] **S3-L42** (low/maintainability) `configs/config.yaml:17` - The key `fly_threshold` is an unusual term for a "recommendation confidence floor" and is plausibly a typo (e.g. `flow_threshold`/`fallback_threshold`). It is currently spelled consistently with the Go yaml tags (`internal/config/config.go:35,79`) and the tests/README/docs, so routing still applies it — no functional break. Please confirm the name is intentional; if it is renamed, the Go struct tags, tests, README and docs must be updated in lockstep.
- [x] **S3-L43** (low/security) `docker-compose.yml:71` - `quality` is the only service without a non-root `user:` override and it bind-mounts the entire repository read-write. The containerized tooling therefore runs as root with full access to `.env` (which holds `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`) and `.git`, and can mutate any host file under the repo. Consider aligning it with the other services (`user: "${HACKATHONKIT_UID:-1000}:${HACKATHONKIT_GID:-1000}"`) and moving the Go caches off `/root` (e.g. `/home/hackathonkit/...`) accordingly, or narrowing the mount scope.
- [x] **S3-L44** (low/maintainability) `docker-compose.yml:76` - No long-running service defines a restart policy, so Compose defaults to `no`. A crash or OOM of `postgres`/`minio` leaves the stack permanently degraded: `hackathonkit-mcp` (which only gates on `service_healthy`/`service_started` at start time) will keep failing its DB/object-store calls with no automatic recovery, requiring a manual `docker compose up`. Add `restart: unless-stopped` to the stateful services (`postgres`, `redis`, `minio`).
- [ ] **S3-L45** (low/security) `internal/atomicfile/atomicfile.go:29` - The parent directory is always created with `0o755`, even when `mode` is `ModeState` (0o600). For least-privilege state files this leaves the containing directory world-traversable/readable, exposing state file names and directory layout to other local users and partially undermining the `ModeState` guarantee documented above. Consider creating the directory with `0o700` when `mode == ModeState` (existing directories are left untouched by `MkdirAll`, so this only affects newly created state dirs).
- [ ] **S3-L46** (low/bug) `internal/config/config.go:236` - Range-guard is inconsistent with `mergeFile`: `mergeFile` refuses a non-positive `default_timeout_sec`, but `mergeNumeric` (called right after it on the same YAML) unconditionally applies the pointer value, so a negative value wins. The same applies to `fly_threshold` and `top_k` below: `internal/routing/fly/router.go` defaults `topK` but uses `settings.FlyThreshold` verbatim, so a negative threshold silently makes every query route to fly with no error/warning. Explicit zero is intentional (see the merge-numeric tests), so only reject negatives rather than reusing `> 0`.
- [ ] **S3-L47** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/config.go:350` - Duplicate error logging: the `if readErr != nil && !os.IsNotExist(readErr)` block at the top of `Routing()` already logs exactly this condition, and this `else if` (whose condition is already implied by being the `else` of `readErr == nil`) prints the same error a second time with different wording. Every non-NotExist read failure therefore emits two conflicting log lines; drop one of the two.
- [ ] **S3-L48** (low/maintainability) `internal/config/config.go:419` - Duplicate/conflicting logging on parse failure: the `else` logs the error with its cause, then the unconditional statement logs the same failure again without the cause. The message is also inaccurate — after this point the function returns `m.evaluationCache` (last known settings) whenever `evaluationInit` is true, not the defaults. Remove the unconditional log and keep a single message that matches the actual fallback; note that read errors (non-NotExist `readErr`) are currently not logged at all in this function.
- [ ] **S3-L49** (low/bug) `internal/config/dotenv.go:38` - The `LookupEnv` guard is re-evaluated for every line, so once this file itself has set a key, a later line redefining that same key is skipped: duplicate keys inside `.env` resolve to the FIRST occurrence. Common dotenv implementations (godotenv, JS dotenv, `sh` sourcing a file) use last-wins, so a file containing `FOO=default` followed by `FOO=override` silently keeps `default`.
