If the intent is only "pre-existing process env wins", consider tracking the keys written by this load in a local set so in-file duplicates stay last-wins (or document the first-wins behavior explicitly).
- [x] **S3-L50** (low/bug) `internal/control/handoff.go:73` - Fields interpolated here (`Task`, `Worktree`, `RiskReasons`, learning `Trigger`/`Insight`) come from ingested records and are written raw into the markdown block. An embedded newline (or line starting with `- `) breaks the bullet structure the resuming agent is expected to follow, and embedded backticks can alter markup. Consider normalizing control characters/newlines (e.g. `strings.ReplaceAll(v, "\n", " ")`) before writing these values.
- [x] **S3-L51** (low/bug) `internal/control/ingest.go:51` - `res.Files` counts decision files, but `res.Events` is only incremented from the JSONL loop and never includes the `eval_decision` events appended by `ingestDecisions`. Callers reading `IngestResult.Events` as the total ingested event count will get an inconsistent (under-counted) value. Either add the decision event count to `res.Events` (e.g. have `ingestDecisions` return it) or document the field's scope.
- [x] **S3-L52** (low/performance) `internal/control/ingest.go:97` - `io.ReadAll` buffers the entire unprocessed tail in memory; the comment above notes these logs grow unbounded, and after a rotation reset (`offset = 0`) this reads the whole file. A large backlog or a big rotated/recreated file can cause a significant memory spike or OOM. Prefer a streaming reader (bufio.Reader/Scanner with an explicit max line size, or json.Decoder over the seeked section) and process line by line.
- [x] **S3-L53** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/ingest.go:120` - Malformed lines permanently advance the cursor with no counter, log, or metric, so corrupted records are dropped irreversibly without any visibility. The skip is documented, but consider emitting a warning (with source name and byte offset) or returning a skipped count so silent data loss can be diagnosed.
- [x] **S3-L54** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/risk.go:96` - Hard-coded 10000 limits on Sessions/Events/EventsBetween silently truncate results (`... ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 10000`). For a busy session with more than 10000 events, the oldest events are dropped and risk is under-counted with no signal to the caller; the same applies when more than 10000 sessions exist. Consider paginating, aggregating counts in SQL, or at least surfacing that a limit was hit.
- [x] **S3-L55** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/store.go:166` - `parseTime` silently returns the zero time (`year 1`) for any unparseable value, and it accepts variable-width layouts (`RFC3339Nano`, `RFC3339`) that do not match the fixed-width `timeLayout` used for writes. Because `EventsBetween`, `latestGlobalTS` and the `Sessions`/`sessions` ordering compare the stored `ts`/`updated_at` TEXT lexicographically under the fixed-width assumption, a legacy/corrupted row written in a different layout can sort outside the intended window (and a session whose `created_at` fails to parse silently widens the risk-attribution window instead of erroring). The failure is indistinguishable from a legitimate zero. Consider returning an error for unparseable non-empty input, or normalizing on write and documenting that mixed-layout rows are unsupported.
- [x] **S3-L56** (low/maintainability) `internal/control/store.go:405` - `COALESCE` only protects against a NULL `updated_at`; for a cursor that was never written the query matches no row and `QueryRow.Scan` returns `sql.ErrNoRows`, not the zero time a caller would expect. This is inconsistent with the sibling `Cursor`, which explicitly maps `ErrNoRows` to `0`. The current caller in `ingest.go` only reaches this path when `offset > 0` (so a row exists), but any other/future caller will get a spurious error instead of the zero time. Return the zero time (with nil error) for a missing row, or document that callers must handle `ErrNoRows`.
- [ ] **S3-L57** (low/bug) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:185` - The deduction check rejects NaN/negative points but not `+Inf`, and the message says "negative points" for NaN. A `+Inf` deduction passes validation, is summed in `groupDeductions` (round2(+Inf) = +Inf) and then makes `json.Marshal` of the aggregate fail. Reject non-finite values and use an accurate message.
- [ ] **S3-L58** (low/bug) `internal/evaluation/evaluation.go:605` - `RankEntry.CriterionMeans` aliases the input `Aggregate.CriterionMeans` map (and `ApplyCommission` stores `&commission`, sharing the caller's `Votes` map). The returned `Ranking` therefore mutates if the caller later touches those maps, which breaks the "deterministic result" contract. Copy the maps when building the entries/ranking.
- [ ] **S3-L59** (low/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:148` - The inter-process file lock is acquired *before* the in-process mutex. Two goroutines calling `Record` concurrently therefore contend on the `.lock` file itself: while the winner is inside the load/save critical section the loser retries on disk, and if the save is slow (large file/slow disk) the loser exhausts all 50 attempts and returns "learning store is locked by another process" even though `s.mu` would have serialized it correctly. Take `s.mu` first so only one goroutine in the process ever touches the lock file, and a lock-file failure then unambiguously means another *process* holds it.
- [ ] **S3-L60** (low/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:168` - `scope` is normalized and validated at the top of `Record` (implying callers may request `project` or `global`), but the reinforcement branch never applies it — only the creation branch does. A repeated trigger+insight pair recorded with `Scope: "global"` silently keeps its stored scope, which contradicts the validation and pushes callers towards `Promote` for a change they believe they already requested. Either honor the requested scope here or document/reject non-default scope for existing entries.
- [ ] **S3-L61** (low/bug) `internal/learning/learning.go:245` - Same silent-swallow pattern as in `Record`: an embedder failure makes every match fall back to keyword overlap with no signal to the caller, so a total embedding outage is indistinguishable from a cold store whose entries were recorded without vectors. Consider returning or logging the error (or a degradation flag) rather than ignoring it.
- [ ] **S3-L62** (low/maintainability) `internal/mcp/contracts.go:31` - `Status` is a bare string populated with untyped literals ("ok"/"partial"/"error") and there is no constant or validation for it. A typo or an unexpected value produced by deserialization passes silently and any downstream branching on `status` quietly falls through. Consider exporting typed constants (e.g. `StatusOK`, `StatusPartial`, `StatusError`) and using them here to make the allowed set explicit and greppable.
- [ ] **S3-L63** (low/maintainability) `internal/mcp/contracts.go:39` - `Fail` encodes the error message into the generic `Data any` field under the magic key "error", so consumers must hard-code that string and lose compile-time guarantees; it is also indistinguishable in shape from a successful `OK`/`Partial` payload. If the contract requires a stable error shape, consider a typed error field on `Envelope` (or a dedicated error struct) so callers get a documented, type-checked accessor.
- [ ] **S3-L64** (low/maintainability) `internal/mcp/registry.go:23` - `Registry` holds a plain `map` plus an `order` slice with no synchronization, yet the type is exported and also reachable through `Server.Registry()`. Current call sites are safe (all `reg.Register(...)` calls happen in `tools.RegisterAll` before `NewServer`, and `ServeStreams` handles requests serially), so this is not an active race today. However, any later dynamic registration (hot-reload, per-session tools, or a concurrent `Serve` caller) would race on `r.tools`/`r.order` with `Get`/`List`/`Names`. Either document the type as "not safe for concurrent use; register all tools before serving" or guard the state with a `sync.RWMutex` to make the invariant explicit.
- [ ] **S3-L65** (low/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:29` - The in-band sentinel can collide with real input: `bytes.TrimSpace` does not strip a NUL byte, so a legitimate frame consisting of a single `0x00` (plus newline) is equal to `oversizeFrame` and is answered with "request exceeds the maximum frame size" instead of a parse error. Any single-byte sentinel is forgeable this way; prefer a typed marker (e.g. a small struct on the channel carrying an `oversize bool`) or a separate channel so a real frame can never be mistaken for the marker.
- [ ] **S3-L66** (low/bug) `internal/mcp/server.go:225` - Residual of L51: the upper-bound guard does not fully cover the overflow. `time.Duration(s.opts.CallTimeoutSec) * time.Second` wraps for large values; when it wraps to a small *positive* multiple of 512 ns (e.g. `CallTimeoutSec = 18446744074` yields ~290 ms), `timeout <= 0` is false and `timeout <= 24h` is true, so the guard is bypassed and every tool call times out nearly immediately instead of being clamped. Additionally, a legitimately large configured value (`> 24h`) silently collapses to 30 s rather than being clamped to the supported maximum. Bound the seconds value before the multiplication.
- [ ] **S3-L67** (low/security) `internal/mcp/server.go:242` - Raw `err.Error()` values (JSON parse failures, tool handler errors and recovered panic values, see also the parse-error response above) are embedded in the RPC error message and in the `content` returned for a failed tool call. For the local stdio capability layer the client is the trusted agent, so impact is limited today, but if this stream is ever exposed beyond the local process it leaks internal paths/operation details across the trust boundary. Consider returning a generic message to the client and keeping the detail in the local log.
- [ ] **S3-L68** (low/maintainability) `internal/metadata/metadata.go:119` - The additive-migration list only covers `artifacts` and `fly_predictions`, while the comment above promises that column additions are expressed as `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` so existing deployments pick them up. `routing_runs` and `eval_runs` have no such statements, so any column added to those tables later (or present only in the `CREATE TABLE` body) is silently never applied to pre-existing tables and the corresponding `INSERT` will fail at runtime. Please either add the missing migrations or narrow the comment to state explicitly that only these two tables are migrated.
- [ ] **S3-L69** (low/bug) `internal/metadata/metadata.go:156` - `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is not atomic against a concurrent creator: if two processes sharing the database (e.g. `demo-api` and `hackathonkit-mcp`) run this startup DDL simultaneously, Postgres can still fail with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "pg_type_typname_nsp_index"` / `relation already exists`. Here that error is fatal to `OpenPostgres`, so the process silently falls back to `Disabled` and loses all metadata recording for its lifetime. Consider taking a `pg_advisory_lock` (or retrying once on the catalog race) around the schema/migrations DDL. This is the unfixed remainder of backlog item L53, which is marked done.
- [ ] **S3-L70** (low/bug) `internal/objectstore/minio.go:64` - Two error-reporting problems in the bucket-creation race branch: (1) any `MakeBucket` failure is converted into a successful `Open` as soon as the follow-up probe reports the bucket exists, so genuine errors (e.g. the bucket is owned by another account and merely visible) are hidden; (2) when the probe itself fails (`existsErr != nil`), the probe error is dropped and the stale `MakeBucket` error is returned, masking the real cause. Prefer matching the specific benign error (minio's `BucketAlreadyOwnedByYou`/`BucketAlreadyExists`) and otherwise returning both causes.
- [ ] **S3-L71** (low/performance) `internal/observability/obs.go:51` - `new(atomic.Int64)` is evaluated on every `Add`/`Inc` call and passed as an interface to `LoadOrStore`, so it escapes to the heap even when the counter already exists. `Inc`/`Add` sit on hot paths (per tool call: `s.opts.Metrics.Add("tool_duration_ms_total", ...)`), so this is a needless allocation per increment. Fast-path the existing counter and only allocate on a miss.
- [ ] **S3-L72** (low/bug) `internal/observability/obs.go:170` - When the backward read stops before offset 0 (the common case for any file larger than one chunk / with more than `limit` lines), the first entry in `buf` is a *partial* record, and the code relies solely on `json.Valid` to drop it. A truncated record whose suffix happens to be syntactically valid JSON (e.g. a numeric/embedded-fragment tail) would be returned as a bogus record. Track the read offset and explicitly discard the first line whenever `offset > 0` before validating the rest.
- [ ] **S3-L73** (low/bug) `internal/openai/client.go:169` - The fallback path assumes the provider returned vectors in input order but, unlike the indexed path below, it never verifies that each `d.Embedding` is actually present. A provider returning `{"data":[{"embedding":null}, ...]}` with a matching count silently yields `nil` vectors for those inputs and `nil` error, whereas the indexed path explicitly rejects the same condition via the missing-vector check. Add the same guard here for consistency.
- [ ] **S3-L74** (low/bug) `internal/openai/client.go:203` - The size guard is silently skipped whenever `f.Stat()` fails (`serr == nil &&`), so an oversized file is copied verbatim into the in-memory `bytes.Buffer`, defeating the `maxAudioBytes` protection exactly when the file metadata is unavailable. `f.Stat()` on a just-opened file rarely fails, but when it does the failure should be surfaced rather than treated as 'size OK'.
- [ ] **S3-L75** (low/maintainability) `internal/openai/client.go:266` - Extracting the payload with `strings.Index(raw, "{")` .. `strings.LastIndex(raw, "}")` is fragile: a brace in surrounding prose, or a second JSON object after the payload (e.g. an example echoed by the model), makes the sliced substring invalid JSON and the whole classification fails with a parse error instead of degrading gracefully. A `json.Decoder` scan for the first complete object (or an explicit error message that includes the raw model text) is more robust and easier to debug.
- [ ] **S3-L76** (low/bug) `internal/plans/plans.go:85` - The 60-rune truncation is lossy: two distinct plan names that agree for the first 60 runes collapse to the same file stem, so `Save` will overwrite an unrelated plan's file and `Load`/`Latest` return whichever was written last. This is the only place the stem is delimited, so nothing down the line can detect the collision. Consider disambiguating the truncated stem with a short fingerprint of the original value (e.g. `out = trimmed + "-" + shortHash(value)`) or returning an error on collision at save time.
- [ ] **S3-L77** (low/bug) `internal/plans/plans.go:133` - The "latest" plan is selected by filesystem `ModTime`, while `Save` persists the semantic timestamp in `Plan.UpdatedAt`. The two can disagree — e.g. after a backup/restore or bulk copy all files share one mtime — and because the comparison is strict `Before`, equal mtimes are resolved by `os.ReadDir`'s lexical filename order rather than by `UpdatedAt`, so a plan that is not the most recently updated can win (deterministically, but arbitrarily). Prefer comparing `plan.UpdatedAt`, falling back to `info.ModTime()` when it is zero. The same applies to the duplicate logic in `Latest`.
- [ ] **S3-L78** (low/bug) `internal/quality/proc_unix.go:20` - `killProcessGroup` can be invoked after the child has already been reaped. Its only caller is `cmd.Cancel` in `quality.go`, and `os/exec` calls `Cancel` from `watchCtx` when the context fires before `Wait` reads `ctxResult` — i.e. after `Process.Wait()` has reaped the PID but while `Wait` is still draining the pipes (exactly the window `WaitDelay = 10s` exists for). Signalling `-pid` after reaping means the PID may already have been recycled into an unrelated process group, which would then receive SIGKILL. Guard on `ProcessState` (set by `Wait` right after reaping, before the pipe drain) so the group kill only targets a live child.
- [ ] **S3-L79** (low/bug) `internal/quality/proc_windows.go:9` - Behavioural divergence from the POSIX build: `proc_unix.go` puts each gate command in its own process group and SIGKILLs the whole group, but on Windows only the direct child is terminated (`configureProcessGroup` is a documented no-op and `killProcessGroup` calls `os.Process.Kill`). `quality.go` runs commands such as `sh scripts/coverage-check.sh` and `go test ./...` that spawn compilers/test binaries; after a timeout these grandchildren survive, keep consuming CPU, and can hold files/ports/locks after the gate has already reported `timeout`. The limitation is acknowledged in the comment, so this is a residual rather than a new regression — if full-tree cleanup is required on Windows, start the child with `syscall.CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`/a Job Object and terminate the job here.
- [ ] **S3-L80** (low/bug) `internal/quality/quality.go:158` - `tail` slices on byte offsets, so the cut can land inside a multi-byte UTF-8 sequence, producing invalid UTF-8 in `Check.Output`. `encoding/json` then replaces the broken bytes with U+FFFD, so the JSON stays valid but the tail of the output is corrupted for diagnostics. Back the offset up to the next rune boundary.
- [ ] **S3-L81** (low/bug) `internal/resources/matrix.go:102` - `Plan` is an exported entry point that can receive a hand-built `Matrix` (as the tests already do). When a capability has no variants this `continue` silently drops the decision while `resolved.Capabilities` (line 100) still advertises that capability, so the returned `TrackPlan` and `[]Decision` disagree and `len(track.Capabilities) != len(decisions)`. Since `Validate` guarantees non-empty lists only for matrices loaded through `LoadMatrix`, either drop the capability from `resolved.Capabilities` or return an explicit error here so the output stays self-consistent for any caller.
- [ ] **S3-L82** (low/maintainability) `internal/resources/probe.go:39` - Residual L62 (marked fixed in docs/REVIEW-BACKLOG.md, but unchanged). `Live` is still set before the request is attempted, so the request-error and non-200 paths serialize `live: true` while `reachable: false` / an `error` is present. The caller (internal/tools/resources.go:296) copies `probe.Live` straight into the agent-facing result. Either set `Live = true` only after a successful 200, or document explicitly that `Live` means "a live probe was attempted" rather than "provider is healthy".
- [ ] **S3-L83** (low/maintainability) `internal/resources/resources.go:153` - `Profile` is passed by value, but `Claimed` is a shared map, so `profile.Claimed["codex_pro"] = ...` mutates the caller's map as a side effect of Save. Callers that retain the profile they passed in (or share it across goroutines) will observe an unexpected mutation/race. Clone `Claimed` into a local map before mutating.
- [ ] **S3-L84** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/embed.go:90` - This guard cannot distinguish "no cache entry yet" from "cached empty/failed result", because `embedCacheTask` is compared against its zero value `""`. On a fresh router `embedTask(ctx, "")` therefore returns `(nil, false)` without ever calling the embedder, while after any non-empty task has been embedded the same call does embed. Note also that failures are cached: `ok == false` stores `vec == nil`, and the `embedCacheVec == nil` clause then replays that negative result for the same task string (a transient provider error is never retried). Add an explicit `embedCacheSet bool` (or a sentinel/nullable task) instead of overloading the zero value, and make the intended negative-cache policy explicit.
- [ ] **S3-L85** (low/performance) `internal/routing/fly/embed.go:215` - A failed build is deliberately not cached (the leader clears `r.building` and leaves `r.centroids` nil), which means every subsequent sequential `Recommend` re-runs the full exemplar batch embed (one provider round-trip for ~55 texts). When both the primary and the hash fallback are unavailable, each request on the routing path pays that cost again with no backoff. Consider a short-lived failure memo / backoff so a down provider does not amplify load, while still allowing recovery.
- [ ] **S3-L86** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/embed.go:325` - On a zero-norm input this returns the caller-owned slice instead of a copy, so the router can retain aliased backing storage (`embedTask` stores this in `r.embedCacheVec`, and `buildCentroids` stores it in long-lived `centroids`). The `Embedder` interface does not promise a freshly allocated slice; an implementation that reuses a scratch buffer would then let a later `Embed` call mutate cached vectors (or race with a reader). Return a fresh copy (e.g. `make([]float64, len(v))`) in the `norm == 0` branch as well.
- [ ] **S3-L87** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/events.go:34` - `brainMu` is only released on the normal path, so any panic inside `brain.scores` leaves the process-wide mutex locked forever. `brain.scores` -> `activate` does an unbounded `make([]float64, b.KC)`; `loadBrain` only rejects `KC == 0`, so a corrupt/hostile brain JSON with a negative or absurdly large `kc` panics there, and every later caller that needs `brainMu` (`Dopamine`, `applyDopamine`, further `ActivationSnapshot`/`mbCategoryScores` calls) then deadlocks permanently. Note that the returned `scores` map and `code` slice are freshly allocated by `scores`/`activate`, so the lock does not need to cover the sampling loop — it can be scoped to just the scoring call via a closure with `defer`, matching the deferred-unlock style used elsewhere in the package.
- [ ] **S3-L88** (low/performance) `internal/routing/fly/hash_encoder.go:28` - `fnv.New64a()` is allocated for every token of every text; each call returns an interface value backed by a fresh heap object, so a large batch causes an allocation per token in a hot loop. The hasher is stateless across tokens once reset, so it can be created once outside the text loop and reused via `Reset()`.
- [ ] **S3-L89** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/hash_encoder.go:57` - `strings.Builder.Len()` counts bytes, not characters, so the single-character filter only works for ASCII. A one-letter token containing a non-ASCII rune (e.g. Cyrillic "я", 2 bytes) passes the `>= 2` check and is hashed, while the ASCII equivalent "a" is dropped — inconsistent behavior for exactly the multilingual corpus this package models. Count runes instead (`utf8.RuneCountInString`).
- [ ] **S3-L90** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/mb.go:206` - A category whose readout is shorter than the activation code is silently dropped from the logits (and therefore from the softmax and the ranking). loadBrain only validates brain.KC != 0 and len(brain.Readout) > 0, not the per-category weight length, so a brain file with mismatched lengths (e.g. trained at a different KC) silently loses MB signal for those categories with no log or error. Validate each readout length against KC when loading, or at least surface the skip.
- [ ] **S3-L91** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/memory.go:115` - Every `os.ReadFile` failure is converted into an empty memory, including transient or permission errors (EACCES/EIO) that are not "file absent". `AddFeedback` then persists the empty set plus the new example, silently destroying the existing feedback memory. Only `os.IsNotExist(err)` should be treated as "no memory yet"; other errors should be returned to the caller.
- [ ] **S3-L92** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/memory.go:123` - The quarantine rename error is discarded. If `os.Rename` fails (e.g. the directory is read-only or a `.corrupt` file already exists on a filesystem that rejects overwrite), an empty `memoryFile` is returned anyway; the caller `AddFeedback` then calls `saveMemory` and overwrites the still-corrupt file, so the recoverable data the comment intends to protect is lost. Surface the failure (return an error) or at least avoid the subsequent overwrite when quarantine did not succeed.
- [ ] **S3-L93** (low/bug) `internal/routing/fly/memory.go:203` - `applyDopamine` returns a bool indicating whether the feedback was actually applied (see internal/routing/fly/mb.go), but the result is discarded here and `AddFeedback` only returns `(size, nil)`. Callers therefore cannot distinguish "stored and learned" from "stored but no reward/punishment applied" (e.g. no brain configured) — internal/tools/system.go hardcodes `"learned": true`. Propagate the result (e.g. return it alongside the size) so the caller can report the real outcome.
- [ ] **S3-L94** (low/maintainability) `internal/routing/fly/router.go:208` - Categories produced by the dynamic encoders but absent from `categoryCapabilities` (reachable through the MBrain path, whose categories come from an arbitrary user-supplied training dataset, see cmd/fly-train) are handled inconsistently: with a non-empty `available` they are never selected as the route and contribute no capability recommendations, silently discarding a potentially strong signal; with an empty `available` `categoryAvailable` returns true, so such a category *can* become `Route` while `Recommendations` stays empty — a result shape no caller expects (the caller ignores `Route` when no recommendation is returned). Consider validating dynamic categories against `categoryCapabilities` (or logging/dropping them explicitly) so the behavior is uniform and the lost signal is visible.
- [ ] **S3-L95** (low/bug) `internal/skills/validate.go:94` - Only plain directories are collected: `DirEntry.IsDir()` is false for a symlink to a directory, so a symlinked skill is silently dropped and never validated, with no problem entry to explain it. Combined with `report.OK = report.Summary.Failed == 0`, a catalog that is empty (or contains only symlinked skills) reports `ok: true`, and the CLI at `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp/main.go:136` exits 0 — a silently emptied/relocated catalog would pass CI. Consider resolving entries with `os.Stat` and reporting skipped/unexpected entries (and treating a zero-skill catalog as a failure).
- [ ] **S3-L96** (low/security) `internal/skills/validate.go:179` - The escape guard is purely lexical: both regexes force a `references/` prefix, so `filepath.IsAbs(ref)` can never be true, and only a literal `..` after `filepath.Clean` is rejected. `os.Stat` then follows symlinks, so a committed symlink under the skill directory (or a path whose clean form stays inside `dir` while a component is a link) can resolve to a target outside the skill directory and still be reported as valid. Resolve the path and verify containment instead of relying on lexical cleaning.
- [ ] **S3-L97** (low/security) `internal/storage/store.go:69` - Residual of the M106 fix: when `EvalSymlinks(s.root)` fails (root not created yet, permission error on an ancestor, symlink loop), containment degrades to a purely lexical comparison that resolves no symlinks at all, including the components *above* `s.root`. A symlinked ancestor of the configured root (e.g. `s.root = /tmp/x/root` where `/tmp/x` is a symlink, or any attacker-writable parent) therefore passes the check and `MkdirAll`/`WriteFile` land at the resolved location instead of under the intended root. The guard should still account for symlinks in the path to the root: resolve the nearest existing ancestor of `s.root` and compare against its resolved form, rather than comparing raw `filepath.Abs` strings.
- [ ] **S3-L98** (low/bug) `internal/storage/store.go:129` - Every `os.Stat` failure is collapsed into "input file not found", so operational failures are misreported: a permission error (EACCES), a symlink loop (ELOOP) or an I/O error on the input tree is presented to callers as a missing input, which sends them down the wrong remediation path and hides a broken mount/permissions problem. Distinguish `os.IsNotExist` from other errors and wrap the rest with `%w`.
- [ ] **S3-L99** (low/bug) `internal/telegram/telegram.go:299` - `limit` is clamped defensively (1..100) but `timeoutSec` is not bounded at all. An out-of-range value is forwarded as-is in the `timeout` field, and `time.Duration(timeoutSec)*time.Second` can overflow int64 for large inputs, producing a negative duration: `context.WithTimeout` then hands an already-expired context to the HTTP call, so `getUpdates` fails immediately with `context deadline exceeded` instead of a clear validation error. Clamp (Telegram accepts a small long-poll window) and reject negatives before doing the duration arithmetic.
- [ ] **S3-L100** (low/bug) `internal/telegram/telegram.go:341` - `From` is modeled as a value struct, so when Telegram omits it (channel posts / anonymous admins, where `sender_chat` is sent instead) `update.Message.From.ID` is the zero value and `FromID` becomes the string `"0"`. Despite the `json:"from_id,omitempty"` tag, callers cannot distinguish "no sender" from user id 0. Only populate `FromID` when a sender is actually present.
- [ ] **S3-L101** (low/bug) `internal/tools/context.go:139` - Per-entry `Info`, `ReadFile` and `Rel` failures are all converted into `return nil`, so unreadable skill/asset files silently disappear from the report (only the outer WalkDir error is surfaced). For a budgeting tool whose callers rely on it as the source of token numbers, this is indistinguishable from 'nothing to load'. Track failures and surface them (at minimum in the envelope warning).
- [ ] **S3-L102** (low/bug) `internal/tools/context.go:269` - Only a key starting at column 0 matches and inline YAML comments are not stripped, so an indented `description:` is missed and `description: text # note` is measured including the comment. Both produce false negatives/positives for `descriptionWarn`. Trim leading whitespace and strip trailing comments from scalar values.
- [ ] **S3-L103** (low/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:44` - `FieldsPerRecord = -1` plus `LazyQuotes = true` makes the parser accept ragged and misquoted input without any signal. Short rows are then silently padded with empty strings in data.query output and counted as `missing` in profiles, so malformed data is presented as a valid table. At minimum warn when a record's field count differs from the header count so the caller can tell the input was malformed.
- [ ] **S3-L104** (low/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:98` - The comment above claims ambiguity is refused when two headers share the prefix, but the `-2` sentinel is never checked: `prefixMatch == -2` simply fails `prefixMatch >= 0` and execution falls through to the suffix and then the `contains` heuristics, which can bind the name to a completely different column. Either enforce the refusal (`if prefixMatch == -2 { return -1 }`) or fix the comment, since the current behaviour is the opposite of what is documented.
- [ ] **S3-L105** (low/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:411` - The operator is validated only inside the per-row switch, so for a table with zero rows (`t.Rows` empty) an unsupported operator such as `where "col eq 5"` returns success with `matched_rows: 0` instead of the documented error. Validate the operator once, before iterating rows.
- [ ] **S3-L106** (low/bug) `internal/tools/data.go:444` - For ordering operators, rows whose cell (or the filter value) is not numeric are silently dropped via `continue`. A filter like `where "name > 5"` applied to a text column therefore reports success with `matched_rows: 0` and no warning, which is indistinguishable from a legitimately empty result. Consider returning an error or appending a warning when non-numeric cells are skipped.
- [ ] **S3-L107** (low/bug) `internal/tools/documents.go:212` - Statement in `documents.ingest` used for the artifact name is only millisecond-precision, and the name is otherwise derived solely from the source path (`shortHash(path)`), not the content. Two concurrent ingests of the same path within the same millisecond therefore produce identical `.txt`/`.meta.json` names and `WriteArtifact` (os.WriteFile) silently overwrites the earlier artifact. Impact is limited because the content is normally identical, but the returned `artifact_id` is not uniquely tied to an extraction. Consider appending a uniqueness component (e.g. nanosecond timestamp or an atomic counter) to the artifact name.
- [ ] **S3-L108** (low/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:369` - Commission vote values are parsed through `argInt` with a default of 0, so a non-numeric, boolean or fractional value (e.g. `{"votes":{"sha":"two"}}`) is silently coerced to 0 and truncation of `1.9` yields 1. Although `ApplyCommission` validates that the vote total equals the member count, this conversion can mask a malformed input behind a confusing "votes (N) must equal..." error, and any coercion that happens to preserve the total changes the tie-break ranking unnoticed. Validate that each vote is a non-negative integer and return an explicit error otherwise.
- [ ] **S3-L109** (low/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:488` - `resolveRepoPath` rejections (absolute path, traversal, symlink escape) are downgraded to dropped-invalid entries here, so a caller/security input error is hidden behind a generic "report(s) dropped as invalid" warning and only inflates the invalid count. Since a rejected path means the report was never loaded (not merely malformed), return the error for path-resolution failures and keep the dropped-invalid behavior only for unreadable/ill-formed report files.
- [ ] **S3-L110** (low/bug) `internal/tools/evaluation.go:570` - `writeRepoJSON` truncates the destination in place (`os.WriteFile`), so an interrupted or concurrent call leaves a partially written aggregate/calibration/decision JSON that later readers (`eval.rank`, `eval.calibrate`) will fail to parse. Write to a temp file in the same directory and `os.Rename` it into place so the replacement is atomic.
- [ ] **S3-L111** (low/bug) `internal/tools/extras.go:577` - The 20MB guard is enforced only on the `os.Stat` result, then the file is read with `os.ReadFile` with no re-check; if the file is replaced or grows between the two calls the whole (potentially much larger) content is read into memory and sent to the API, bypassing the intended bound. Also, unknown extensions fall through `mimeForExt` to `application/octet-stream`, which is forwarded as an image MIME type. Consider re-checking the size after reading (or reading with a bounded reader) and validating that the extension maps to a supported image type before calling the model.
- [ ] **S3-L112** (low/bug) `internal/tools/files.go:104` - For a zero-byte file, `TrimSuffix`/`Count` yield 0 and `+1` reports `lines = 1`, which misdescribes an empty file. Guard the empty case so it reports zero lines (a trailing-newline-only file is similarly off).
- [ ] **S3-L113** (low/bug) `internal/tools/files.go:106` - Size-cap boundary off-by-one: at exactly the configured limit, both `io.LimitReader(f, limit)` (files.go) and `maxBytesReader` (xlsx.go) misclassify a clean EOF as overrun/truncation. `len(b) >= maxTextInspectBytes` is true when the file is exactly the limit and nothing was truncated, producing a spurious "text content truncated" warning; `maxBytesReader` returns "zip entry exceeds the configured size limit" for an entry whose uncompressed size is exactly the limit because the XML decoder reads past the root element to confirm EOF. Probe/read one extra byte (limit+1) or compare against the actual size to distinguish truncation/overrun from an exact-size file.
- [ ] **S3-L114** (low/bug) `internal/tools/plan.go:83` - Duplicate stage ids and negative `timebox_minutes` are accepted here. `plan.update` only ever matches the first stage with a given id, so a duplicated id produces a stage that can never be updated, and `argInt` passes negative values straight through into `plans.Stage.TimeboxMinute`. Track seen ids (skip or reject duplicates) and clamp/reject negative timeboxes; declare `seen := map[string]struct{}{}` before the loop.
- [ ] **S3-L115** (low/bug) `internal/tools/resources.go:49` - Read-modify-write on the shared profile without synchronization: `resources.Load` here and `resources.Save` below are separated by user input processing, and `resources.Save` performs a whole-file overwrite (atomic, but last-writer-wins). In-process tool dispatch is serial today, so the exposure is limited, but the same profile file is also written by `cmd/hackathonkit-mcp` (`resources.SetAnswer` + `resources.Save`), so a concurrent `--resources-set` invocation or any future concurrent caller silently loses the other side's answers (e.g. a claimed provider flag reverts to `false`). Consider serializing the load/mutate/save (file lock or re-load + merge the pending fields immediately before `Save`).
- [ ] **S3-L116** (low/bug) `internal/tools/resources.go:256` - This branch can produce a `partial` envelope with an empty `warnings[]`: it is taken when the profile exists but a claimed provider is missing its env keys (i.e. `resources.Missing(statuses)` non-empty) while `warnings` stays empty because the profile is present and the track resolves fine. Every other tool in this package always attaches an explanatory warning to a partial envelope, and clients surface `warnings`, not `data.missing`, so the partial status becomes unexplained. Append a warning naming the missing keys when `warnings` is empty.
- [ ] **S3-L117** (low/security) `internal/tools/security.go:126` - The raw `os.Open`/`readCapped` errors are surfaced verbatim through `mcp.Fail`, and Go's `*PathError` embeds the absolute resolved path (e.g. `open /srv/app/data/artifacts/x.md: no such file or directory`, `open /srv/app/data/input/x: permission denied`), disclosing the server's filesystem layout at the public tool boundary. This is the normal error path for a missing `artifact_id`, because `ArtifactPath` does not stat the file. Return a generic message for the caller (e.g. `fmt.Errorf("cannot read artifact %s", artifact)`) and keep the detailed error server-side, mirroring the treatment already recorded as L90 for `files.describe`.
- [ ] **S3-L118** (low/performance) `internal/tools/system.go:134` - The second `if d.Store != nil` block is redundant — nothing between the fly-events block and this one can change `d.Store`, so both blocks can be merged under a single nil check (this matches backlog L100, which is marked fixed but is still present). Also note `ActivationSnapshot` re-embeds `task` internally (`r.embedTask`, internal/routing/fly/events.go:30) immediately after `Recommend` embedded the same text, adding a second encoder/LLM call per recommend whenever a store is configured; consider reusing the vector computed by `Recommend` or making the snapshot opt-in.
- [ ] **S3-L119** (low/bug) `internal/tools/telegram.go:33` - The error path collapses every failure into a hard `Fail` and discards `result`/`err` context. `SendMessage` returns the partially built `SendResult` when a later chunk fails, so a long message may already have been delivered (or partly delivered) while the tool reports a plain error; a caller that retries then duplicates the already-sent chunks. Consider returning `mcp.Partial` carrying `result.ChatID`/`result.Chunks` (and the error as a warning) when the send failed after chunking began.
- [ ] **S3-L120** (low/bug) `internal/tools/telegram.go:94` - Residual from the L101 fix: the discovery failures are now surfaced in the result data, but the metric path is still skipped, so `telegram_errors_total` undercounts (every other failure path in this file calls `recordTelegramError`). The `GetChat` branch just above has the same gap. Record both so the tool result and metrics agree.
- [ ] **S3-L121** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/telegram.go:163` - A wrong-typed `commands` argument (e.g. a string or an object instead of an array) hits the same branch as a missing field and reports "commands is required for set_commands", which misleads the caller about the actual problem. Also note the trailing `if len(commands) == 0` check below is unreachable: `raw` is already non-empty and each iteration either returns an error or appends, so it can never trigger.
- [ ] **S3-L122** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/timeseries.go:45` - This guard (and `columnIndex` at internal/tools/data.go:66-115, which also returns -1 for ambiguous prefix/suffix/contains matches) cannot distinguish "column omitted" from "the column you named does not exist / is ambiguous". A caller that explicitly passes `time_column`/`value_column` and receives this message is told to pass them explicitly, which gives no hint about the real problem (typo, ambiguity, or the two args pointing at the same header). Additionally, `time_column == value_column` is accepted silently and produces a degenerate series (time regressed on itself, meaningless slope/z-scores). Recommend reporting which column failed to resolve and rejecting `timeIdx == valueIdx`.
- [ ] **S3-L123** (low/bug) `internal/tools/timeseries.go:71` - Off-by-one: with `idx = int(i*len/maxPoints)` the largest index produced is `int((maxPoints-1)*len/maxPoints)`, which is provably `< len-1` whenever `len > maxPoints`. The final (most recent) point is therefore never included in the downsampled series, while the envelope reports `end` as `points[len(points)-1].T`. Consumers that plot `series` alongside `start`/`end` will see the chart stop before the declared end (and the last sample can be up to one step stale). Force the final index to `len(points)-1`.
- [ ] **S3-L124** (low/bug) `internal/tools/timeseries.go:255` - When the trailing window has zero variance (`s == 0`) the z-score is undefined (division by zero / infinite), yet this branch emits every value that differs from the window mean as an anomaly with `Score` hard-coded to `threshold`. That is not a z-score: the reported score is fabricated and equals exactly the configured threshold for arbitrarily large deviations, so any consumer ranking or thresholding on `Score` (see also the truncation at the bottom of this function) is misled. Either skip zero-variance windows (no anomaly instead of a synthetic one), or keep the row but document/mark the score as not-computed so callers do not treat it as a real z-score.
- [ ] **S3-L125** (low/maintainability) `internal/tools/timeseries.go:286` - `anomalies` is sorted lexicographically by the RFC3339 timestamp string and then truncated to the first `limit` entries, so when more than `limit` anomalies exist the caller receives the chronologically earliest ones rather than the most significant ones — even though `Score` exists and `anomaly_count` reports the full total. The timestamp-string sort is only valid while every value is produced by `.UTC().Format(time.RFC3339)` (fixed width, `Z` suffix); if a non-UTC or sub-second format is ever introduced here the ordering silently breaks. Consider ranking by `math.Abs(Score)` descending before applying `limit` (or documenting that the earliest anomalies win).
- [ ] **S3-L126** (low/bug) `internal/tools/util.go:20` - Comma normalization is skipped entirely whenever the string also contains a dot, so the very common thousands+decimal formats are dropped: `parseNumber("1,234.56")` and `parseNumber("1.234,56")` both return `ok == false`. In `describeTable` that silently excludes the cell from the numeric sample and can flip the column to `"text"` (or `"timestamp"`) — a silent misclassification of ordinary CSV/XLSX data. Consider stripping the thousands separators from the integer part before splitting off the fraction; a preview would be: `intPart/frac := strings.Cut(s, ".")` and applying `commaIsThousands(intPart)` / `ReplaceAll` to `intPart` only.
- [ ] **S3-L127** (low/maintainability) `plugins/hackathonkit/hooks/codex-hooks.json:25` - Residual from L108 (docs/REVIEW-BACKLOG.md marks it fixed, but no per-hook `timeout` is present in this manifest): the top-level description promises every hook is best-effort and "never blocks a session", yet nothing here bounds execution. With `"matcher": ".*"` this PostToolUse hook spawns `node` on *every* tool call, so a slow or hung invocation (e.g. a `node` process blocked on a stdin read) is limited only by the harness default and holds up each tool result. Add an explicit per-hook `timeout` (seconds) to this entry and to the `session-start`/`stop` entries so the best-effort contract is enforced declaratively instead of relying solely on hooks.js.
- [ ] **S3-L128** (low/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:8` - `$0` is used literally; symlinks are not canonicalized. When the launcher is invoked through a symlink, `dirname` resolves to the symlink's directory, so `../../..` yields an unrelated root and the launcher may exec the wrong `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` or report a confusing missing-compose path. Consider resolving the real script location (e.g. `readlink -f` when available) or failing clearly when the resolved root does not contain `docker-compose.yml`.
- [ ] **S3-L129** (low/bug) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hackathonkit-mcp-launcher.sh:21` - This error message is misleading: it is reached whenever the `-f && -x` guard at line 11 fails, which includes the case where `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` exists but is not executable (or is a directory). The user is told no local binary exists when in fact one is present but unusable. Distinguish the two cases to make diagnosis possible.
- [ ] **S3-L130** (low/maintainability) `plugins/hackathonkit/scripts/hooks.js:242` - `pruneExpiredSessions()` duplicates the expiry logic already embedded in `readActive()` (both compare mtime against `SESSION_TTL_MS` and unlink), but with slightly different semantics: `pruneExpiredSessions` only uses mtime while `readActive` uses `started_at`/`updated_at`/mtime. Extract a single `isExpired(full, entry?)` helper so the two paths cannot drift.
- [ ] **S3-L131** (low/bug) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:27` - The leading `[[:space:]]*` allows a match with no separator, so a `-buildvcs=...` substring embedded inside a quoted GOFLAGS value (e.g. `-ldflags="-X a -buildvcs=b"`) is silently stripped and the remaining flags are corrupted. The bare `-buildvcs` form is also not normalized and ends up duplicated with the appended `-buildvcs=false`. Anchor the match to a real token boundary and cover the bare flag form.
- [ ] **S3-L132** (low/performance) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:35` - All `go test` stdout/stderr is buffered in the `TEST_OUT` shell variable. For verbose suites this can consume substantial memory, and on success the entire output is discarded so warnings printed by tests are lost. Consider redirecting to a temp file (or teeing only on failure) instead of capturing everything in memory.
- [ ] **S3-L133** (low/maintainability) `scripts/coverage-check.sh:41` - The pipeline has no error propagation (`pipefail` is unavailable in POSIX sh), so a failing `go tool cover` is masked by awk's exit status; only the indirect empty-`TOTAL` check catches it. The same `go tool cover -func` invocation is also executed twice (once here, once when printing the worst files). Capture the output once and verify the command's exit status explicitly.
- [ ] **S3-L134** (low/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:92` - `INFRA_STARTED` is set only after `docker compose up -d` returns successfully. If `up` fails partway (e.g. one container starts, another errors out), the script exits via `set -e` with `INFRA_STARTED=0`, so `stop_infra` is skipped and the partially-started containers are left running despite the failure path claiming to clean up. Set the flag before starting (or always attempt a stop once `up` has been invoked).
- [ ] **S3-L135** (low/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:118` - Hardcoded host/port assumes the MinIO API is always published as 127.0.0.1:9000. Any change to the compose port mapping (or a different MinIO port) makes this probe fail the run even though the service is healthy. Derive the published port from compose instead of hardcoding it.
- [ ] **S3-L136** (low/bug) `scripts/docker-check.sh:129` - Non-POSIX `seq` dependency: the script's shebang is `sh` and this loop relies on `seq`, which is not guaranteed by POSIX. If `seq` is unavailable the `for` list is empty, the body never executes, and the script fails with the misleading "minio container is not accepting exec connections" without ever probing. Use a POSIX counter loop instead.
- [x] **S3-L137** (low/bug) `scripts/doctor.ps1:16` - `$PSCommandPath` is empty when the script is dot-sourced or pasted into a session, in which case `$root` becomes empty and every `Join-Path $root ...` check silently probes relative paths (or errors), producing bogus results. `scripts/smoke.ps1` explicitly guards this (`if (-not $PSCommandPath) { ... exit 1 }`); add the same guard here for consistency and to fail loudly instead of reporting misleading checks.
- [x] **S3-L138** (low/bug) `scripts/doctor.ps1:86` - The version is scraped with a regex anchored on nothing, so it can match a nested/incorrect `"version"` key rather than the top-level one. Worse, the check only tests file existence: when `install-state.json` exists but no `version` is found (or the file is malformed), the detail still claims "missing configs/install-state.json; run scripts/setup.ps1", which is factually wrong and misleads triage. Parse the JSON and distinguish "file missing" from "version absent".
- [ ] **S3-L139** (low/maintainability) `scripts/doctor.sh:174` - The greedy `.*` prefix makes sed capture the *last* `"summary"` occurrence on a line, and the pattern assumes `summary` is a flat string; any nested/repeated key or a non-string summary yields an empty or wrong value in the doctor output. Same brittle-text-parsing concern as the `present` grep below — use a JSON parser when one is available.
- [ ] **S3-L140** (low/maintainability) `scripts/env-set.sh:41` - Command substitution already strips *all* trailing newlines from `$(cat)`, so the following `${VALUE%...}` removes nothing and is dead code; the comment is also misleading because it claims only a single trailing newline is treated as a terminator. If multiline secrets must preserve trailing blank lines, read via a mechanism that does not use command substitution.
- [ ] **S3-L141** (low/maintainability) `scripts/env-set.test.sh:17` - `echo` is not safe for the diagnostic text here: in `sh` implementations whose builtin `echo` interprets backslash escapes (dash), `\\` is collapsed to `\`, so the failure detail for the escape test prints `got: B=p\q\r` instead of the real `B=p\q\\r` — the message misleads exactly when a value-fidelity assertion fails. Use `printf` for the harness output.
- [x] **S3-L142** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.ps1:38` - The post-render validation re-reads the temp file instead of validating the `$manifest` string that was just written. Because the file is written as UTF-8 **without BOM**, and `Get-Content -Raw` without `-Encoding` decodes with the system ANSI code page on Windows PowerShell 5.1, the validated text can differ from the bytes actually published (mojibake for any non-ASCII repo root/template content), weakening the validation guarantee. Validate the in-memory value (or read back with `-Encoding UTF8`).
- [ ] **S3-L143** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:22` - `mktemp` creates the temp file with mode 0600 and `mv` preserves that mode, so the published `.mcp.json` is silently downgraded to 0600 regardless of the previous manifest's mode/umask (e.g. it becomes unreadable to other users/processes that were previously able to read it). Set an explicit, sane mode before publishing, e.g. `chmod 0644 "$TMP"` (or chmod the final path after `mv`).
- [ ] **S3-L144** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:23` - The INT/TERM handlers only remove the temp file and then return, so after Ctrl-C/signal the shell resumes and continues into the `grep`/`mv` steps operating on a temp file that no longer exists (the final `mv` then fails, producing a confusing error instead of a clean interrupt). Also note the trap fires after a successful `mv` as well. Prefer `trap 'rm -f "$TMP"' EXIT` plus a handler that exits, e.g. `trap 'rm -f "$TMP"; exit 130' INT TERM`.
- [ ] **S3-L145** (low/bug) `scripts/gen-mcp-manifest.sh:44` - The guard only detects the two known tokens and never validates that the rendered output is actually JSON, so a template introducing any other placeholder (e.g. `{{SOMETHING_ELSE}}`) or malformed output caused by escaping gaps silently overwrites the previous good manifest with an invalid file (residual of backlog M164). Consider matching placeholders generically (`grep -q '{{[A-Z_][A-Z_]*}}' "$TMP"`) and, when a JSON parser is available, validating `$TMP` before the `mv`.
- [x] **S3-L146** (low/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.ps1:3` - Resetting `$global:LASTEXITCODE` to 0 is ineffective/misleading: a subsequent native call always overwrites it, and if `codex` is not installed PowerShell raises a terminating `CommandNotFoundException` that aborts the script before this guard is ever evaluated (so the intended "unavailable" message is never printed). Guard the invocation with `Get-Command codex -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` or a try/catch to produce the intended failure message.
- [x] **S3-L147** (low/maintainability) `scripts/plugin-check.ps1:69` - `$stderrText` is only ever assigned inside the outer `finally` block but is read later in the exit-code and missing-response branches. This relies on PowerShell block scoping leaking the variable to script scope; if the `finally` body is skipped or throws before assignment, the later `if ($stderrText)` reads an undefined value. Initialize `$stderrText = ""` before the `try` so its lifetime is explicit.
- [ ] **S3-L148** (low/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:18` - The `trap` is installed only after both `mktemp` calls. If the second `mktemp` fails (or a signal arrives during the two calls) under `set -e`, `JSON_FILE` is never removed and leaks in TMPDIR. Install the trap first with defensive expansions so it is valid even if a variable is unset.
- [ ] **S3-L149** (low/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:58` - `os.path.abspath` neither expands `~` nor resolves a relative path against the installed plugin location: it simply joins the value with the *current* working directory, so a `source.path` of `~/plugin` or a repo-relative path still yields a wrong `.mcp.json` path and a misleading "missing" failure. Expand the user component and, if `codex` can report relative paths, resolve them against a known install root before joining.
- [ ] **S3-L150** (low/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:106` - The command from the manifest is executed as-is with `cwd=tmp`. If `server["command"]` (or an entry in `args`) is relative, it resolves against the temporary directory instead of the plugin source or PATH, producing a false failure — or, for a shell/interpreter-mediated relative path, launching an unintended binary. The generated template uses an absolute launcher path, so this is a hardening gap; reject non-absolute commands (or resolve them against `source`) and fail with a clear message.
- [ ] **S3-L151** (low/bug) `scripts/plugin-check.sh:130` - `obj.get("id") in (1, 2, 3)` is loosened by Python equality: `True == 1` and `1.0 == 1` both match, so a malformed response such as `{"id": true, "result": {}}` is stored under key `True`, which aliases key `1` and can make the id-1 checks pass. Also the response is only matched by id, never validated against the expected method (e.g. `tools/list` should return a `tools` array, `tools/call` a `content` field). Require a strict integer id (`type(obj.get("id")) is int`) and validate the payload shape.
- [ ] **S3-L152** (low/test) `scripts/quality-gate.sh:3` - No automated shell test covers this launcher: `scripts/` has `env-set.test.sh` and `smoke.test.sh`, but no `quality-gate` regression test, so the branches added by this campaign (stale-binary rebuild, temp build, EXIT-trap cleanup, exit-code propagation — including the earlier "passing gate reported as exit 2" regression) are unverified. Add a test that stubs `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` / `go` on `PATH` and asserts the propagated exit code and that the temp binary is removed.
- [ ] **S3-L153** (low/maintainability) `scripts/quality-gate.sh:36` - `exit $?` is unreachable on failure: with `set -e` active, a non-zero status from the gate terminates the shell immediately with that status, so this line only ever runs with `$? = 0`. The failure code does propagate today (via `set -e`), but the line reads as if it were the propagation mechanism, and anyone who later relaxes `set -e` around this call silently loses the gate's exit status. Capture the status explicitly instead of relying on the implicit abort.
- [x] **S3-L154** (low/maintainability) `scripts/repair.ps1:27` - The profile is copied verbatim from `install-state.json` without checking it against the values the tooling understands. `write-install-state.ps1` rejects anything other than `core`/`full`, so a hand-edited or corrupted state file surfaces later as the confusing message "[repair] write-install-state failed" instead of pointing at the bad profile. Validate the value here and warn/fall back to `full`.
- [x] **S3-L155** (low/maintainability) `scripts/repair.ps1:42` - The nested `if ($runtime -eq "binary")` block is not indented, which makes the control flow read as if the rebuild were unconditional and the closing brace ambiguous. It is also a silent no-op when `runtime` is `docker` even though binaries are missing. Please indent the nested block (and, if the docker path genuinely never needs local binaries, say so in a comment or log at verbose level) so the intended reachability of each branch is obvious.
- [ ] **S3-L156** (low/bug) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:55` - The banner's promise does not match the behaviour of the secret prompts. When stdin is not a tty, `ask_yes` treats EOF as "no", but if the pipe actually contains `y` answers, `read_secret` still performs blocking `read`s that consume the remaining injected data, and stty-based echo suppression is skipped (guarded by `[ -t 0 ]`). With a pipe that stays open the script can block indefinitely at a secret prompt with no way to tell the user. Confirm this degraded path is intended; safer options are to refuse to run when stdin is not a tty (require an explicit flag) or to skip the secret prompts entirely in that mode.
- [ ] **S3-L157** (low/bug) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:90` - The `else` branch is a misleading no-op: because `set_key` returns early when the value is empty, `set_key OPENAI_API_KEY ""` never removes a previously stored `OPENAI_API_KEY`. So answering "no" (or skipping) records `openai_api=false` in the profile while the stale credential stays in `.env` indefinitely (REVIEW-BACKLOG L126). This is also inconsistent with the other providers, which have no `else` branch. Either drop the dead branch or, better, warn the user that an existing key is being kept (or add an unset mode to `env-set.sh`).
- [ ] **S3-L158** (low/test) `scripts/setup-resources.sh:105` - There is no automated test for this script (only README/docs/`doctor.sh` reference it), so the two paths that regressed here — the empty-secret / `set_key` no-op path and the track `case` filter (including values like `99`, `8`, `12-`) — have no regression coverage. A small shell test that feeds scripted stdin (e.g. all `y` with empty secrets, and an invalid track) and asserts on `configs/resources.json` + `.env` would pin the intended behaviour.
- [x] **S3-L159** (low/maintainability) `scripts/setup.ps1:2` - `$Profile` is an automatic PowerShell variable (the path to the user's profile script); PowerShell variable names are case-insensitive, so this parameter shadows it for the whole script scope, which is confusing and can break code (or dot-sourced helpers) that relies on `$PROFILE`. Consider a non-colliding name such as `-Mode`/`-ProfileName` (it must be renamed consistently in `write-install-state.ps1`, which also declares `$Profile`).
- [ ] **S3-L160** (low/maintainability) `scripts/setup.sh:46` - If `.env.example` is missing (or `cp` fails for any reason), `set -e` aborts the whole bootstrap with only `cp`'s raw error message. Check for the template first and emit an actionable message so the failure is diagnosable.
- [x] **S3-L161** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.ps1:74` - `$parsedCount` is incremented on every successfully parsed line but never read (the per-line order assertion it used to back was removed). It is dead state that misleadingly suggests an assertion still exists — drop the variable and its increment.
- [x] **S3-L162** (low/test) `scripts/smoke.ps1:80` - The assertions only check that each response has a `result` property and no `error`; the payload semantics are never verified. A server replying `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"result":{}}` would pass, so regressions in tool registration or in the initialize handshake go undetected. Consider asserting the key fields per response (e.g. `initialize` returns a `protocolVersion`, `tools/list` returns a non-empty `tools` array, `system.context` returns content).
- [ ] **S3-L163** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.sh:40` - `cd "$ROOT"` permanently mutates the working directory for the remainder of the script (the `-f` argument is already absolute, so it is unnecessary). It also makes the script's behavior depend on an implicit global side effect. Drop the `cd` and rely on `-f "$ROOT/docker-compose.yml"`.
- [ ] **S3-L164** (low/bug) `scripts/smoke.sh:67` - In auto mode a stale `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` only produces a warning but is still executed, and there is no fallback to the compose runtime. A stale build can therefore silently pass (testing old behavior) or fail with misleading diagnostics. Consider aborting/falling back when the binary is older than the sources, or make the warning an explicit opt-in.
- [ ] **S3-L165** (low/bug) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:10` - Trapping INT/TERM with only the cleanup action does not terminate the script: the handler removes `$TMP` and execution then resumes, so the remaining `check`/`grep` calls run against a deleted workspace and report misleading failures (and `set -e` may abort mid-accounting). Terminate explicitly in the signal traps.
- [ ] **S3-L166** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:20` - `check` writes to the globals `name`/`ok`/`detail` and prints `${detail:+: $detail}` unquoted, so the value is subject to word splitting and pathname expansion; a detail containing `*` (or a leading `-n`) can be mangled by `echo`. Quote the expansion instead.
- [ ] **S3-L167** (low/maintainability) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:50` - PATH is hard-pinned to `$TMP/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` while the generated stubs use `#!/usr/bin/env sh`. On systems where `env` is not under `/usr/bin` (e.g. NixOS) or where `sh`/coreutils/python3 live in `/usr/local/bin`, the stubs fail to launch or smoke.sh silently takes the non-python3 fallback branch, so the suite tests a different code path than CI. Prepending the stub dir to the inherited PATH is more robust.
- [ ] **S3-L168** (low/test) `scripts/smoke.test.sh:71` - `--binary` when `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` is absent (smoke.sh:46-49, exit 1 with the "--binary requires ..." diagnostic) is never exercised — the only negative case is `--compose` without docker. A regression that silently falls back to docker instead of failing loudly under `--binary` would go unnoticed.
- [x] **S3-L169** (low/maintainability) `scripts/sync-harness.ps1:14` - Failure detection relies solely on `$LASTEXITCODE` being set to non-zero. That only works because `sync-skills.ps1` currently ends with an explicit `exit 0`/`exit 1`; if the child is refactored to report failures via `Write-Error -ErrorAction Continue` or to return without `exit` (or if its terminating error is swallowed), `$LASTEXITCODE` stays at the value set by `$global:LASTEXITCODE = 0` above and a failed sync passes this guard silently. Prefer an explicit success signal (try/catch around the call, or check `$?`/a returned status) rather than depending on the child's `exit` contract. Also, `Write-Error` followed by `exit 1` is redundant under `Stop` (the `Write-Error` throws first, so `exit 1` never executes) — a pattern repeated at the other guards in this file.
- [x] **S3-L170** (low/test) `scripts/sync-harness.ps1:24` - Source validation only checks that at least one subdirectory exists; it does not verify that the directories actually contain skill payloads (e.g. `SKILL.md`). Empty or partially-populated directories, or a source consisting only of stray root-level files, pass validation and are mirrored as an effectively empty/invalid mirror that downstream consumers will fail to load. Validate the expected payload per skill directory (or at least that at least one `SKILL.md` is present).
- [ ] **S3-L171** (low/bug) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:26` - The copy assumes `plugins/hackathonkit/skills` exists. If it is missing (or is a file), `cp -R` fails with a generic error and the freshly created `$CLAUDE_TMP` is only removed by the EXIT trap, producing a confusing failure far from the cause. Check the source explicitly first and report a clear message.
- [ ] **S3-L172** (low/maintainability) `scripts/sync-harness.sh:69` - This JSON list duplicates the four manifest paths already hard-coded in the existence loop above, so the two can drift: a manifest added to one loop is checked for existence but silently skips JSON validation (or vice versa). Define the JSON manifests once (e.g. `JSON_MANIFESTS="..."`) and iterate over that list in both loops.
- [ ] **S3-L173** (low/bug) `scripts/sync-skills.sh:35` - Verification only compares aggregate counts of directories/files/`SKILL.md` and never checks content, modes or symlink handling (`cp -R` may dereference or preserve links depending on the platform, so counts can diverge from real content). Additionally, a failing `find` in these pipelines is masked: `set -e` ignores non-final pipeline failures, so an unreadable subtree passes as "matching". Consider verifying file lists (`find ... -print0 | sort -z | xargs -0 cmp`-style) and checking `find`'s exit status.
- [x] **S3-L174** (low/maintainability) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:10` - The root heuristic only requires `docker-compose.yml` + `go.mod`, which many unrelated Go/compose projects satisfy. Run from such a directory, this script would permanently delete that project's `bin`, `configs\install-state.json` and `data\control`. Consider also asserting a hackathonkit-specific marker (e.g. `plugins\hackathonkit\.mcp.json`) so the destructive branch cannot trigger on an unrelated checkout.
- [x] **S3-L175** (low/bug) `scripts/uninstall.ps1:53` - Removal is keyed on folder *name* matching the shipped skills, so a user-authored skill whose folder happens to share a name with a shipped skill is silently deleted, contradicting the comment that user-authored skills survive. Consider removing only skills the installer actually recorded (e.g. from `configs\install-state.json`) or that contain a plugin marker file, and warn/skip on name collisions.
- [ ] **S3-L176** (low/maintainability) `scripts/uninstall.sh:11` - The `[ -z "$ROOT" ]` test is unreachable: `set -e` (line 3) makes the script abort as soon as `ROOT="$(... cd ... && pwd)"` returns non-zero, so when the directory cannot be resolved the user gets only a raw `cd: can't cd to ...` from the shell instead of this friendly refusal. Either disable `-e` for that assignment (`ROOT="$(... )" || ROOT=`) or check the resolution failure explicitly so the intended guard message is actually emitted.
- [ ] **S3-L177** (low/bug) `scripts/uninstall.sh:77` - The warning summary only accounts for path failures. When `CODEX_FAIL=1` but every `rm -rf` succeeded, this line prints `finished with warnings (0 path failure(s))`, which is confusing/misleading about the cause. Include the codex status, or emit a distinct message when only the codex step failed.
- [ ] **S3-L178** (low/documentation) `scripts/uninstall.sh:80` - The completion message claims `data/` was kept even though `data/control` is in the removal list above, which contradicts it. `scripts/uninstall.ps1:84` phrases this correctly ("data/input, data/artifacts, logs/ and committed files were kept (data/control was removed)"); align the wording or drop the `data/` claim.
- [ ] **S3-L179** (low/maintainability) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:12` - With `set -e` active, a non-zero exit from the binary terminates the shell on the line above, so `exit $?` is unreachable on the failure path and only ever runs with 0. The effective behaviour is also inconsistent with the `go run` branch below, which collapses every failure to `exit 1` while this branch forwards the binary's raw status. Make the propagation explicit (and decide/reuse one documented status) instead of relying on `set -e` side effects:
- [ ] **S3-L180** (low/bug) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:17` - `cd "$ROOT"` is unchecked apart from `set -e`: if it fails, the script exits silently with `cd`'s status and never reaches the labelled diagnostics (including the exit-2 "no runtime available" path). Fail loudly so a broken ROOT is distinguishable from a validation failure:
- [ ] **S3-L181** (low/test) `scripts/validate-skills.sh:25` - The wrapper exposes three distinct exit paths (0 success, 1 validation failure, 2 no runtime) but has no self-test or harness reference, unlike the sibling scripts covered by `scripts/smoke.test.sh`. Add cases that assert the exact status codes and stderr labels for: prebuilt `bin/hackathonkit-mcp` present (passing and failing), the `go run` fallback (including a build failure, where the `(go run exit $?)` bug above would be caught), and the no-runtime path, so regressions in these branches are actually verified.
- [ ] **S3-L182** (low/security) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:7` - ROOT is trusted based only on the presence of docker-compose.yml, and the path is used verbatim: a relative path (`./foo`) or a `configs` entry that is a symlink can redirect the `mkdir -p`/`mktemp`/`mv` writes outside the intended repository. Canonicalise ROOT (resolving symlinks) before validating and use the resolved value for all subsequent operations.
- [ ] **S3-L183** (low/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:18` - The pattern still allows arbitrary leading whitespace and matches any line start containing `"version":`, so an indented/nested `"version"` key that appears before the top-level one in plugin.json is still picked up, and the value is never validated. Anchor the match to the top-level key (no leading indentation, or parse via a real JSON parser such as `python3 -c`/`jq` when available) and sanity-check the extracted value before writing it.
- [ ] **S3-L184** (low/bug) `scripts/write-install-state.sh:53` - `mktemp` creates the temp file with mode 0600 and `mv` preserves that mode, so `configs/install-state.json` is only readable by the invoking user. Other users/services that read the state snapshot (e.g. doctor run by a different account) will get permission errors. Set explicit permissions before publishing.
